  - dark browns and other deep earth tones

### [build](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/build_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɪld/` · **US:** `/bɪld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bui/build/build__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bui/build/build__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - build (something) They have permission to build 200 new homes.
  - to build a plant/facility/structure
  - We build computer systems for large companies.
  - Robins build nests almost anywhere.
  - They don't have the money to build roads.
  - They're going to build on the site of the old power station.
  - be built from/of/out of something The houses are built out of local stone.
  - be built in something apartment blocks built in brick and concrete
  - build something for somebody They had a house built for them.
  - build somebody something David built us a shed in the back yard.

### [building](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/building) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɪldɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈbɪldɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bui/build/building__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bui/build/building__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a tall/high-rise/ten-storey building
  - Housing is limited and most people live in high-rise apartment buildings.
  - The architects have presented the design of a new office building.
  - the restoration of historic buildings
  - to design/construct/erect a building
  - The old industrial buildings have all been demolished.
  - In 2018 the building was converted into a house.

### [bus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bus_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bʌs/` · **US:** `/bʌs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bus/bus__/bus__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bus/bus__/bus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - by bus Shall we walk or go by bus?
  - I didn't want to get on the wrong bus.
  - Many students board the school bus before 7 a.m.
  - He was seriously injured when the band's tour bus crashed.
  - I was going to catch the airport shuttle bus.
  - (US English) I ride the city bus every day.
  - a bus company/driver
  - A regular bus service connects the train station with the town centre.
  - He can't afford the bus fare into the city.

### [business](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/business) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɪznəs/` · **US:** `/ˈbɪznəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bus/busin/business__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bus/busin/business__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - There will be some changes to the way we conduct business.
  - in business She has set up in business as a hairdresser.
  - When he left school, he went into business with his brother.
  - the music/entertainment/movie business
  - the insurance/banking business
  - the food/restaurant/hotel business
  - Retail is a tough business.
  - in the… business She works in the software business.
  - We need to concentrate on our core business (= the main thing that our business does).
  - business owners/leaders
  - a business partner
  - the business community
  - business opportunities/interests/ventures
  - the business cycle/sector/world

### [busy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/busy_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɪzi/` · **US:** `/ˈbɪzi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bus/busy_/busy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bus/busy_/busy__us_2.mp3)

  - I'm afraid the doctor is busy at the moment. Can he call you back?
  - The principal is a very busy woman.
  - I'll be too busy to come to the meeting.
  - She was always too busy to listen.
  - I've got enough work to keep you busy.

### [but](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/but_1) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/bʌt/` · **US:** `/bʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/but/but__/but__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/but/but__/but__us_2_rr.mp3)

  - His mother won't be there, but his father might.
  - It isn't that he lied exactly, but he does tend to exaggerate.

### [butter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/butter_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbʌtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈbʌtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/but/butte/butter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/but/butte/butter__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Fry the onions in butter.
  - a pat/knob/tablespoon of butter
  - (North American English) a stick (= small pack) of butter
  - Melt the butter in a small pan, add the flour and stir well.
  - Do you want butter or margarine on your toast?

### [buy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/buy_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/baɪ/` · **US:** `/baɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/buy/buy__/buy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/buy/buy__/buy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - buy (something) Where did you buy that dress?
  - She had no money to buy a ticket.
  - They can't afford to buy school books.
  - If you're thinking of getting a new car, now is a good time to buy.
  - buy something from somebody/something I bought it from a friend for £10.
  - buy something off somebody/something (informal) She bought it off eBay for $50.
  - buy somebody something He bought me a new coat.
  - buy something for somebody He bought a new coat for me.
  - buy something + adv./prep. I bought my car second-hand.
  - They buy all their groceries in bulk .
  - + adv./prep. It's generally cheaper if you buy online.
  - They make their money by buying cheap and selling dear.

### [by](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/by_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/baɪ/` · **US:** `/baɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/by_/by__g/by__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/by_/by__u/by__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The house is heated by gas.
  - May I pay by credit card?
  - I will contact you by letter.
  - to travel by boat/bus/car/plane
  - to travel by air/land/sea
  - Switch it on by pressing this button.

### [bye](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bye_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/baɪ/` · **US:** `/baɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bye/bye__/bye__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bye/bye__/bye__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Bye! See you next week.
  - She waved bye-bye and got into the car.
  - Bye for now Dad!


## C

### [cafe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cafe) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæfeɪ/` · **US:** `/kæˈfeɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/caf/cafe_/cafe__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/caf/cafe_/cafe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are small shops and pavement cafes around every corner.
  - an outdoor cafe serving drinks and light meals
  - at a cafe They were having lunch at a cafe near the station.
  - in a cafe We stopped for a coffee in our favourite cafe.

### [cake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cake_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/keɪk/` · **US:** `/keɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cak/cake_/cake__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cak/cake_/cake__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a piece/slice of cake
  - to make/bake a cake
  - a chocolate cake
  - a birthday cake
  - (British English) a cake tin (= for cooking a cake in)
  - (North American English) a cake pan
  - a plate of cream cakes
  - There's carrot cake for dessert.
  - a box of cake mix

### [call](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/call_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːl/` · **US:** `/kɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cal/call_/call__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cal/call_/call__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to get/receive a call from somebody
  - to give somebody/make a call
  - I'll take (= answer) the call upstairs.
  - I left a message but he didn't return my call.
  - a local/long-distance call
  - An emergency call was made at 9 p.m.
  - They put out a distress call and were towed into harbour

### [call](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/call_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːl/` · **US:** `/kɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cal/call_/call__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cal/call_/call__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - call somebody/something + noun They decided to call the baby Mark.
  - His name's Hiroshi but everyone calls him Hiro.
  - be called + noun What's their son called?
  - I don't know anyone called Scott.
  - I've forgotten what the firm he works for is called.
  - What's it called again? Oh yes, a router.
  - to be commonly/often called something
  - This group of artists and poets is sometimes called the New York School.
  - call somebody/something after somebody/something They called their first daughter after her grandmother.
  - The cheese is called after the town of the same name.
  - call somebody/something by something We call each other by our first names here.
  - The plant is called by different names in different parts of the country.

### [camera](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/camera) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæmrə/` · **US:** `/ˈkæmrə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/camer/camera__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/camer/camera__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Just point the camera and press the button.
  - The camera has captured the drama of the event.
  - a TV/video camera
  - Most phones have a built-in camera.
  - Digital cameras are easy to use.
  - Closed-circuit cameras have been installed throughout the building.
  - a camera crew
  - Different camera angles show different perspectives.

### [can](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/can1) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/kæn/` · **US:** `/kæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/can/can__/can__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/can/can__/can__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can run fast.
  - Can you call back tomorrow?
  - He couldn't answer the question.
  - The stadium can be emptied in four minutes.
  - I can't promise anything, but I'll do what I can.
  - Please let us know if you cannot attend the meeting.

### [cannot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cannot) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkænɒt/` · **US:** `/ˈkænɑːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/can/canno/cannot__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/can/canno/cannot__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I cannot believe the price of the tickets!

### [capital](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/capital_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæpɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈkæpɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cap/capit/capital__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cap/capit/capital__us_3.mp3)

### [capital](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/capital_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæpɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈkæpɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cap/capit/capital__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cap/capit/capital__us_3.mp3)

  - Cairo is the capital of Egypt.

  - Rennes is the provincial capital of Brittany.

### [car](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/car) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɑː(r)/` · **US:** `/kɑːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/car__/car__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/car__/car__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Paula got into the car and drove off.
  - by car How did you come?’ ‘By car.’
  - in a/the car Are you going in the car?
  - Her husband was driving the car at the time of the accident.
  - Where can I park the car?
  - There were parked cars on both sides of the road.
  - a car driver/manufacturer/dealer
  - a car accident/crash
  - I can't find my car keys.

### [card](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/card_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɑːd/` · **US:** `/kɑːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/card_/card__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/card_/card__us_1.mp3)

### [career](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/career_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/kəˈrɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/caree/career__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/caree/career__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a teaching career
  - career in something She plans to pursue a career in medicine.
  - He had a successful career in television journalism.
  - career as something He enjoyed a long and distinguished career as a historian.
  - She has been concentrating on her career.
  - a change of career
  - That will be a good career move (= something that will help your career).
  - We wish our graduates every success in whatever career path they have chosen.
  - a career soldier/diplomat (= a professional one)
  - (British English) a careers adviser/officer (= a person whose job is to give people advice and information about jobs)

### [carrot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/carrot) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkærət/` · **US:** `/ˈkærət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/carro/carrot__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/carro/carrot__us_1.mp3)

  - carrot sticks (= long thin pieces)

### [carry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/carry_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæri/` · **US:** `/ˈkæri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/carry/carry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/carry/carry__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - carry somebody/something He was carrying a large bag.
  - to carry a sign/banner/flag

  - carry somebody/something + adv./prep. She carried her baby in her arms.
  - The injured were carried away on stretchers.

### [cat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cat_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kæt/` · **US:** `/kæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cat/cat__/cat__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cat/cat__/cat__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˌsiː ˈdiː/` · **US:** `/ˌsiː ˈdiː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cd_/cd_1_/cd_1_gb_1_abbr.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cd_/cd__u/cd__us_1.mp3)

  - He had an impressive CD collection.
  - the fall in the number of people buying CDs

### [cent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cent_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sent/` · **US:** `/sent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cen/cent_/cent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cen/cent_/cent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A one-minute phone call to the UK cost 10 cents.
  - The price of diesel was increased by 17 cents per litre.
  - The $300 he'd spent on a ticket was worth every cent.

### [centre](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/centre_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsentə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈsentər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cen/centr/centre__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cen/centr/centre__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He walked to the centre of the circle.
  - in the centre of something There was a long table in the centre of the room.
  - The statue is in the very centre of the temple.
  - in the centre The brownie should be firm on the outside but soft in the centre.
  - at the centre of something Aristotle believed that the Earth was at the centre of the universe.
  - (figurative) The executive at the centre of the bribery scandal was convicted last week.
  - chocolates with soft centres

### [century](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/century) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsentʃəri/` · **US:** `/ˈsentʃəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cen/centu/century__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cen/centu/century__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A century ago, Valparaiso was the country's main port.
  - He was born more than a century after the death of Confucius.
  - for centuries They have lived there for centuries.
  - over the centuries Their techniques have evolved over the centuries.
  - through the centuries Those stories have been told again and again through the centuries.

### [chair](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chair_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃeə(r)/` · **US:** `/tʃer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chair/chair__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chair/chair__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a table and chairs
  - on a chair Sit on your chair!
  - in a chair an old man asleep in a chair (= an armchair)
  - She was sitting in her favourite chair.
  - He sighed and leaned back in his chair.
  - ‘Sit down,’ she said, pointing to the empty chair next to her.
  - a comfortable chair
  - a wooden/leather/plastic chair
  - a folding/reclining/swivel chair
  - an office/a desk chair

### [change](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/change_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃeɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/tʃeɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chang/change__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chang/change__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - change in something We're hoping for a change in the weather.
  - There was no change in the patient's condition overnight.
  - I need to make some major changes in my life.
  - a change in policy/law
  - The past decade has seen dramatic changes in global poverty.
  - change to something We need to inform you of significant changes to the tax system.
  - She is someone who hates change.
  - social/political/economic change
  - The award-winning director firmly believes in the power of film to bring about positive change.

### [change](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/change_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃeɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/tʃeɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chang/change__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chang/change__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Rick hasn't changed. He looks exactly the same as he did at school.
  - We examined changing attitudes towards education.
  - The company failed to adapt to changing circumstances.
  - Things have changed dramatically since then.
  - Her life changed completely when she won the lottery.
  - change with something My attitude to life has changed with age.

### [chart](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chart_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɑːt/` · **US:** `/tʃɑːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chart/chart__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chart/chart__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Some shares have lost two-thirds of their value since being issued (see chart).
  - chart of somebody/something The charts of 138 patients with chronic heart failure were reviewed.
  - The accompanying chart shows data for eight countries.
  - a sales chart (= showing the level of a company’s sales)
  - an astrological chart
  - a weather chart
  - comparative charts of prices of food items now and in 2012
  - in a chart Gas prices have risen over the past decade, as shown in the chart below.

### [cheap](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cheap_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/tʃiːp/` · **US:** `/tʃiːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/cheap/cheap__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/cheap/cheap__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cheap imports/flights
  - Their cheap prices have helped them pick up new customers.
  - They're offering incredibly cheap fares to Eastern Europe.
  - Cycling is a cheap way to get around.
  - A good education is not cheap.
  - Electricity is relatively cheap in Ireland.
  - The device isn't exactly cheap at £500.
  - A falling dollar will boost the economy by making exports cheaper.
  - a cheaper alternative/option
  - Renewable energy is getting cheaper all the time.
  - The cheapest rates are usually available online.
  - These desperate people are used as a source of cheap labour (= workers who are paid very little, especially unfairly).
  - cheap to do Game shows are cheap to produce.
  - it is cheap to do something Why go by train when it's so cheap to hop on a plane?

### [check](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/check_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃek/` · **US:** `/tʃek/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/check/check__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/check/check__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - check something She gave me the minutes of the meeting to read and check.
  - Check the oil and water before setting off.
  - Check your work before handing it in.
  - Customs officers have the right to check all luggage going through customs.
  - The cars were checked to see whether the faults had been spotted.
  - check something for something Check the container for cracks or leaks.
  - Be sure to check labels for specific information.
  - check something against something I'll need to check these figures against last year's.

### [cheese](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cheese) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃiːz/` · **US:** `/tʃiːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/chees/cheese__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/chees/cheese__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Cheddar cheese
  - (British English) goat’s cheese (= made from the milk of a goat)
  - a cheese sandwich
  - a piece/slice/block of cheese
  - I had cheese on toast (= toasted bread covered in melted cheese) for lunch.
  - a selection of French cheeses
  - a cheese knife (= a knife with a special curved blade with two points on the end, used for cutting and picking up pieces of cheese)
  - a snack of bread and cheese
  - a wedge/hunk/chunk of cheese
  - a cheese sauce/salad

### [chicken](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chicken_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃɪkɪn/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃɪkɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/chi/chick/chicken__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/chi/chick/chicken__us_2.mp3)

  - the sound of chickens clucking

### [child](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/child) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃaɪld/` · **US:** `/tʃaɪld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/chi/child/child__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/chi/child/child__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a child of 3/a 3-year-old child
  - men, women and children
  - an organization that campaigns for the rights of the unborn child
  - The film is not suitable for young children.

### [chocolate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chocolate) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃɒklət/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃɔːklət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cho/choco/chocolate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cho/choco/chocolate__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a chocolate bar
  - chocolate chip cookies
  - a chocolate cake
  - a chocolate factory
  - a bar/piece of chocolate
  - strawberries dipped in chocolate

### [choose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/choose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃuːz/` · **US:** `/tʃuːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cho/choos/choose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cho/choos/choose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You choose—I can't decide.
  - choose between A and/or B She had to choose between staying in the UK or going home.
  - choose something Sarah chose her words carefully.
  - It depends which career path you choose.
  - There are several different options you can choose.
  - This site has been chosen for the new school.
  - choose A from B We have to choose a new manager from a shortlist of five candidates.
  - There are plenty of restaurants to choose from.
  - choose A over B The children chose milk over juice.
  - choose somebody/something as/for something He chose banking as a career.
  - We chose Phil McSweeney as/for chairperson.
  - choose whether, what, etc… You'll have to choose whether to buy it or not.
  - choose to do something We chose to go by train.
  - They chose to ignore my advice.
  - choose somebody to be/do something We chose Phil McSweeney to be chairperson.

### [cinema](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cinema) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪnəmɑː/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪnəmə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cin/cinem/cinema__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cin/cinem/cinema__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the local cinema
  - in a cinema The film has just opened in cinemas across the UK.
  - We were sitting in the cinema, waiting for the film to begin.
  - at a cinema a screening at a cinema in Leicester Square in London

### [city](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/city) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪti/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cit/city_/city__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cit/city_/city__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Riots occurred in all the major cities.
  - the country’s capital city
  - Mexico City has a population of more than 20 million people.
  - Manchester City Football Club
  - in a city We live in a big city.
  - the city of… a map of the city of Boston

### [class](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/class_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klɑːs/` · **US:** `/klæs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/class/class__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/class/class__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in the/your class We were in the same class at school.
  - She is the youngest in her class.
  - He came top of the class.
  - The whole class was/were told to stay behind after school.

### [classroom](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/classroom) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklɑːsrʊm/` · **US:** `/ˈklæsrʊm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/class/classroom__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/class/classroom__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - classroom activities

### [clean](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clean_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kliːn/` · **US:** `/kliːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cle/clean/clean__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cle/clean/clean__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Are your hands clean?
  - She wiped all the surfaces clean.
  - (British English) It is your responsibility to keep the room clean and tidy.
  - (North American English) Keep your room neat and clean.
  - I can't find a clean shirt (= one I haven't worn since it was washed).
  - The hotel was spotlessly (= extremely) clean.
  - The council does a good job keeping the streets clean.

### [clean](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clean_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kliːn/` · **US:** `/kliːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cle/clean/clean__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cle/clean/clean__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I spent all day cooking and cleaning.
  - clean something to clean the windows/bath/floor/house
  - His mother told him to clean his room.
  - He gently cleaned the wound and dressed it.
  - Have you cleaned your teeth?
  - The villa is cleaned twice a week.

### [climb](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/climb_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/klaɪm/` · **US:** `/klaɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/climb/climb__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/climb/climb__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - climb (up) something to climb a mountain/tree
  - She climbed up the stairs.
  - to climb a ladder/wall
  - They climbed the steps to the front door.
  - The car slowly climbed the hill.
  - The boarding party began to climb up the side of the ship.
  - climb (up) As they climbed higher, the air became cooler.
  - You can climb to the top of the tower and take in the view.
  - He climbed up and inspected the damage to the roof.

### [clock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clock_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klɒk/` · **US:** `/klɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/clock/clock__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/clock/clock__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The clock struck twelve/midnight.
  - The clock is fast/slow (= showing a time later/earlier than the true time).
  - The clock has stopped.
  - The clock is right/wrong.
  - My clock said 9.02.
  - by a clock It was ten past six by the kitchen clock.
  - the clock face (= the front part of a clock with the numbers on)
  - The hands of the clock crept slowly around.
  - The sound of a clock ticking somewhere in the house kept him awake.
  - Ellen heard the loud ticking of the clock in the hall.
  - on a clock She checked the time on the clock.

### [close](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/close1_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kləʊz/` · **US:** `/kləʊz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/close/close__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/close/close__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He closed the door firmly.
  - Would anyone mind if I closed the window?
  - She closed the gate behind her.
  - It's dark now—let's close the curtains.
  - I closed my eyes against the bright light.
  - The doors open and close automatically.

### [clothes](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clothes) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kləʊz/` · **US:** `/kləʊz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/cloth/clothes__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/cloth/clothes__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I bought some new clothes for the trip.
  - They were dressed in designer clothes (= clothes designed by famous fashion designers).
  - to put on/take off your clothes
  - clean/dirty clothes
  - a police officer in civilian clothes (= not in uniform)
  - work clothes (= for wearing to work)
  - She was wearing casual clothes.
  - maternity/baby clothes (= for pregnant women/babies)
  - winter/summer clothes
  - a clothes shop
  - She has no clothes sense (= she does not know what clothes look attractive).
  - Bring a change of clothes with you.

### [club](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/club_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klʌb/` · **US:** `/klʌb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clu/club_/club__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clu/club_/club__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - For details on club membership email the address below.
  - The club hopes to raise at least $10 000 for six local charities.

### [coat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coat_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊt/` · **US:** `/kəʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coat_/coat__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coat_/coat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to wear a coat
  - to put on/take off your coat
  - a fur coat
  - a leather coat
  - a long winter coat
  - in a coat a man in a black coat
  - I dressed in my finest blue wool coat.
  - He slipped the letter into his coat pocket.

### [coffee](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coffee) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒfi/` · **US:** `/ˈkɔːfi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cof/coffe/coffee__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cof/coffe/coffee__us_1_rr.mp3)
