### [cold](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cold_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊld/` · **US:** `/kəʊld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/cold_/cold__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/cold_/cold__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm cold. Turn the heating up.
  - to feel cold
  - cold weather/temperatures/air
  - a cold day/night/winter/wind
  - a cold room/house
  - cold hands and feet
  - a cold beer
  - hot and cold water in every room
  - I was thoroughly cold and wet now.
  - It's freezing cold.
  - Hurry up—your dinner's getting cold
  - a bitterly cold morning
  - You look cold. Do you want to go back indoors?
  - Isn't it cold today?
  - It has suddenly turned very cold.
  - the coldest May on record
  - (British English) The water has gone cold.

### [cold](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cold_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊld/` · **US:** `/kəʊld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/cold_/cold__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/cold_/cold__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've got a cold.
  - to have a cold
  - a bad/heavy/slight/nasty cold
  - to catch a cold
  - If you stay out in the rain you'll catch cold!
  - to get a cold
  - She was suffering from a cold.

### [college](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/college) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒlɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːlɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colle/college__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colle/college__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a secretarial college
  - the Royal College of Art
  - a college course/student
  - She wanted to go to college to become a nurse.
  - at college She's at college.

### [colour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/colour_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌlə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌlər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colou/colour__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colou/colour__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's your favourite colour?
  - bright/vibrant/bold colours
  - She always wears dark colours.
  - I'd like to paint this room in a lighter colour.
  - a warm colour palette (= the range of colours used for something)
  - available in 12 different colours
  - the colour of the sky
  - skin/hair colour
  - in colour red/green/blue, etc. in colour
  - Her hair is a reddish-brown colour.
  - The stage lights changed colour from red to blue.
  - Foods which go through a factory process lose much of their colour, flavour and texture.

### [come](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/come_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kʌm/` · **US:** `/kʌm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/come_/come__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/come_/come__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adv./prep. He left and said he was never coming back.
  - He came into the room and shut the door.
  - She comes to work by bus.
  - My son is coming home soon.
  - Come here!
  - Come and see us soon!
  - Here comes Jo! (= Jo is coming)
  - There's a storm coming.
  - come to do something They're coming to stay for a week.

### [common](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/common_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmən/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commo/common__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commo/common__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a common problem/occurrence/practice
  - a common feature/theme
  - the most common type of injury
  - Breast cancer is the most common form of cancer among women in this country.
  - Jackson is a common English name.
  - Some birds that were once a common sight are now becoming rare.
  - a common spelling mistake
  - Heart disease was the most common cause of death in those aged over 85.
  - common in somebody/something The disease is very common in young horses.
  - Allergies to milk are quite common in childhood.

### [company](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/company) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌmpəni/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌmpəni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compa/company__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compa/company__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - insurance/oil/tech/technology/pharmaceutical companies
  - It is the world's largest software company.
  - He works for the National Bus Company.
  - Carrington and Company of London
  - a large/small company
  - She runs her own TV production company.
  - to own/start/found a company
  - Smoking in the workplace is against company policy.
  - Company profits were 5 per cent lower than last year.
  - a company executive/director
  - a company pension

### [compare](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compare_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpeə(r)/` · **US:** `/kəmˈper/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compa/compare__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compa/compare__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - compare A and B It is interesting to compare their situation and ours.
  - We compared the two reports carefully.
  - The internet allows you to compare prices from a variety of companies.
  - How can you compare the two things? They are so different!
  - Compare and contrast the characters of Jack and Ralph.
  - compare A with/to B We compared the results of our study with those of other studies.
  - My own problems seem insignificant compared with other people's.
  - I've had some difficulties, but they were nothing compared to yours (= they were not nearly as bad as yours).
  - Standards in healthcare have improved enormously compared to 40 years ago.
  - They receive just over three years of schooling, compared to a national average of 7.3.
  - an increase of over 11% compared to the same period last year
  - About a third of American adults are obese compared with 24 per cent of Britons

### [complete](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complete_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpliːt/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpliːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complete__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complete__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a complete list/sequence/picture/profile
  - I've collected the complete set.
  - a complete guide to events in Oxford
  - the complete works of Tolstoy
  - You will receive payment for each complete day that you work.
  - You've made my life complete.
  - A Chinese New Year celebration would not be complete without fireworks.
  - No trip to Moscow would be complete without a visit to Lenin's tomb.

### [complete](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complete_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpliːt/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpliːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complete__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complete__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to complete a course/project
  - to complete a task/mission
  - to complete your education/training
  - She's just completed a master's degree in Law.
  - He has recently completed his first year at Durham University.
  - The work should be completed by December.
  - She successfully completed the London Marathon in April.

### [computer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/computer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpjuːtə(r)/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpjuːtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compu/computer__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compu/computer__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a home computer
  - by computer The whole process is run and monitored by computer.
  - on a computer He spends all day playing on his computer.
  - a computer system/program/network
  - computer software/graphics/hardware
  - a computer screen
  - a computer programmer/user
  - a computer virus
  - computer-aided design

### [concert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/concert) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnsət/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnsərt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcert__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconc/xconcert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to attend/go to a concert
  - The orchestra gave a concert in Miami in January.
  - a series of concerts organized by the Oxford Chamber Music Society
  - a classical/rock/pop concert
  - a benefit/charity concert
  - in concert They're in concert at Wembley arena.
  - at a concert They also performed at a concert in Rome last month.
  - concert of something a concert of music by Bach
  - concert by somebody/something I got dragged along to concerts by bands I hated.
  - concert for somebody/something a fundraising concert for tsunami victims
  - a concert hall
  - a concert pianist/tour/series

### [conversation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conversation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒnvəˈseɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːnvərˈseɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconversation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconv/xconversation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a phone conversation
  - The main topic of conversation was the likely outcome of the election.
  - I tried to make conversation (= to speak in order to appear polite).
  - conversation with somebody I had a long conversation with her the other day.
  - She was engaged in conversation with a colleague.
  - (British English) to get into conversation with somebody
  - (North American English) to get into a conversation with somebody
  - conversation between A and B I overheard a conversation between two colleagues.
  - conversation about something We had to listen to endless conversations about high prices and food shortages.
  - in conversation Don was deep in conversation with the girl on his right.
  - I spent an hour in conversation with him.
  - The conversation turned to gardening.
  - Desperately he struggled to keep the conversation going.
  - I see no point in continuing this conversation.
  - She tried to steer the conversation away from the topic of marriage.
  - He felt a sudden alarm at the turn the conversation was taking.

### [cook](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cook_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kʊk/` · **US:** `/kʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/cook_/cook__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/cook_/cook__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where did you learn to cook?
  - Add the onion and cook for three minutes.
  - cook something to cook a meal
  - to cook food/dinner
  - What's the best way to cook trout?
  - (especially British English) I always have a cooked breakfast (= consisting of cooked food)
  - cook something for somebody He cooked lunch for me.
  - cook somebody something He cooked me lunch.

### [cooking](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cooking_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʊkɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈkʊkɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/cooki/cooking__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/cooki/cooking__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My husband does all the cooking.
  - a book on Indian cooking
  - We always use olive oil for cooking.
  - Cooking is her passion.

### [cool](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cool_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kuːl/` · **US:** `/kuːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/cool_/cool__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/cool_/cool__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cool air/water
  - a cool breeze/drink/climate
  - Cooler weather is forecast for the weekend.
  - These plants prefer cooler temperatures.
  - Let's sit in the shade and keep cool.
  - The wooden shutters help keep the building cool.
  - Store lemons in a cool dry place.

### [correct](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correct_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrekt/` · **US:** `/kəˈrekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correct__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correct__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you have the correct time?
  - the correct answer
  - When asked to pick the correct meaning from four alternatives, students consistently chose the correct one.
  - Please check that these details are correct.
  - ‘Are you in charge here?’ ‘That's correct.’
  - As always, your grandmother is absolutely correct.
  - correct in something They were correct in their assessment of the situation.
  - correct in doing something Am I correct in saying that you know a lot about wine?
  - it is correct to do something It is correct to say that Camus's philosophy can be divided into two periods.
  - it is correct (that)… It is correct that there are some similarities between the two cases.

### [correct](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correct_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrekt/` · **US:** `/kəˈrekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correct__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correct__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - correct something to correct an error
  - Read through your work and correct any mistakes that you find.
  - I thought we had corrected the problem.
  - Their eyesight can be corrected in just a few minutes by the use of a laser.
  - They issued a statement correcting the one they had made earlier.
  - correct yourself He stopped and corrected himself. ‘I mean fifteen, not fifty.’
  - correct something for something The data were corrected for differences in age.

### [cost](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cost_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɒst/` · **US:** `/kɔːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cos/cost_/cost__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cos/cost_/cost__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cost of something the high/low cost of housing
  - at a cost of something A new computer system has been installed at a cost of £80 000.
  - You could buy a used car at a fraction of the cost of a new one.
  - We did not even make enough money to cover the cost of the food.
  - Consumers will have to bear the full cost of these pay increases.
  - The plan had to be abandoned on grounds of cost.
  - cost to somebody The total cost to you (= the amount you have to pay) is £3 000.
  - cost for somebody/something Energy is a major cost for businesses.
  - She estimates the cost for this project at $150 000.

### [cost](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cost_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɒst/` · **US:** `/kɔːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cos/cost_/cost__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cos/cost_/cost__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cost something How much did it cost?
  - I didn't get it because it cost too much.
  - Tickets cost ten dollars each.
  - Calls to the helpline cost 45p per minute.
  - Don't use too much of it—it cost a lot of money.
  - All these reforms will cost money (= be expensive).
  - Good food need not cost a fortune (= cost a lot of money).
  - cost somebody something The meal cost us about £40.
  - This is costing the taxpayer £10 billion a year.
  - cost something to do something The hospital will cost an estimated £2 billion to build.
  - It costs a fortune to fly first class.

### [could](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/could) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/kʊd/` · **US:** `/kʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/could/could__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/could/could__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She said that she couldn't come.
  - I couldn't hear what they were saying.
  - Sorry, I couldn't get any more.

### [country](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/country) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌntri/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌntri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/country__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/country__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - European countries
  - developing countries
  - It's good to meet people from different parts of the country.
  - in a country She didn't know what life in a foreign country would be like.
  - It's the most popular music festival in the country.
  - across the country House prices are rising across the whole country.
  - around the country Demonstrations were held in cities around the country.
  - from a country people from other countries

### [course](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/course_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːs/` · **US:** `/kɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/cours/course__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/cours/course__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a French/chemistry course
  - course in something He took a course in Russian language and literature.
  - I want to do a course in art and design.
  - The department offers short courses in drama, fiction, and poetry.
  - course on something She teaches a course on internet crime.
  - on a course My boss has been on a course this week.
  - You need to go on a management training course.
  - He has recently completed the five-week course.
  - The college runs specialist language courses.
  - I enrolled in a ten-week online course.

### [cousin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cousin) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌzn/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌzn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/cousi/cousin__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/cousi/cousin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's my cousin.
  - We're cousins.
  - This is my cousin Richard.
  - He's a cousin once removed (= separated by one generation of our family).
  - She's a cousin of the bride.
  - cousin to somebody He claims to be a cousin to the Queen.

### [cow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cow_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kaʊ/` · **US:** `/kaʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cow/cow__/cow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cow/cow__/cow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to milk a cow
  - cow’s milk
  - a herd of dairy cows (= cows kept for their milk)

### [cream](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cream_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kriːm/` · **US:** `/kriːm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/cream/cream__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/cream/cream__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We had strawberries and cream for dessert.
  - whipped cream
  - a dollop of fresh cream
  - scones with cream and jam
  - I asked for two coffees with cream.
  - Would you like milk or cream in your coffee?
  - (British English) cream cakes (= containing cream)

### [create](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/create) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kriˈeɪt/` · **US:** `/kriˈeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/creat/create__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/creat/create__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Scientists disagree about how the universe was created.
  - The main purpose of industry is to create wealth.
  - The government plans to create more jobs for young people.
  - Create a new directory and put all your files into it.
  - Try this new dish, created by our head chef.
  - The agreement will create new opportunities for the aviation industry.
  - Severe storms created some travel problems.
  - the newly created position of executive editor

### [culture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/culture_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌltʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌltʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cul/cultu/culture__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cul/cultu/culture__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - African/American/European/Islamic culture
  - working-class culture
  - the study of Japanese language and culture
  - Cooking is a hugely important part of French culture.
  - He is no longer in touch with youth culture.
  - The film examines the culture clash between the generations.

### [cup](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cup_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kʌp/` · **US:** `/kʌp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cup/cup__/cup__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cup/cup__/cup__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He filled the cup with water.
  - a coffee cup
  - a cup and saucer
  - a plastic/paper cup

### [customer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/customer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌstəmə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌstəmər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cus/custo/customer__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cus/custo/customer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - marketing strategies to target potential customers
  - The company rewards loyal customers with discounts.
  - one of the shop’s best customers
  - They know me—I'm a regular customer.
  - the customer service department
  - Improving customer satisfaction is a core company strategy.
  - The firm has excellent customer relations.
  - How do you attract new online customers?
  - Our success is measured by how well we serve our customers.

### [cut](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cut_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kʌt/` · **US:** `/kʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cut/cut__/cut__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cut/cut__/cut__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cut something She cut her finger on a piece of glass.
  - cut yourself He cut himself (= his face) shaving.
  - Sometimes people experiencing distress cut themselves (= deliberately).
  - cut something + adj. She had fallen and cut her head open.
  - cut into something She picked up the knife and cut into the meat.
  - cut through something You need a powerful saw to cut through metal.
  - (figurative) The canoe cut through the water.


## D

### [dad](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dad) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dæd/` · **US:** `/dæd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dad/dad_1/dad_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dad/dad__/dad__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That's my dad over there.
  - Do you live with your mum or your dad?
  - Is it OK if I borrow the car, Dad?

### [dance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dance_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɑːns/` · **US:** `/dæns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dan/dance/dance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dan/dance/dance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you know any other Latin American dances?
  - The next dance will be a waltz.
  - The girls performed a graceful dance.
  - a dance class/routine
  - Find a partner and practise these new dance steps.

### [dance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dance_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɑːns/` · **US:** `/dæns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dan/dance/dance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dan/dance/dance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you want to dance?
  - He asked me to dance.
  - They stayed up all night singing and dancing.
  - dance to something They danced to the music of a string quartet.
  - dance with somebody Ruth danced all evening with Richard.
  - dance together Ruth and Richard danced together all evening.

### [dancer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dancer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɑːnsə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdænsər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dan/dance/dancer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dan/dance/dancer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's a fantastic dancer.
  - a ballet dancer
  - He's a principal dancer with the Royal Ballet.

### [dancing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dancing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɑːnsɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈdænsɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dan/danci/dancing__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dan/danci/dancing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There was music and dancing till two in the morning.
  - I love Irish dancing.
  - dancing classes

### [dangerous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dangerous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdeɪndʒərəs/` · **US:** `/ˈdeɪndʒərəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dan/dange/dangerous__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dan/dange/dangerous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The situation is extremely dangerous.
  - Fog and ice are making the roads very dangerous.
  - potentially dangerous levels of pesticides
  - It's one of the most dangerous places in the world.
  - The prisoners who escaped are violent and dangerous.
  - (British English) a conviction for dangerous driving
  - dangerous for somebody Poor air quality is particularly dangerous for young children.
  - dangerous for somebody to do something It would be dangerous for you to stay here.
  - dangerous to somebody/something The evidence is clear that smoking is dangerous to health.

### [dark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dark_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɑːk/` · **US:** `/dɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dar/dark_/dark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dar/dark_/dark__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dark room/street/forest/night
  - What time does it get dark in summer?
  - Then the theatre went dark.
  - It was dark outside and I couldn't see much.
  - The sky was still dark.
  - Use lights to illuminate dark corners.

### [date](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/date_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/deɪt/` · **US:** `/deɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dat/date_/date__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dat/date_/date__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘What's the date today?’ ‘The 10th.’
  - Write today's date at the top of the page.
  - They haven't set a date for the wedding yet.
  - We need to fix a date for the next meeting.
  - I can't come on that date.
  - Please give your name, address and date of birth.
  - (especially North American English) name, address and birth date
  - There's no word yet on a UK release date for the film.
  - a start/launch/completion date
  - There's no date on this letter.
  - Anyone who knows of the vehicle’s whereabouts from that date until 31 October is asked to contact Bangor police station.

### [daughter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/daughter) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɔːtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdɔːtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dau/daugh/daughter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dau/daugh/daughter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We have two sons and a daughter.
  - a baby/teenage daughter
  - They have three grown-up daughters.
  - She's the eldest daughter of an Oxford professor.
  - our younger/youngest daughter
  - Living alone and trying to bring up a small daughter is no easy task.

### [day](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/day) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/deɪ/` · **US:** `/deɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/day/day__/day__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/day/day__/day__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘What day is it today?’ ‘Monday.’
  - I go to the gym every day.
  - We spent five days in Paris.
  - I saw Tom three days ago.
  - We're going away in a few days.
  - The house should be ready in a few days' time.
  - The situation has been deteriorating for the past few days.
  - within two, 30, etc. days Full payment must be made within 21 days.
  - On that day Rosa Parks did something that changed history.
  - I saw her again the next day.
  - He called me to apologize three days later.
  - He resigned the following day.
  - We'd had this big argument the day before.
  - They left the day before yesterday (= two days ago).
  - We're meeting the day after tomorrow (= in two days).
  - We can't go there today. You can go another day.
  - New Year’s Day
  - a/per day Take the medicine three times a day.
  - They are producing 1.5 million barrels of oil per day.
  - The helpline is open 24 hours a day.

### [dear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dear_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/dear_/dear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/dear_/dear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Dear Sir or Madam
  - Dear Mrs Jones
  - Dear Beth/Dad/Aunt Susan
  - (old-fashioned) My dear sister

### [December](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/december) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈsembə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsembər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decem/december_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decem/december__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was born in December.
  - (British English) The meeting is on the fifth of December/December the fifth.
  - (North American English) The meeting is on December fifth.
  - We went to Japan last December.
  - I arrived at the end of December.
  - last December's celebrations
  - It was a cold December morning.

### [decide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/decide) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈsaɪd/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decid/decide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decid/decide__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's up to you to decide.
  - You will have to decide soon.
  - I can't tell you what to do—you'll have to decide for yourself.
  - decide to do something We've decided not to go away after all.
  - Why did you decide to look for a new job?
  - She gave up politics and decided instead to focus on charity work.
  - decide (that)… The government has already decided that the law needs to be changed.
  - She decided that she wanted to live in France.
  - decide what, whether, etc… You have the right to decide what you want to do.
  - I can't decide what to wear.
  - She couldn’t decide whether he was telling the truth or not.
  - decide between A and B It was difficult to decide between the two candidates.
  - decide against something She finally decided against a career in medicine.
  - decide against doing something They decided against taking legal action.
  - decide something Sales figures will ultimately decide the future of these types of games.
  - We might be hiring more people but nothing has been decided yet.
  - The venue for the concert has yet to be decided.
  - it is decided (that)… It was decided that the school should purchase new software.

### [delicious](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/delicious) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈlɪʃəs/` · **US:** `/dɪˈlɪʃəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/del/delic/delicious__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/del/delic/delicious__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who cooked this? It's absolutely delicious.
  - to taste/smell/look delicious
  - a delicious meal/recipe/treat
  - This dish is delicious with cream.
  - The food was delicious.

### [describe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/describe) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈskraɪb/` · **US:** `/dɪˈskraɪb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/descr/describe__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/descr/describe__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - describe somebody/something The next section describes our findings in detail.
  - to describe a method/process/situation
  - describe somebody/something to somebody Can you describe him to me?
  - describe somebody/something for somebody They described for us exactly what happened.
  - describe somebody/something as something The man was described as tall and dark, and aged about 20.
  - describe yourself as something She describes herself as an artist.
  - describe how, what, etc… Describe how you did it.
  - Words cannot describe what I felt at that moment.
  - describe doing something Witnesses described seeing strange lights in the sky.

### [description](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/description) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈskrɪpʃn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈskrɪpʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/descr/description__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/descr/description__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Follow the link below for a more detailed description.
  - description of somebody/something a brief description of the software
  - The name means ‘no trees’ and it is an accurate description of the island.
  - Police have issued a description of the gunman.
  - This is a film that defies description (= is difficult or impossible to describe).
  - the novelist’s powers of description
  - beyond description a personal pain that goes beyond description (= is too great to express in words)

### [design](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/design_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈzaɪn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈzaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desig/design__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desig/design__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The basic design of the car is very similar to that of earlier models.
  - special new design features
  - The system has a fundamental design flaw.
  - The steel frame is left visible and is part of the overall design concept.
  - The magazine will appear in a new design from next month.

### [design](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/design_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈzaɪn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈzaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desig/design__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desig/design__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - design something He designed and built his own house.
  - to design a building/product/website
  - a well designed kitchen
  - The blocks were specially designed and manufactured in Italy.
  - design something for somebody/something They asked me to design a poster for the campaign.
  - design somebody something Could you design us a poster?

### [desk](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/desk) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/desk/` · **US:** `/desk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desk_/desk__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desk_/desk__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at a desk I spend all day sitting at a desk.
  - behind a desk The mayor sat behind his desk.
  - a desk drawer/lamp/calendar
  - He abandoned a career at sea for a desk job.

### [detail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/detail_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdiːteɪl/` · **US:** `/dɪˈteɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/det/detai/detail__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/det/detai/detail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an expedition planned down to the last detail
  - The Ministry refused to reveal any more details of the attack.
  - Tell me the main points now; leave the details till later.
  - The finer details of the plan have still to be worked out.
  - These are all relatively minor details.
  - ‘We had a terrible time—’ ‘Oh, spare me the details (= don't tell me any more).’

### [dialogue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dialogue) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdaɪəlɒɡ/` · **US:** `/ˈdaɪəlɔːɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dia/dialo/dialogue__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dia/dialo/dialogue__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Learners are asked to listen to three short dialogues.
  - Practise the dialogue with a partner.
  - The novel has long descriptions and not much dialogue.

### [dictionary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dictionary) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪkʃənri/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪkʃəneri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dic/dicti/dictionary__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dic/dicti/dictionary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a Spanish-English dictionary
  - a slang dictionary
  - a dictionary of slang
  - in a dictionary If you don't know the meaning of a word, look it up in the dictionary.

### [die](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/die_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/daɪ/` · **US:** `/daɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/die/die__/die__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/die/die__/die__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My father died suddenly at the age of 48.
  - At least six people have died in the accident.
  - That plant's going to die if you don't water it!
  - He later died in hospital.
  - They died fighting for their country.
  - She died peacefully after a long illness.
  - to die with dignity
  - die of/from something to die of/from cancer (= cancer is the cause of death)
  - die with something A further 156 people have died with Covid-19 (= they had the disease when they died).
  - die for something He was ready to die for his cause.
  - I'll never forget it to my dying day (= until I die).
  - (informal) I nearly died when I saw him there (= it was very embarrassing).
  - die a… death to die a natural/slow/horrible/violent death
  - die + adj. She died young.
  - At least they died happy.
  - die + noun He died a hero.
  - He died a poor man.

### [diet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/diet_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdaɪət/` · **US:** `/ˈdaɪət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/die/diet_/diet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/die/diet_/diet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to eat a healthy, balanced diet
  - I loved the Japanese diet of rice, vegetables and fish.
  - a vegetarian/vegan diet
  - The traditional Mediterranean diet is fairly high in fat.
  - to receive advice on diet

### [difference](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/difference) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪfrəns/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪfrəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dif/diffe/difference__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dif/diffe/difference__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Americans and Brits speak the same language, but there are big cultural differences.
  - difference between A and B There are no significant differences between the education systems of the two countries.
  - He was studying the similarities and differences between humans and animals.
  - I can never tell the difference (= distinguish) between the twins.
  - difference in something There's no difference in the results.
  - There is a fundamental difference in approach to conservation in Japan.
  - She noticed a marked difference in the children on her second visit.
  - Her work questions notions of gender difference.
  - What a difference! You look great with your hair like that.
  - difference among something Differences among beetle species may prove to be a key factor.
  - despite differences Despite their differences, the two are great friends.

### [different](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/different) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪfrənt/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪfrənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dif/diffe/different__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dif/diffe/different__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - different from somebody/something American English is significantly different from British English.
  - different to somebody/something (especially British English) It's slightly different to what I'm used to.
  - different than somebody/something (especially North American English) He's different than the rest.
  - It's completely different now than it was a year ago.
  - People often give very different accounts of the same event.
  - My son's terribly untidy; my daughter's no different.
  - The room looks different without the furniture.
  - Now he spoke in a different and kinder voice.
  - Let's try a different approach.

### [difficult](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/difficult) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪfɪkəlt/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪfɪkəlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dif/diffi/difficult__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dif/diffi/difficult__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The competition judges were given a very difficult task.
  - a difficult decision/question
  - difficult for somebody Asking for help is extremely difficult for some people.
  - it is difficult to do something It's really difficult to read your writing.
  - something is difficult to do Your writing is really difficult to read.
  - It is difficult to imagine a woman in the 1950s behaving like this.
  - She finds it very difficult to get up early.
  - it is difficult for somebody to do something It's difficult for them to get here much before seven.
  - It has become increasingly difficult for young people to buy a house.

### [dinner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dinner) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɪnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdɪnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/din/dinne/dinner__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/din/dinne/dinner__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's time for dinner.
  - When do you have dinner?
  - for dinner What shall we have for dinner tonight?
  - to dinner As I'm single, I tend to go out to dinner (= eat in a restaurant) a lot.
  - (North American English also) to go to dinner
  - I'd like to take you out to dinner tonight.
  - Let's invite them to dinner tomorrow.
  - What time do you serve dinner?
  - It's your turn to cook dinner.
  - She didn't eat much dinner.
  - I never eat a big dinner.
  - Christmas/Thanksgiving dinner
  - a three-course dinner
  - a roast/steak/turkey dinner
  - a family sitting around the dinner table
  - a dinner plate
  - He was trying hard to impress his dinner guests.
  - (British English) school dinners (= meals provided at school in the middle of the day)

### [dirty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dirty_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɜːti/` · **US:** `/ˈdɜːrti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dir/dirty/dirty__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dir/dirty/dirty__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - dirty hands/clothes/dishes
  - My thumb had left a dirty mark on the paper.
  - Try not to get too dirty!
  - She didn't want to get her dress dirty.
  - They found him huddled under a dirty old blanket
  - I always get given the dirty jobs (= jobs that make you become dirty).

### [discuss](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/discuss) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈskʌs/` · **US:** `/dɪˈskʌs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/discu/discuss__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/discu/discuss__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - discuss something with somebody Have you discussed the problem with anyone?
  - discuss something I'm not prepared to discuss this on the phone.
  - to discuss the issue/matter/topic
  - to discuss a plan/proposal
  - They met to discuss the possibility of working together.
  - discuss when, what, etc… We need to discuss when we should go.
  - discuss doing something We briefly discussed buying a second car.
  - discuss somebody/something doing something They discussed the whole family possibly travelling together.

### [dish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dish_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪʃ/` · **US:** `/dɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dis/dish_/dish__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dis/dish_/dish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a baking/serving dish
  - a casserole dish
  - in a dish Bake in a shallow dish for 45 mins.
  - dish of something They helped themselves from a large dish of pasta.

### [do](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/do1_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/duː/` · **US:** `/duː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/do_/do__g/do__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/do_/do__u/do__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What are you doing this evening?
  - We will do what we can to help.
  - Are you doing anything tomorrow evening?
  - The company ought to do something about the poor service.
  - What have you done to your hair?
  - There's nothing to do (= no means of passing the time in an enjoyable way) in this place.
  - There's nothing we can do about it (= we can't change the situation).
  - What can I do for you (= how can I help)?

### [do](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/do1_2) *(auxiliary verb)*
- **UK:** `/duː/` · **US:** `/duː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/do_/do__g/do__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/do_/do__u/do__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't like fish.
  - They didn't go to Paris.
  - Don't forget to write.
  - Does she speak French?

### [doctor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/doctor_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɒktə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdɑːktər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/doc/docto/doctor__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/doc/docto/doctor__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You'd better see a doctor about that cough.
  - to ask/consult/visit your doctor
  - The doctor prescribed antibiotics.
  - The doctor told me to stay in bed.
  - What does your doctor advise?
  - Dr Staples (= as a title/form of address)
  - He's training to be a doctor.

### [dog](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dog_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɒɡ/` · **US:** `/dɔːɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dog/dog__/dog__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dog/dog__/dog__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I took the dog for a walk.
  - I'm just going to walk the dog.
  - I could hear a dog barking.
  - Stray dogs roamed the streets at night.
  - dog walkers/owners
  - dog food/biscuits
  - a dog and its puppies
  - A labrador is a breed of dog.

### [dollar](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dollar) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɒlə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdɑːlər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dol/dolla/dollar__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dol/dolla/dollar__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You will be paid in American dollars.
  - Do you have a dollar?
  - a dollar bill
  - He sold the company in a multimillion-dollar deal.
  - American taxpayers ought to have more say in how their tax dollars are spent.
  - China produces billions of dollars worth of exports.
  - per dollar The company generates an additional $4 in sales per dollar spent on ads.

### [door](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/door) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/dɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/doo/door_/door__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/doo/door_/door__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Open the door!
  - Close the door behind you, please.
  - The door closed behind him.
  - to shut/slam/lock/unlock the door
  - a knock on/at the door
  - to knock/bang on the door
  - to answer the door (= to go and open it because somebody has knocked on it or rung the bell)
  - the front/back/side door (= at the entrance at the front/back/side of a building)
  - the bedroom/kitchen/car/garage door
  - a four-door saloon car
  - the fridge door
  - the door frame/handle/lock
  - door to something There are double doors to the rear garden.
  - The door to his office opened.
  - door into/onto something Each bedroom has a door onto the balcony.

### [down](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/down_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/daʊn/` · **US:** `/daʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dow/down_/down__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dow/down_/down__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She jumped down off the chair.
  - He looked down at her.
  - We watched as the sun went down.
  - She bent down to pick up her glove.
  - Mary's not down yet (= she is still upstairs).
  - The baby can't keep any food down (= in her body).
  - I stuck the label down with sellotape.

### [down](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/down_2) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/daʊn/` · **US:** `/daʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dow/down_/down__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dow/down_/down__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The stone rolled down the hill.
  - Tears ran down her face.
  - Her hair hung down her back to her waist.

### [downstairs](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/downstairs_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdaʊnˈsteəz/` · **US:** `/ˌdaʊnˈsterz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dow/downs/downstairs__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dow/downs/downstairs__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I couldn't sleep so I went downstairs and watched TV.
  - I heard someone coming downstairs.
  - She rushed downstairs and burst into the kitchen.
  - Wait downstairs in the hall.

### [draw](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/draw_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/drɔː/` · **US:** `/drɔː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dra/draw_/draw__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dra/draw_/draw__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You draw beautifully.
  - draw something to draw a picture/map/diagram
  - She drew a house.
  - a crudely drawn child's face
  - draw something with something He drew a circle in the sand with a stick.

### [dress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dress_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dres/` · **US:** `/dres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dre/dress/dress__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dre/dress/dress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a long white dress
  - to wear/put on/take off a dress
  - in a dress a young woman in a pink silk dress

### [dress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dress_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dres/` · **US:** `/dres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dre/dress/dress__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dre/dress/dress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I dressed quickly.
  - Get up and get dressed!
  - dress in something He had dressed in a black T-shirt and jeans.
  - dress somebody/yourself in something She dressed the children in their best clothes.
  - dress yourself You're old enough to dress yourself now.
  - dress somebody/something The children spend hours dressing and undressing their dolls.
  - Her mother always dressed them identically.

### [drink](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/drink_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/drɪŋk/` · **US:** `/drɪŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dri/drink/drink__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dri/drink/drink__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Can I have a drink?
  - Do you want a drink?
  - a hot/cold drink
  - Passengers were left without food and drink for hours.
  - There are snacks and fizzy drinks in the kitchen.
  - We were offered free drinks to make up for the wait.
  - drink of something a drink of water
  - drink from something She took a drink from the glass and then put it down.

### [drink](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/drink_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/drɪŋk/` · **US:** `/drɪŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dri/drink/drink__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dri/drink/drink__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - drink something I don't drink coffee.
  - What would you like to drink?
  - In hot weather, drink plenty of water.
  - drink (from something) She drank from a tall glass.
  - He was drinking straight from the bottle.
  - She opened the can and drank thirstily.

### [drive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/drive_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/draɪv/` · **US:** `/draɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dri/drive/drive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dri/drive/drive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Can you drive?
  - Don't drive so fast!
  - I drove to work this morning.
  - Shall we drive (= go there by car) or go by train?
  - drive something to drive a car/vehicle/truck/bus
  - He drives a taxi (= that is his job).

### [driver](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/driver) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdraɪvə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdraɪvər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dri/drive/driver__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dri/drive/driver__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a bus/train/taxi driver
  - a car/van driver
  - an ambulance driver
  - a good/careful driver
  - She climbed into the driver's seat.
  - The accident was the other driver's fault.
  - Police have warned drivers to take care in the icy conditions.
  - (British English) a learner driver (= one who has not yet passed a driving test)
  - (North American English) a student driver
  - (British English) a lorry driver
  - (especially North American English) a truck driver
  - What he wanted most was to be a racing driver.
  - The car comes equipped with a driver's airbag.
  - The driver of the other car walked away from the crash with minor cuts and bruises.

### [during](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/during) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdjʊərɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈdʊrɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dur/durin/during__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dur/durin/during__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - during the 1990s
  - There are extra flights to Colorado during the winter.
  - Please remain seated during the performance.

### [DVD](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dvd) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌdiː viː ˈdiː/` · **US:** `/ˌdiː viː ˈdiː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dvd/dvd_1/dvd_1_gb_2_abbr.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dvd/dvd__/dvd__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [each](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/each) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/iːtʃ/` · **US:** `/iːtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eac/each_/each__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eac/each_/each__us_1.mp3)

  - Each answer is worth 20 points.
  - Each of the answers is worth 20 points.
  - The answers are worth 20 points each.
  - ‘Red or blue?’ ‘I'll take one of each, please.’
  - Look at the pictures and answer the questions about each one.
  - We each have our own car.
  - There aren't enough books for everyone to have one each.
  - They lost $40 each.
  - Each day that passed he grew more and more desperate.
