### [ear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ear) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ear/ear__/ear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ear/ear__/ear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the left/right ear
  - He put his hands over his ears.
  - She's had her ears pierced.
  - The elephant flapped its ears.
  - in somebody's ear She whispered something in his ear.
  - He was now screaming in my ear.
  - He was always there with a sympathetic ear (= was always willing to listen to people).
  - an ear infection

### [early](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/early_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɜːli/` · **US:** `/ˈɜːrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ear/early/early__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ear/early/early__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the early morning
  - my earliest memories
  - The project is still in the early stages.
  - the early 1990s
  - in the early years of the twentieth century
  - in the early days of space exploration (= when it was just beginning)
  - it is too early to do something It is too early to tell how badly the economy will be affected.
  - He's in his early twenties.
  - Mozart’s early works (= those written at the beginning of his career)
  - Early booking is essential, as space is limited.
  - The ruling overturned the court’s earlier decision.
  - The earliest possible date I can make it is the third.
  - The earliest description of this species dates from 1703.

### [early](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/early_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɜːli/` · **US:** `/ˈɜːrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ear/early/early__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ear/early/early__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We arrived early the next day.
  - early in something early in the week/year/season/morning
  - We meet the hero quite early in the film.
  - as early as something He started writing music as early as 1989.
  - The best rooms go to those who book earliest.

### [east](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/east_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/iːst/` · **US:** `/iːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eas/east_/east__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eas/east_/east__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - East Africa
  - They live on the east coast.
  - the east bank of the river
  - They live on the city's east side.
  - The park and lake are at the east end of the complex.

### [east](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/east_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/iːst/` · **US:** `/iːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eas/east_/east__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eas/east_/east__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The house faces east.
  - The storm is expected to head east.

### [east](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/east_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/iːst/` · **US:** `/iːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eas/east_/east__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eas/east_/east__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which way is east?
  - A gale was blowing from the east.
  - an east-facing terrace
  - to the east (of…) a town to the east of (= further east than) Chicago
  - The train line facilitates travel between east and west.

### [easy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/easy_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈiːzi/` · **US:** `/ˈiːzi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eas/easy_/easy__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eas/easy_/easy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an easy way to make bread
  - Reaching the summit is no easy task.
  - an easy win/victory
  - It's much easier if you speak the language.
  - easy to do something It's easy to forget how we lived before the internet.
  - The tool is quick and easy to use.
  - Their house isn't the easiest place to get to.
  - There are no easy answers in cases like this.
  - Several schools are within easy reach (= not far away).
  - easy for somebody (to do something) It's easy for you to criticize.
  - He didn't make it easy for me to leave.
  - It can't be easy for her, on her own with the children.

### [eat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eat) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/iːt/` · **US:** `/iːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eat/eat__/eat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eat/eat__/eat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was too nervous to eat.
  - to eat well/healthily
  - She doesn't eat properly (= doesn't eat food that is good for her).
  - eat something I don't eat meat.
  - to eat breakfast/lunch/dinner
  - Would you like something to eat?
  - I couldn't eat another thing (= I have had enough food).

### [egg](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/egg_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/eɡ/` · **US:** `/eɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/egg/egg__/egg__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/egg/egg__/egg__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The female sits on the eggs until they hatch.
  - The fish lay thousands of eggs at one time.
  - crocodile eggs

### [eight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eight#eight_sng_1) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/eɪt/` · **US:** `/eɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eig/eight/eight__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eig/eight/eight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are only eight of these rare animals left.
  - eight of Sweden’s top financial experts
  - Ten people were invited but only eight turned up.
  - Can you lend me eight dollars?
  - an eight-month contract
  - Look at page eight.
  - Eight and one is nine.
  - Two eights is sixteen.
  - I can't read your writing—is this meant to be an eight?
  - The bulbs are planted in sixes or eights (= groups of six or eight).
  - We moved to America when I was eight (= eight years old).
  - Shall we meet at eight (= at eight o'clock), then?

### [eighteen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eighteen) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˌeɪˈtiːn/` · **US:** `/ˌeɪˈtiːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eig/eight/eighteen__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eig/eight/eighteen__us_1.mp3)

### [eighty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eighty) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪti/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eig/eight/eighty__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eig/eight/eighty__us_1.mp3)

### [elephant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/elephant) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈelɪfənt/` · **US:** `/ˈelɪfənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ele/eleph/elephant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ele/eleph/elephant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - herds of elephants
  - a bull elephant
  - a baby elephant
  - a conservationist who cares for orphaned elephants

### [eleven](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eleven) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈlevn/` · **US:** `/ɪˈlevn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ele/eleve/eleven__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ele/eleve/eleven__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are only eleven of these rare animals left.
  - eleven of Sweden’s top financial experts
  - Twenty people were invited but only eleven turned up.
  - Can you lend me eleven dollars?
  - an eleven-month contract
  - Look at page eleven.
  - Eleven and two is thirteen.
  - Two elevens are twenty-two.
  - I can't read your writing—is this meant to be an eleven?
  - The bulbs are planted in sevens or elevens (= groups of seven or eleven).
  - We moved to America when I was eleven (= eleven years old).
  - Shall we meet at eleven (= at eleven o'clock), then?

### [else](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/else) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/els/` · **US:** `/els/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/els/else_/else__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/els/else_/else__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What else did he say?
  - I don't want anything else, thanks.
  - I'm taking a few clothes and some books, not much else.

### [email](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/email_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈiːmeɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈiːmeɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ema/email/email__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ema/email/email__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I will be on vacation for a week in November, with no access to email.
  - They use email, conference calls and chat rooms to discuss business.
  - by email to send a message by email
  - via email Automated notifications will be sent via email or text message.
  - through email (especially North American English) Membership notifications go out through email in the fall.
  - What's your email address?
  - an email account/message/attachment
  - an email newsletter/list

### [email](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/email_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈiːmeɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈiːmeɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ema/email/email__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ema/email/email__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - email somebody Patrick emailed me yesterday.
  - email something (to somebody) I'll email the documents to her.
  - email somebody something I'll email her the documents.
  - email for something Call or email for a free information pack.

### [end](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/end_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/end/` · **US:** `/end/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/end__/end__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/end__/end__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's the end of an era.
  - the end of the book
  - at the end of something at the end of the week
  - at the end There’ll be a chance to ask questions at the end.
  - by the end of something He wants the reports by the end of the month.
  - to the end of something She remained active as an artist to the end of her life.
  - towards the end (of something) I was getting bored towards the end of the talk.
  - until the end We didn't leave until the very end.
  - until the end of something I'm staying until the end of this week.
  - We had to hear about the whole journey from beginning to end.
  - He is nearing the end of his career.
  - Our end-of-season sale starts next week.

### [end](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/end_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/end/` · **US:** `/end/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/end/end__/end__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/end/end__/end__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - At last the war ended.
  - The road ends here.
  - How does the story end?
  - The problems did not end there.
  - end with something Her note ended with the words: ‘See you soon.’
  - end by doing something The speaker ended by suggesting some topics for discussion.
  - end something They decided to end their relationship.
  - to end the occupation/conflict/violence
  - A back injury effectively ended her career.
  - end something with something The team ended the season with a 4–0 win.
  - + speech ‘And that was that,’ she ended.

### [enjoy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enjoy) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈdʒɔɪ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈdʒɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/enj/enjoy/enjoy__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/enj/enjoy/enjoy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - enjoy something We thoroughly enjoyed our time in New York.
  - Thanks for a great evening. I really enjoyed it.
  - She seems to be enjoying life in Paris.
  - enjoy doing something I enjoy playing tennis and squash.
  - I really enjoyed watching the film again.

### [enough](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enough_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈnʌf/` · **US:** `/ɪˈnʌf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eno/enoug/enough__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eno/enoug/enough__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I hadn't trained enough for the game.
  - This house isn't big enough for us.
  - She's old enough to decide for herself.
  - We didn't leave early enough.
  - They've been in this business long enough to know what they're doing.
  - Tell them it's just not good enough.

### [enough](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enough_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈnʌf/` · **US:** `/ɪˈnʌf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eno/enoug/enough__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eno/enoug/enough__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Have you made enough copies?
  - Is there enough room for me?
  - I didn't have enough clothes to last a week.
  - Don't ask me to do it. I've got enough problems as it is.
  - (old-fashioned) There was food enough for all.

### [enough](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/enough_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈnʌf/` · **US:** `/ɪˈnʌf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eno/enoug/enough__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eno/enoug/enough__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Six bottles should be enough.
  - Have you had enough (= to eat)?
  - If enough of you are interested, we'll organize a trip to the theatre.
  - There was nowhere near enough for everybody.
  - We've nearly run out of paper. Do you think there's enough for today?

### [euro](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/euro_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjʊərəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈjʊrəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eur/euro_/euro__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eur/euro_/euro__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I paid five euros for it.
  - 10 million euro
  - in euros The price is given in euros.
  - a 30-million-euro deal
  - A half-kilo pack of pasta costs around 70 euro cents.

### [even](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/even_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈiːvn/` · **US:** `/ˈiːvn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/even_/even__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/even_/even__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She didn't even call to say she wasn't coming.
  - He never even opened the letter (= so he certainly didn't read it).
  - It wouldn't have been possible 30 years ago, maybe even 20.
  - It was cold there even in summer (= so it must have been very cold in winter).
  - Even a child can understand it (= so adults certainly can).

### [evening](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/evening) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈiːvnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈiːvnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/eveni/evening__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/eveni/evening__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Remember to call me this evening.
  - We haven't spoken to him since yesterday evening.
  - I'll see you tomorrow evening.
  - Come over on Thursday evening.
  - in the evening What do you usually do in the evening?
  - Mosquitoes usually bite in the early evening.
  - for the evening She's going to her sister's for the evening.
  - on the evening of on the evening of May 15
  - He hardly spoke throughout the evening.
  - We spent many enjoyable summer evenings together.
  - I found it in the pocket of the jacket I had been wearing the previous evening.
  - The evening meal is served at 7 p.m.
  - She watched the evening news at 6 p.m. as usual.
  - As the evening wore on, Sam became more and more unhappy.
  - Jim's comedy routine was the highlight of the evening's entertainment.

### [event](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/event) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈvent/` · **US:** `/ɪˈvent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/event/event__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/event/event__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - recent/current/world events
  - The tragic events of last Monday occurred only 200 metres from the shore.
  - Historians long ago established that the event never happened.
  - TV viewers watched in horror as events unfolded.
  - a dramatic turn of events (= change in what is happening)
  - Everyone was frightened by the strange sequence of events.
  - The police have reconstructed the chain of events leading to the murder.
  - an unfortunate series of events
  - Why should we believe his version of events (= description of what happened from his point of view)?
  - The decisions we take now may influence the course of events (= the way things happen) in the future.
  - In the normal course of events (= if things had happened as expected) she would have gone with him.
  - He found that the report he had written had been overtaken by events (= it was no longer relevant).
  - (medical) A minority of patients suffered adverse events, including heart attacks.

### [ever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ever) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/ever_/ever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/ever_/ever__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Nothing ever happens here.
  - Don't you ever get tired?
  - If you're ever in Miami, come and see us.
  - ‘Have you ever been to Rome?’ ‘Yes, I have, actually. Not long ago.’
  - ‘Have you ever thought of changing your job?’ ‘No, never/No I haven’t.’
  - She hardly ever (= almost never) goes out.
  - We've only ever talked on the phone.
  - I don't think I ever really thanked you properly.
  - He wondered whether it would ever actually happen.
  - We see them very seldom, if ever.
  - (informal) I'll never ever do that again!
  - I'm never speaking to you ever again!

### [every](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/every) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevri/` · **US:** `/ˈevri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/every/every__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/every/every__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She knows every student in the school.
  - I could hear every word they said.
  - We enjoyed every minute of our stay.
  - I go to the gym every day.
  - every week/month/year
  - I get that wrong every single time.
  - I read every last article in the newspaper (= all of them).
  - They were watching her every movement.
  - Every one of their songs has been a hit.

### [everybody](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/everybody) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevribɒdi/` · **US:** `/ˈevribʌdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/every/everybody__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/every/everybody__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Everybody knows Tom.
  - Have you asked everybody?
  - Didn't you like it? Everybody else did.

### [everyone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/everyone) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevriwʌn/` · **US:** `/ˈevriwʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/every/everyone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/every/everyone__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Everyone cheered and clapped.
  - Everyone has a chance to win.
  - Everyone brought their partner to the party.
  - (formal) Everyone brought his or her partner to the party.
  - The police questioned everyone in the room.
  - The teacher commented on everyone's work.
  - Everyone else was there.

### [everything](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/everything) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈevriθɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈevriθɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eve/every/everything__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eve/every/everything__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Everything had gone.
  - When we confronted him, he denied everything.
  - Take this bag, and leave everything else to me.
  - She seemed to have everything—looks, money, intelligence.

### [exam](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exam) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪɡˈzæm/` · **US:** `/ɪɡˈzæm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exa/exam_/exam__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exa/exam_/exam__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to take an exam
  - to pass/fail an exam
  - (British English) I hate doing exams.
  - (British English, formal) to sit an exam
  - (British English) to mark an exam
  - (North American English) to grade an exam
  - I failed the college entrance exam (= to get a place at college).
  - exam in something Every term we would have exams in maths, English, French, etc.
  - in an exam (British English) She did well in her exams.
  - on an exam (North American English) She did well on her exams.
  - an exam paper
  - I got my exam results today.
  - A lot of students suffer from exam nerves.

### [example](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/example) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪɡˈzɑːmpl/` · **US:** `/ɪɡˈzæmpl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exa/examp/example__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exa/examp/example__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let me give you an example.
  - It is important to cite examples to support your argument.
  - to provide/offer an example
  - I shall take four different examples from contemporary literature to illustrate my point.
  - example of something Can you show me an example of what you mean?
  - This dictionary has many examples of how words are used.
  - Just to give you an example of his generosity—he gave me his old car and wouldn't take any money for it.
  - The progress the students have made serves as an example of the benefit of small class sizes.
  - By way of example, we'll look at our most recent advertising campaign.

### [excited](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/excited) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈsaɪtɪd/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈsaɪtɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/excit/excited__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/excit/excited__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Staff got excited when they heard they were getting a bonus.
  - excited about something I am really excited about the opportunity to work with her.
  - excited about doing something The children were excited about opening their presents.
  - excited at something I'm really excited at the prospect of working abroad.
  - excited at doing something She was very excited at becoming a great aunt.
  - excited by something Don't get too excited by the sight of your name in print.
  - excited for somebody I'm so excited for you!
  - excited for something I woke up bright and early, excited for the day ahead.
  - excited to do something He was very excited to be asked to play for Wales.
  - excited (that)… He is very excited that he will get to meet his idol.
  - The new restaurant is nothing to get excited about (= not particularly good).
  - An excited crowd of people gathered around her.

### [exciting](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exciting) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈsaɪtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈsaɪtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exc/excit/exciting__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exc/excit/exciting__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This is an exciting opportunity for me.
  - one of the most exciting developments in biology in recent years
  - an exciting prospect/possibility
  - an exciting story/discovery
  - It's an exciting time for the entire industry.
  - It's the most exciting thing that's happened to these people in a long time.
  - They waited and waited for something exciting to happen.
  - I still find the job exciting.
  - exciting for somebody That's what makes the game so exciting for the fans.
  - it is exciting (for somebody) to do something It was really exciting for them to see him in person.

### [exercise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exercise_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeksəsaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈeksərsaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exe/exerc/exercise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exe/exerc/exercise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Swimming is good exercise.
  - the importance of regular exercise
  - health problems resulting from a lack of physical exercise
  - strenuous/vigorous exercise
  - gentle/moderate exercise
  - I don't get much exercise sitting in the office all day.
  - John never does any exercise.
  - (British English) to take exercise
  - Diet and exercise are equally important.
  - The mind needs exercise as well as the body.

### [exercise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/exercise_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeksəsaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈeksərsaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exe/exerc/exercise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exe/exerc/exercise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an hour’s class of exercising to music
  - How often do you exercise?
  - exercise something Horses need to be exercised regularly.

### [expensive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/expensive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspensɪv/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspensɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expen/expensive__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expen/expensive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an expensive car/restaurant/holiday
  - I can't afford it—it's too expensive.
  - Making the wrong decision could prove expensive.
  - That dress was an expensive mistake.
  - expensive to do Art books are expensive to produce.
  - expensive for somebody The new regulations are likely to be very expensive for employers.
  - expensive for somebody to do Bonds can be expensive for individuals to invest in.
  - it is expensive to do something It's expensive to live in London.
  - it is expensive for somebody to do something It is now more expensive for Europeans to visit here.

### [explain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/explain) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪkˈspleɪn/` · **US:** `/ɪkˈspleɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/exp/expla/explain__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/exp/expla/explain__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - explain (something) He was trying to explain the difference between hip hop and rap.
  - First, I'll explain the rules of the game.
  - The theory is not explained in detail.
  - ‘Let me explain!’ he added helpfully.
  - explain (something) to somebody It was difficult to explain the concept to beginners.
  - explain that… I explained that an ambulance would be coming soon.
  - explain who, how, etc… He explained who each person in the photo was.
  - Can you explain how this machine works?
  - explain to somebody who, how, etc… She explained to the children exactly what to do in an emergency.
  - + speech ‘It works like this,’ she explained.
  - it is explained that… It was explained that attendance was compulsory.

### [extra](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/extra_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈekstrə/` · **US:** `/ˈekstrə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/ext/extra/extra__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/ext/extra/extra__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - extra money/cash/funding
  - Breakfast is provided at no extra charge.
  - The rate for a room is £60, but breakfast is extra.
  - plans to offer increased broadband speeds at no extra cost
  - Employees are expected to put in extra hours without pay.
  - The DVD comes with lots of extra features.
  - extra help for single parents
  - a diet that can help you lose that extra weight
  - The conference is going to be a lot of extra work.
  - The government has promised an extra £1 billion for healthcare.
  - Take extra care on the roads this evening.

### [eye](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/eye_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/aɪ/` · **US:** `/aɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/e/eye/eye__/eye__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/e/eye/eye__/eye__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The suspect has dark hair and green eyes.
  - to close/open your eyes
  - to lower your eyes (= to look down)
  - She rolled her eyes in disgust.
  - Keep your eyes shut!
  - in somebody's eyes/eye There were tears in his eyes.
  - I have something in my eye.
  - She had a haunted look in her dark eyes.
  - His eyes filled with tears.
  - The ending was so sad it brought tears to my eyes.
  - I could see a pair of blue eyes peering out at me.
  - Her eyes widened in disbelief.
  - His eyes narrowed suspiciously.
  - Their eyes lit up when they saw all the presents.
  - to make/avoid eye contact with somebody (= to look/avoid looking at them at the same time as they look at you)
  - All eyes were on him (= everyone was looking at him) as he walked on to the stage.
  - a feast/treat/delight for the eyes


## F

### [face](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/face_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/feɪs/` · **US:** `/feɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fac/face_/face__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fac/face_/face__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pretty/pale/round face
  - He buried his face in his hands.
  - to hide/cover your face
  - on somebody's face You should have seen the look on her face when I told her!
  - The expression on his face never changed.
  - in the face She was red in the face with embarrassment.
  - At that time, her face was on the covers of all the magazines.

### [fact](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fact) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fækt/` · **US:** `/fækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fac/fact_/fact__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fac/fact_/fact__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - fact about something First, some basic facts about healthy eating.
  - fact (that)… Isn't it a fact that the firm is losing money?
  - It's a well-known fact that dogs have an acute sense of smell.
  - I know for a fact (= I am certain) that she's involved in something illegal.
  - The report is based on hard facts (= information that can be proved to be true).
  - Get your facts right (= make sure your information is correct) before you start making accusations.
  - It's about time you learnt to face (the) facts (= accepted the truth about the situation).
  - (informal) We didn't get on well, and that's a fact.
  - The judge instructed both lawyers to stick to the facts of the case.

### [fall](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fall_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːl/` · **US:** `/fɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fal/fall_/fall__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fal/fall_/fall__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The rain was falling steadily.
  - They were injured by falling rocks.
  - fall + adv./prep. Several of the books had fallen onto the floor.
  - The label must have fallen off.
  - The leaves were falling from the trees.
  - The seeds fall to the ground and germinate.
  - One of the kids fell into the river.
  - + noun He fell 20 metres onto the rocks below.

### [false](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/false) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːls/` · **US:** `/fɔːls/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fal/false/false__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fal/false/false__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A whale is a fish. True or false?
  - Predictions of an early improvement in the housing market proved false.
  - She gave false information to the insurance company.
  - He used a false name to get the job.
  - His career was ruined by false accusations.
  - The allegations are completely false.
  - This claim is simply false.
  - The law can punish knowingly false statements.

### [family](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/family_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfæməli/` · **US:** `/ˈfæməli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fam/famil/family__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fam/famil/family__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - family life
  - The job was starting to affect his family life.
  - your family background
  - Only close family members called her ‘Sally’.
  - a family friend (= a friend of the whole family)
  - family ties/connections
  - It's a family tradition.
  - She has become interested in her family history.
  - He has a family history of heart disease.

### [family](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/family_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfæməli/` · **US:** `/ˈfæməli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fam/famil/family__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fam/famil/family__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I introduced Neil to the other members of my family.
  - The event promises games and fun for the entire family.
  - This is a summer movie for the whole family.
  - Every family has its own story to tell.
  - All my family enjoy skiing.
  - one-parent/single-parent families
  - a family of four/five, etc Taking a family of four to the cinema is expensive.
  - families with young children
  - He's a friend of the family (= he is known and liked by the parents and the children).
  - From early childhood he was a mystery to the rest of his family.
  - He barely earns enough money to pay his rent and feed his family.
  - She works tirelessly to provide for her family.

### [famous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/famous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfeɪməs/` · **US:** `/ˈfeɪməs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fam/famou/famous__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fam/famou/famous__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a famous artist/actor
  - The actor was one of many famous faces at the party.
  - Loch Ness is probably the most famous lake in Scotland.
  - Tiger Woods is one of the most famous names in golf.
  - Many rich and famous people have stayed at the hotel.
  - So this is the famous dress! (= the one we have heard a lot about but have not seen).
  - The song was made famous by Frank Sinatra.
  - This was the book that made her famous.
  - famous for something He became internationally famous for his novels.
  - famous as something She was more famous as a writer than as a singer.

### [fantastic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fantastic) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fænˈtæstɪk/` · **US:** `/fænˈtæstɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fan/fanta/fantastic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fan/fanta/fantastic__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's done a fantastic job.
  - This was a fantastic opportunity for students.
  - You look fantastic!
  - It was a fantastic achievement.
  - The weather was absolutely fantastic.
  - You've got the job? Fantastic!
  - it is fantastic to do something It was fantastic to see so many families enjoying themselves.

### [far](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/far_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/fɑː(r)/` · **US:** `/fɑːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/far/far__/far__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/far/far__/far__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We didn't go far.
  - Have you come far?
  - It's not far to the beach.
  - There's not far to go now.
  - It's too far to walk.
  - + adv./prep. The restaurant is not far from here.
  - countries as far apart as Japan and Brazil
  - He looked down at the traffic far below.
  - Far away in the distance, a train whistled.
  - The farther north they went, the colder it became.
  - a concert of music from near and far
  - (figurative) Legends are usually far removed from the reality.

### [farm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/farm_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɑːm/` · **US:** `/fɑːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/far/farm_/farm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/far/farm_/farm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 200-hectare farm
  - a dairy farm
  - an organic farm
  - a pig/sheep/poultry farm
  - He runs the farm on his own.
  - on a farm to live on a farm
  - She works on the family farm.
  - at a farm At harvest time they helped out at the farm.
  - a farm worker/labourer
  - farm animals
  - farm buildings/machinery
  - farm income/subsidies
  - (North American English) a farm family

### [farmer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/farmer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɑːmə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːrmər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/far/farme/farmer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/far/farme/farmer__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My parents are dairy farmers.
  - The land is owned by a local farmer.
  - A lot of conventional farmers have converted to organic.
  - a new plan to help smallholder farmers
  - Settlers were primarily subsistence farmers.

### [fast](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fast_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fɑːst/` · **US:** `/fæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fas/fast_/fast__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fas/fast_/fast__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fast car/horse
  - He's just become the world's fastest runner.

### [fast](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fast_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/fɑːst/` · **US:** `/fæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fas/fast_/fast__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fas/fast_/fast__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't drive so fast!
  - How fast were you going?
  - I can't go any faster.
  - The water was rising fast.
  - Her heart beat faster.
  - (formal) Night was fast approaching.
  - He came round the corner blindingly fast.
  - By a few easy steps you can make your PC run faster.

### [fat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fat_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fæt/` · **US:** `/fæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fat/fat__/fat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fat/fat__/fat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a big fat man/woman
  - You'll get fat if you eat so much chocolate.
  - He grew fatter and fatter.
  - I was ashamed of my fat flabby legs.

### [father](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/father_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɑːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fat/fathe/father__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fat/fathe/father__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My father died in 2017.
  - Ben's a wonderful father.
  - Our new boss is a father of three (= he has three children).
  - father to somebody You've been like a father to me.
  - He was a wonderful father to both his natural and adopted children.
  - I never met my biological father.
  - (old-fashioned) Father, I cannot lie to you.

### [favourite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/favourite_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfeɪvərɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈfeɪvərɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fav/favou/favourite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fav/favou/favourite__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's one of my favourite movies.
  - What is your all-time favourite song?
  - Who is your favourite writer?
  - January is my least favourite month.

### [favourite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/favourite_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfeɪvərɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈfeɪvərɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fav/favou/favourite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fav/favou/favourite__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which one's your favourite?
  - The band played all my old favourites.
  - One of my personal favourites is a movie that should please every age group.
  - favourite of somebody's This song is a particular favourite of mine.
  - favourite with somebody The programme has become a firm favourite with young people.
  - These biscuits are great favourites with the children.

### [February](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/february) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfebruəri/` · **US:** `/ˈfebrueri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/feb/febru/february__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/feb/febru/february__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was born in February.
  - (British English) The meeting is on the fifth of February/February the fifth.
  - (North American English) The meeting is on February fifth.
  - We went to Japan last February.
  - I arrived at the end of February.
  - last February's election
  - It was a cold February morning.

### [feel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/feel_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fiːl/` · **US:** `/fiːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fee/feel_/feel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fee/feel_/feel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adj. The bus ride made me feel sick.
  - Are you feeling comfortable?
  - She sounded more confident than she felt.
  - I was feeling guilty.
  - You'll feel better after a good night's sleep.
  - I feel sorry for him.
  - I feel bad about leaving you like this.
  - She felt betrayed.
  - + adv./prep. How are you feeling today?
  - I know exactly how you feel (= I feel sympathy for you).
  - We all felt the same way.
  - Luckily I was feeling in a good mood.
  - feel something I felt the need to explain.
  - I felt a sense of relief.
  - feel like something I felt like a complete idiot.
  - She made me feel like a child by doing everything for me.
  - feel as if/though… I feel as if nobody cares.
  - She felt as though she was going to cry.
  - feel + noun to feel an idiot/a fool

### [feeling](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/feeling) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfiːlɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈfiːlɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fee/feeli/feeling__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fee/feeli/feeling__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a feeling of something a feeling of guilt/helplessness/anger/sadness
  - He struggled with feelings of isolation and loneliness.
  - You might experience feelings of dizziness and nausea.
  - a strange/horrible feeling
  - You need to stop having these guilty feelings.
  - I've got a tight feeling in my stomach.
  - (informal) ‘I really resent the way he treated me.’ ‘I know the feeling (= I know how you feel).’
  - ‘I'm going to miss you.’ ‘The feeling's mutual (= I feel exactly the same).’

### [festival](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/festival) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfestɪvl/` · **US:** `/ˈfestɪvl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fes/festi/festival__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fes/festi/festival__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a film/music festival
  - the Edinburgh Festival
  - the Cannes Film Festival
  - The pub is holding its annual beer festival later this week.
  - a rock festival (= where bands perform, often outdoors and over a period of several days)

### [few](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/few_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fjuː/` · **US:** `/fjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/few/few__/few__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/few/few__/few__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We've had a few replies.
  - I need a few things from the store.
  - Quite a few people are going to arrive early.
  - I try to visit my parents every few weeks.

### [few](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/few_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/fjuː/` · **US:** `/fjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/few/few__/few__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/few/few__/few__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We've had a few replies.
  - I need a few things from the store.
  - Quite a few people are going to arrive early.
  - I try to visit my parents every few weeks.

### [few](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/few_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/fjuː/` · **US:** `/fjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/few/few__/few__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/few/few__/few__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I recognized a few of the other people.
  - I've seen most of his movies. Only a few are as good as his first one.
  - Could you give me a few more details?

### [fifteen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fifteen) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˌfɪfˈtiːn/` · **US:** `/ˌfɪfˈtiːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fif/fifte/fifteen__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fif/fifte/fifteen__us_3.mp3)

### [fifth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fifth_1) *(ordinal number)*
- **UK:** `/fɪfθ/` · **US:** `/fɪfθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fif/fifth/fifth__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fif/fifth/fifth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Today is the fifth (of May).
  - the fifth century BC
  - It's her fifth birthday.
  - My office is on the fifth floor.
  - It's the fifth time that I've been to America.
  - Her mother had just given birth to another child, her fifth.
  - the world’s fifth-largest oil exporter
  - He finished fifth in the race.
  - Edward V (= Edward the Fifth)

### [fifty](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fifty) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪfti/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪfti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fif/fifty/fifty__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fif/fifty/fifty__us_2.mp3)

### [fill](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fill_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɪl/` · **US:** `/fɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fil/fill_/fill__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fil/fill_/fill__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - fill something Please fill this glass for me.
  - to fill a space/vacuum/void
  - Smoke filled the room.
  - Her image filled the screen.
  - The wind filled the sails.
  - A Disney film can always fill cinemas (= attract a lot of people to see it).
  - The school is filled to capacity.
  - fill something with something She filled the page with writing.
  - bags filled with household waste
  - Carl took a mug and filled it to the brim with hot coffee.
  - fill something + adj. Fill a pan half full of water.
  - You've filled it too full.
  - The room was filling quickly.
  - fill with something Her eyes suddenly filled with tears.
  - The room filled with smoke.
  - The sails filled with wind.

### [film](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/film_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɪlm/` · **US:** `/fɪlm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fil/film_/film__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fil/film_/film__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let's stay in and watch a film.
  - We're going out to see a film.
  - There's a good film on tonight (= showing at the cinema or on TV).
  - a horror/documentary/feature film
  - an action/animated film
  - a silent film (= one recorded without sound)
  - a film about something As a student he made a short film about his home town.
  - to shoot/direct/produce a film
  - The film was shot on location in France.
  - The film has been downloaded some 21 million times since its release.
  - a film crew/critic/director/producer
  - an international film festival
  - a film version/adaptation of a novel by Henry James
  - His film credits (= the films he has made) as director include ‘Mood Music’ and ‘Lies’.
  - He's a great composer of film music.

### [final](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/final_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfaɪnl/` · **US:** `/ˈfaɪnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/final/final__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/final/final__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - his final act as party leader
  - Jamie is in his final year at Stirling University.
  - the final week/day/minutes of something
  - The referee blew the final whistle.
  - The project is in its final stages.
  - The final round of voting takes place on Sunday.
  - They find each other in the final chapter of the book.
  - I'd like to return to the final point you made.

### [find](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/find_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/faɪnd/` · **US:** `/faɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/find_/find__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/find_/find__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - find somebody/something Look what I've found!
  - We've found a great new restaurant near the office.
  - A man out walking his dog found the body in a ditch.
  - find somebody/something + adj. A whale was found washed up on the shore.
  - They found a car abandoned on top of the cliff.

### [fine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fine_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/faɪn/` · **US:** `/faɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/fine_/fine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/fine_/fine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘How are you?’ ‘Fine, thanks.’
  - I was feeling fine when I got up this morning.
  - ‘How’s your throat?’ ‘It’s fine as long as I don’t cough.’

### [finish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/finish_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪnɪʃ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪnɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/finis/finish__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/finis/finish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - finish (something) Haven't you finished your homework yet?
  - She finished law school last year.
  - You only get points if you finish the race.
  - I thought you'd never finish!
  - We've just finished the project.
  - finish what… Let me just finish what I'm doing.
  - finish doing something Be quiet! He hasn't finished speaking.
  - finish by doing something He finished by telling us about his trip to Spain.
  - + speech ‘And that was all,’ she finished.

### [fire](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fire_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfaɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfaɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fir/fire_/fire__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fir/fire_/fire__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most animals are afraid of fire.
  - When did people start to cook with fire?

### [first](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/first_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/fɜːst/` · **US:** `/fɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fir/first/first__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fir/first/first__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Do you want a drink?’ ‘I'll finish my work first.’
  - First I had to decide what to wear.
  - Who came first in the race (= who won)?
  - It plunged nose first into the river.
  - After the war, Erica went on to marry first one American millionaire and then another.

### [first](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/first_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/fɜːst/` · **US:** `/fɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fir/first/first__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fir/first/first__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - her first husband
  - the first turning on the right
  - I didn't take the first bus.
  - It was the first time they had ever met.
  - It's always exciting to visit a country for the first time.
  - students in their first year at college
  - First impressions can be misleading.
  - She resolved to do it at the first (= earliest) opportunity.
  - King Edward I (= pronounced ‘King Edward the First’)
  - the first of May/May 1st
  - His second book is better than his first.

### [first](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/first_1) *(ordinal number)*
- **UK:** `/fɜːst/` · **US:** `/fɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fir/first/first__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fir/first/first__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - her first husband
  - the first turning on the right
  - I didn't take the first bus.
  - It was the first time they had ever met.
  - It's always exciting to visit a country for the first time.
  - students in their first year at college
  - First impressions can be misleading.
  - She resolved to do it at the first (= earliest) opportunity.
  - King Edward I (= pronounced ‘King Edward the First’)
  - the first of May/May 1st
  - His second book is better than his first.

### [fish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fish_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɪʃ/` · **US:** `/fɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fis/fish_/fish__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fis/fish_/fish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They caught several fish.
  - In the pool she could see little silvery fish darting around.
  - tropical/marine/freshwater fish
  - Herring are relatively oily fish.
  - shoals (= groups) of fish
  - There are about 30 000 species of fish in the world.
  - The list of endangered species includes nearly 600 fishes.
  - a fish tank/pond
  - Fish stocks in the Baltic are in decline.
  - Fish farming has boomed in the past three decades.

### [five](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/five) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/faɪv/` · **US:** `/faɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fiv/five_/five__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fiv/five_/five__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are only five cookies left.
  - five of Sweden’s top financial experts
  - Ten people were invited but only five turned up.
  - Do you have change for five dollars?
  - a five-month contract
  - Look at page five.
  - Five and four is nine.
  - Three fives are fifteen.
  - I can't read your writing—is this meant to be a five?
  - The bulbs are planted in threes or fives (= groups of three or five).
  - We moved to America when I was five (= five years old).
  - Shall we meet at five (= at five o'clock), then?

### [flat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flat_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/flæt/` · **US:** `/flæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fla/flat_/flat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fla/flat_/flat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They're renting a furnished flat on the third floor.
  - to buy/sell a flat
  - in a flat Do you live in a flat or a house?
  - a basement/ground-floor/top-floor flat
  - a two-bedroom flat
  - The park was surrounded by high-rise flats and office blocks.
  - a new block of flats
  - Many large old houses have been converted into flats.
  - Children from the flats (= the block of flats) across the street were playing outside.

### [flight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flight_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/flaɪt/` · **US:** `/flaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fli/fligh/flight__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fli/fligh/flight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a smooth/comfortable/bumpy flight
  - a domestic/an international flight
  - a transatlantic flight
  - The first prize is a return flight to Delhi.
  - China's first manned space flight
  - a hot-air balloon flight
  - flight (from A) (to B) We met on a flight from London to Paris.
  - It is vital that people get up and walk about if they are on a long-haul flight.

### [floor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/floor_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/flɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/flɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/flo/floor/floor__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/flo/floor/floor__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a wooden/concrete/tiled floor
  - His glass fell to the floor and broke.
  - on the floor The body was lying on the kitchen floor.
  - ceramic floor tiles
  - The furniture and floor coverings date from the 1920s.
  - The alterations should give us extra floor space.
  - The study was stacked from floor to ceiling with books.

### [flower](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flower_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈflaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈflaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/flo/flowe/flower__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/flo/flowe/flower__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The plant has a beautiful bright red flower.
  - in flower The roses are in flower early this year.
  - The crocuses are late coming into flower.
  - a flower bud/petal

### [fly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fly_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/flaɪ/` · **US:** `/flaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fly/fly__/fly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fly/fly__/fly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Penguins can't fly.
  - + adv./prep. A stork flew slowly past.
  - A wasp had flown in through the window.
  - Geese fly south in the fall.

### [follow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/follow) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɒləʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fol/follo/follow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fol/follo/follow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - follow somebody/something Follow me please.
  - follow somebody + adv./prep. He followed her into the house.
  - They were followed down the street by a bunch of photographers.
  - Wherever she led, they followed.
  - Sam walked in, with the rest of the boys following closely behind.

### [food](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/food) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fuːd/` · **US:** `/fuːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/foo/food_/food__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/foo/food_/food__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Pat had prepared food and drink for the work party.
  - to grow/cook/serve/eat food
  - Do you like Italian food?
  - Demand for organic food has been growing fast.
  - Families that eat together consume healthier food.
  - the food industry
  - People worry about food safety (= if food is safe to eat).
  - steps to protect the nation's food supply
  - Global food shortages and crop failures would have a devastating effect.
  - baby food (= prepared especially for babies)
  - cat/dog/pet food (= for a cat/dog/pet to eat)
  - You should eat more fresh foods.
  - The store specializes in frozen foods.

### [foot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/foot_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fʊt/` · **US:** `/fʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/foo/foot_/foot__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/foo/foot_/foot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My feet are aching.
  - She kicked the ball with her right foot.
  - He's broken several bones in his left foot.
  - The whole audience rose to its feet (= stood up) and cheered.
  - to leap/jump/spring to your feet (= to stand up quickly and easily)
  - to scramble/struggle/stagger to your feet (= to stand up slowly and with difficulty)
  - on foot We came on foot (= we walked).
  - on your feet I've been on my feet (= standing or walking around) all day.
  - Come on lads—on your feet and do some work!
  - walking around the house in bare feet (= not wearing shoes or socks)
  - We were stamping our feet (= hitting them on the ground) to keep warm.
  - Please wipe your feet (= your shoes) on the mat.
  - The hot sand burned the soles of my feet.
  - He is currently resting a foot injury.
  - a foot pump (= operated using your foot, not your hand)
  - a foot passenger (= one who travels on a ferry without a car)

### [football](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/football) *(noun)*

  - We settled down to watch the football on TV.
  - On Saturday we went to a football match.
  - a Premier League football team
  - a football player
  - I'm not a big football fan.
  - This was the best game of football I have seen all season.

### [for](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/for_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/fɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/fɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/for__/for__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/for__/for__us_1_rr.mp3)

  - This is the place for me (= I like it very much).

### [forget](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/forget) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fəˈɡet/` · **US:** `/fərˈɡet/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/forge/forget__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/forge/forget__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Why weren't you at the meeting?’ ‘Sorry—I forgot.’
  - forget about something In the excitement I forgot all about my little brother.
  - forget to do something Take care, and don't forget to write.
  - He forgot to mention he was bringing a guest.
  - I forgot to ask him for his address.
  - forget something/somebody I forgot my purse (= I did not remember to bring it).
  - ‘Hey, don't forget me!’ (= don't leave without me)
  - Aren't you forgetting something? (= I think you have forgotten to do something)

### [form](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/form_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːm/` · **US:** `/fɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/form_/form__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/form_/form__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - all the millions of different life forms on the planet today
  - form of something forms of transport/government/entertainment
  - one of the most common forms of cancer
  - Swimming is one of the best forms of exercise.
  - Along with talking and singing, drawing must be the oldest form of communication.

### [form](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/form_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɔːm/` · **US:** `/fɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/form_/form__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/form_/form__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - form something Bend the wire so that it forms a ‘V’.
  - Rearrange the letters to form a new word.
  - Games can help children learn to form letters.
  - Do you know how to form the past tense?
  - form something into something Form the dough into balls with your hands.
  - be formed from something The chain is formed from 136 links.
  - be formed of something The table was formed of two large slabs of stone.

- **UK:** `/friː/` · **US:** `/friː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fre/free_/free__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fre/free_/free__us_1.mp3)

### [Friday](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/friday) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfraɪdi/` · **US:** `/ˈfraɪdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fri/frida/friday_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fri/frida/friday__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's Friday today, isn't it?
  - She went to dance class last Friday.
  - Are you busy next Friday?
  - Friday morning/afternoon/evening
  - We'll discuss this at Friday's meeting.
  - I work Friday to Tuesday.
  - I work Fridays to Tuesdays.
  - on Friday We'll meet on Friday (= next Friday).
  - We met on Friday (= last Friday).
  - (informal or North American English) We'll meet Friday.
  - on Fridays The museum is closed on Fridays (= every Friday).
  - (especially North American English) The museum is closed Fridays.
  - on a Friday He was born on a Friday.
  - I always do yoga on a Friday (= every Friday).
  - I went to Nairobi on Monday, and came back the following Friday.
  - (British English) ‘When did the accident happen?’ ‘It was the Friday (= the Friday of the week we are talking about).’
  - (British English) Come back Friday week (= a week after next Friday).

  - a close/good/dear friend
  - a childhood/family/longtime friend
  - We've been friends for years.
  - They had a quarrel, but they're friends again now.
  - to become/remain friends
  - as a friend I'm asking you as a friend not to tell anyone.
  - She wanted to share the news with family and friends.
  - She has a wide circle of friends.
  - I heard about it through a friend of a friend.

- **UK:** `/fʊl/` · **US:** `/fʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/ful/full_/full__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/ful/full_/full__us_1.mp3)

  - a full bottle of wine
  - She could only nod, because her mouth was full.
  - The theatre was less than half full.
  - full of something My suitcase was full of books.
  - There were cardboard boxes stuffed full of clothes.
  - full up (British English) Sorry, the hotel is full up tonight.

### [fun](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fun_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fʌn/` · **US:** `/fʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fun/fun__/fun__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fun/fun__/fun__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We had a lot of fun at Sarah's party.
  - Sailing is good fun.
  - It was great fun! You should have come too.
  - That was the most fun I have had in years.
  - Have fun! (= Enjoy yourself)
  - ‘What fun!’ she said with a laugh.
  - We won't let a bit of rain spoil our fun.
  - for fun I decided to learn Spanish, just for fun.
  - I didn't do all that work just for the fun of it.
  - it is fun doing something It's not much fun going to a party on your own.
  - It's no fun getting up at 4  a.m. on a cold, rainy morning.
  - Walking three miles in the pouring rain is not my idea of fun.
  - The whole family can join in the fun at Water World.
  - ‘What do you say to a weekend in New York?’ ‘Sounds like fun.’

### [funny](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/funny) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfʌni/` · **US:** `/ˈfʌni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fun/funny/funny__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fun/funny/funny__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a funny story/joke
  - He was a very funny guy as well as a kind person.
  - That's the funniest thing I've ever heard.
  - The movie is full of funny moments.
  - I found the play funny and moving.
  - It's not funny! Someone could have been hurt.
  - I was really embarrassed, but then I saw the funny side of it.
  - (ironic) Oh very funny! You expect me to believe that?
  - ‘What's so funny?’ she demanded.

### [future](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/future_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfjuːtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfjuːtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fut/futur/future__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fut/futur/future__us_2.mp3)

  - We need to plan for the future.
  - in the future The movie is set in the future.
  - at some point/time in the future
  - I don't expect any of these things to happen in the near future (= soon).
  - Things will continue as they are for the foreseeable future.
  - the immediate/not-too-distant/long-term/distant future
  - Nobody can predict the future.
  - What does the future hold?
  - of the future What will the cities of the future look like?
  - Kevin talked about his hopes for the future.

### [glass](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/glass_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɡlɑːs/` · **US:** `/ɡlæs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gla/glass/glass__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gla/glass/glass__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sheet/pane of glass
  - I cut myself on a piece of broken glass.
  - frosted/toughened glass
  - a glass bottle/jar
  - sliding glass doors
  - a glass roof/dome/panel
  - under glass The vegetables are grown under glass (= in a greenhouse).

### [go](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/go_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɡəʊ/` · **US:** `/ɡəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/go_/go__g/go__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/go_/go__u/go__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adv./prep. She went into her room and shut the door behind her.
  - I have to go to Rome on business.
  - She has gone to China (= is now in China or is on her way there).
  - She has been to China (= she went to China and has now returned).
  - Are you going home for Christmas?
  - go to do something She has gone to see her sister this weekend.

### [good](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/good_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɡʊd/` · **US:** `/ɡʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/goo/good_/good__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/goo/good_/good__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a good book
  - good food
  - The piano was in really good condition.
  - Your work is just not good enough.
  - The results were pretty good.
  - Sorry, my English is not very good.
  - They've done a reasonably good job.
  - Keep up the good work!
  - It's a good way to make friends.
  - The sound and picture are both of good quality.
  - Things are looking good at the moment.
  - You'll never marry her—she's much too good for you.

### [goodbye](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/goodbye) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɡʊdˈbaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˌɡʊdˈbaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/goo/goodb/goodbye__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/goo/goodb/goodbye__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Goodbye! It was great to meet you.
  - She didn't even say goodbye to her mother.
  - Say goodbye to Mary for me.
  - We waved them goodbye.
  - We've already said our goodbyes.
  - Kiss me goodbye!
  - (figurative) Take out our service contract and say goodbye to costly repair bills.

### [goodbye](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/goodbye) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɡʊdˈbaɪ/` · **US:** `/ˌɡʊdˈbaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/goo/goodb/goodbye__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/goo/goodb/goodbye__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Goodbye! It was great to meet you.
  - She didn't even say goodbye to her mother.
  - Say goodbye to Mary for me.
  - We waved them goodbye.
  - We've already said our goodbyes.
  - Kiss me goodbye!
  - (figurative) Take out our service contract and say goodbye to costly repair bills.

### [grandfather](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/grandfather) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɡrænfɑːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɡrænfɑːðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gra/grand/grandfather__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gra/grand/grandfather__us_1.mp3)
