  - a large group/city/area/crowd/family
  - a large number of people
  - There were some very large amounts of money involved.
  - Drought hit large parts of the country.
  - A large proportion of old people live alone.
  - The company has grown large enough to employ over 100 people.
  - The kitchen is relatively large for a modern apartment.
  - That's too large to fit in my suitcase!
  - The population is slightly larger than that of Canada.
  - Women usually do the larger share of the housework.
  - Seoul is one of the world's largest cities.
  - Who's the rather large (= fat) lady in the hat?

### [last](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/last1_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/lɑːst/` · **US:** `/læst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/las/last_/last__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/las/last_/last__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We caught the last bus home.
  - It's the last house on the left.
  - She was last to arrive.

### [late](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/late_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/leɪt/` · **US:** `/leɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/late_/late__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/late_/late__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm sorry I'm late.
  - late for something She's late for work every day.
  - My flight was an hour late.
  - We apologize for the late arrival of this train.
  - Interest will be charged for late payment.
  - Because of the cold weather the crops are later this year.
  - Here is a late news flash.
  - Some children are very late developers.

### [late](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/late_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/leɪt/` · **US:** `/leɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/late_/late__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/late_/late__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I got up late.
  - Can I stay up late tonight?
  - She has to work late tomorrow.
  - The Friday afternoon meeting started late, as usual.
  - The birthday card arrived three days late.
  - late for something I'm running late for school.

### [later](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/later_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/later/later__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/later/later__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - See you later.
  - I met her again three years later.
  - His father died later that year.
  - We're going to Rome later in the year.
  - She later became a doctor.
  - I discovered later that she'd known all about it from the beginning.

### [laugh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/laugh_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɑːf/` · **US:** `/læf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lau/laugh/laugh__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lau/laugh/laugh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to give a laugh
  - a short/nervous/hearty laugh
  - His first joke got the biggest laugh of the night.
  - with a laugh ‘It was pretty crazy,’ she said with a laugh.

### [laugh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/laugh_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɑːf/` · **US:** `/læf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lau/laugh/laugh__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lau/laugh/laugh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to laugh out loud
  - to laugh loudly/aloud/heartily
  - When I heard her excuse, I just laughed.
  - I was laughing so hard that I had tears running down my cheeks.
  - The show was hilarious—I couldn't stop laughing.
  - laugh at something You never laugh at my jokes!
  - laugh about something She was laughing hysterically about something he had said.
  - She always makes me laugh.
  - He burst out laughing (= suddenly started laughing).
  - She laughed to cover her nervousness.
  - I told him I was worried but he laughed scornfully.
  - She laughed and said, ‘You silly man!’
  - + speech ‘You're crazy!’ she laughed.

### [learn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/learn) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɜːn/` · **US:** `/lɜːrn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/learn/learn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/learn/learn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - learn something to learn a language/skill/trade
  - He had the opportunity to learn English in Australia.
  - learn (something) from somebody/something I learned a lot from my father.
  - Everyone in the class had the opportunity to learn from each other.
  - learn (something) from doing something You can learn a great deal just from watching other players.
  - learn (about something) She's very keen to learn about Japanese culture.
  - The book is about how children learn.
  - She received no training but quickly learnt on the job (= while doing the job).
  - learn to do something He's learning to play the trumpet.
  - Most people learn to read as children.
  - learn how to do something Today we learnt how to use the new software.
  - learn what, where, etc… Students need to learn what to do in an emergency.

### [leave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leave_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/liːv/` · **US:** `/liːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/leave/leave__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/leave/leave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Come on, it's time we left (= time for us to leave).
  - She left without saying goodbye.
  - leave for something The plane leaves for Dallas at 12.35.
  - What time do you have to leave for work?
  - leave something The plane leaves Heathrow at 12.35.
  - I hate leaving home.
  - She left the house around 8 this morning.
  - The whole family was forced to leave the country.

### [left](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/left_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/left/` · **US:** `/left/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lef/left_/left__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lef/left_/left__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Fewer people write with their left hand than with their right.
  - your left knee/leg/foot/arm
  - the left side of the field
  - The university is on the left bank of the river.
  - Take a left turn at the intersection.
  - (sport) a left back/wing
  - a left hook (= a short hard hit with your closed left hand)

### [left](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/left_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/left/` · **US:** `/left/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lef/left_/left__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lef/left_/left__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Turn left at the intersection.
  - Look left and right before you cross the road.
  - There is more to Paris than just the Eiffel Tower (pictured left).

### [left](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/left_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/left/` · **US:** `/left/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lef/left_/left__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lef/left_/left__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - on the left Take the next road on the left.
  - on the left of somebody/something My dad's in the front row, on the extreme left of the picture.
  - to the left Twist your body to the left, then to the right.
  - to the left of somebody/something To the left of the library is the bank.
  - on somebody's left She was sitting on my left.
  - to somebody's left To your left, you can see the ruins of the old manor house.
  - from the left the figures in the second column from the left
  - Arabic script is read from right to left.
  - the top/bottom/far left
  - first/second/third, etc. left She is pictured third left (= in the third position from the left) with her students.

### [leg](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leg_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/leɡ/` · **US:** `/leɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/leg/leg__/leg__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/leg/leg__/leg__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Sit on the floor, stretching your legs out in front of you.
  - your left/right leg
  - I broke my leg playing football.
  - She has a broken leg.
  - He lost a leg in a motorcycle accident.
  - She had to have her leg amputated.
  - How many legs does a centipede have?
  - The bear rose up on its hind legs.
  - a wooden leg
  - between somebody's legs He dribbled the ball between the defender's legs.
  - on somebody's leg I had a big bruise on my leg.
  - a leg injury

### [lesson](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lesson) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlesn/` · **US:** `/ˈlesn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/les/lesso/lesson__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/les/lesso/lesson__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She gives piano lessons.
  - I’m having driving lessons.
  - lesson in something He took lessons in Thai cookery.
  - lesson on something a history lesson on the Roman Empire
  - lesson with somebody Beethoven's lessons with Haydn continued until 1794.
  - lesson from somebody His parents paid for lessons from local dance teachers.
  - lesson for somebody Lessons for beginners can be arranged.
  - (especially British English) Our first lesson on Tuesdays is French.
  - (especially British English) What did we do last lesson?

### [let](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/let_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/let/` · **US:** `/let/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/let/let__/let__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/let/let__/let__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Shall we check it again?’ ‘Yes, let's.’
  - let's do something Let's go to the beach.
  - Right, let's begin.
  - (formal) Let us review these points in more detail.
  - Let's not tell her what we did.
  - (British English) Don't let's tell her what we did.
  - Now let's not be silly about this.

### [letter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/letter_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈletə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈletər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/let/lette/letter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/let/lette/letter__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to write/send (somebody) a letter
  - to receive/get a letter
  - (British English) to post a letter
  - (North American English) to mail a letter
  - a thank-you/resignation/rejection letter
  - letter to somebody In a letter to members, the Chairman explained the changes.
  - I wrote a letter to the editor of our local paper.
  - letter from somebody You may need a letter from your doctor.
  - a letter of something a letter of recommendation/complaint/apology
  - by letter You will be notified by letter.

### [library](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/library) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlaɪbri/` · **US:** `/ˈlaɪbreri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lib/libra/library__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lib/libra/library__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a public/university/school library
  - a library book
  - a toy library (= for borrowing toys from)
  - How often do you go to the library?
  - You can borrow it from your local library.
  - I got this very interesting book out of the library.
  - a plan to provide mobile library services in rural environments

### [lie](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lie1_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/laɪ/` · **US:** `/laɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lie/lie__/lie__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lie/lie__/lie__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lie + adv./prep. to lie on your back/side/front
  - She lay in bed listening to music.
  - Lie there for a bit until you feel better.
  - lie + adj. I lay awake all night worrying about it.
  - The cat was lying fast asleep by the fire.

### [life](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/life_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/laɪf/` · **US:** `/laɪf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lif/life_/life__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lif/life_/life__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This could mean the difference between life and death.
  - The body was cold and showed no signs of life.
  - I wish we could bring dinosaurs back to life.
  - In spring the countryside bursts into life.

### [light](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/light_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/laɪt/` · **US:** `/laɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lig/light/light__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lig/light/light__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's got light blue eyes.
  - Lighter shades suit you best.
  - People with pale complexions should avoid wearing light colours.
  - a box of light and dark chocolates

### [light](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/light_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/laɪt/` · **US:** `/laɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lig/light/light__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lig/light/light__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - bright/dim light
  - a room with good natural light
  - White paint reflects the light.
  - The light was beginning to fail (= it was beginning to get dark).
  - The knife gleamed as it caught the light (= as the light shone on it).
  - The substance causes the bacteria to emit light.
  - Some light filters through the dense tree cover.
  - by the light of something She could just see by the light of the candle.
  - in the fading light of a summer’s evening
  - into the light Bring it into the light so I can see it.
  - towards the light The plants grow towards the light.
  - travelling at the speed of light
  - a beam/ray of light
  - a light source/beam
  - changes in light intensity

### [like](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/like_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/laɪk/` · **US:** `/laɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lik/like_/like__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lik/like_/like__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's wearing a dress like mine.
  - He's very like his father.
  - She looks nothing like (= not at all like) her mother.
  - That sounds like (= I think I can hear) him coming now.
  - It tastes rather like chicken.

### [like](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/like_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/laɪk/` · **US:** `/laɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lik/like_/like__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lik/like_/like__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - like somebody/something She's nice. I like her.
  - I really like him.
  - Do you like their new house?
  - Which tie do you like best?
  - How did you like Japan (= did you find it pleasant)?
  - She's never liked swimming.
  - I don't like the way he's looking at me.
  - She liked the idea of being her own boss.
  - The company didn't like the fact that I'd taken maternity leave.
  - You've got to go to school, whether you like it or not.
  - like doing something We like having people to stay.
  - like somebody/something doing something I didn't like him taking all the credit.
  - (formal) I didn't like his taking all the credit.
  - like to do something I like to see them enjoying themselves.
  - like it when… I like it when you do that.

### [line](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/line_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/laɪn/` · **US:** `/laɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lin/line_/line__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lin/line_/line__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a straight/solid/dotted/dashed line
  - a wavy/diagonal line
  - a vertical/horizontal line
  - parallel lines
  - Draw a thick black line across the page.

### [lion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlaɪən/` · **US:** `/ˈlaɪən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lio/lion_/lion__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lio/lion_/lion__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The lion shook its mane and roared.
  - a pride of lions (= a group of them)
  - Over the past two months, 13 lions have been killed by poachers.
  - He had once been chased and almost eaten by a lion.
  - Young Masai men gather for traditional lion hunts.

### [list](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/list_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɪst/` · **US:** `/lɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lis/list_/list__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lis/list_/list__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The guest list includes numerous celebrities.
  - on the list Is your name on the list?
  - I'll add you to the email list.
  - Her novel shot to the top of the bestseller list.
  - He plays drums, guitar, piano, flute, the list goes on.
  - list of something a list of names/candidates/questions
  - I made a list of things to do.
  - They compiled a list of all the items they'd need.
  - to draw up a list
  - Going to the bank tops my list of priorities today.
  - Having to wait hours came high on the list of complaints.

### [list](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/list_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɪst/` · **US:** `/lɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lis/list_/list__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lis/list_/list__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We were asked to list our ten favourite songs.
  - The key items are listed below.
  - Towns in the guide are listed alphabetically.

### [listen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/listen_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪsn/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪsn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lis/liste/listen__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lis/liste/listen__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Listen! What's that noise? Can you hear it?
  - Sorry, I wasn't really listening.
  - He had been listening at the door.
  - listen to somebody/something to listen to music/the radio
  - to listen to a song/an album
  - I listened carefully to her story.
  - He was listening intently to what she was saying.
  - You haven’t been listening to a word I’ve said!

### [little](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/little_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lit/littl/little__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lit/littl/little__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a little house
  - a little group of tourists
  - a little old lady
  - We passed through several nice little towns.
  - It was a tiny little room.
  - ‘Which do you want?’ ‘I'll take the little one.’
  - She gave a little laugh.
  - (British English) We should manage, with a little bit of luck.
  - Here's a little something (= a small present) for your birthday.

### [little](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/little_2) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lit/littl/little__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lit/littl/little__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a little milk/sugar/tea
  - If you have any spare milk, could you give me a little?
  - I've only read a little of the book so far.
  - (formal) It caused not a little/no little (= a lot of) confusion.
  - After a little (= a short time) he got up and left.

### [little](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/little_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lit/littl/little__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lit/littl/little__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a little milk/sugar/tea
  - If you have any spare milk, could you give me a little?
  - I've only read a little of the book so far.
  - (formal) It caused not a little/no little (= a lot of) confusion.
  - After a little (= a short time) he got up and left.

### [live](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/live1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɪv/` · **US:** `/lɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/liv/live_/live__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/liv/live_/live__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to live in a house/a flat/an apartment
  - We used to live in London.
  - people living in rural areas
  - We live in a world where technological advance is the norm.
  - Where do you live?
  - She needs to find somewhere to live.
  - It's a great place to live.
  - Both her children still live at home.
  - He lived and worked there until his death in 2015.
  - (informal) Where do these plates live (= where are they usually kept)?

### [local](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/local_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈləʊkl/` · **US:** `/ˈləʊkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/local/local__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/local/local__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - local people/residents/businesses
  - members of the local community
  - the local police
  - local elections
  - A local man was accused of the murder.
  - Our children go to the local school.
  - a local newspaper (= one that gives local news)
  - decisions made at the local level
  - It was difficult to understand the local dialect.

### [long](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/long_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/lɒŋ/` · **US:** `/lɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lon/long_/long__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lon/long_/long__us_8.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She had long dark hair.
  - He walked down the long corridor.
  - It was the world's longest bridge.
  - a long journey/walk/drive/flight
  - We're a long way from anywhere here.
  - It's a long way away.
  - He had long, thin fingers.
  - a lush meadow of long grass

### [long](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/long_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/lɒŋ/` · **US:** `/lɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lon/long_/long__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lon/long_/long__us_8.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Have you been here long?
  - Stay as long as you like.
  - The party went on long into the night.
  - You shouldn't have to wait too long.
  - Now we're living longer and retiring later.
  - This may take longer than we thought.
  - Shows don't usually last this long.
  - I won't be long (= I'll return, be ready, etc. soon).
  - How long have you been waiting?
  - Those carefree college years are long gone.
  - These reforms are long overdue.
  - Sophie needs to think long and hard about where she's going next.

### [look](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/look_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lʊk/` · **US:** `/lʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loo/look_/look__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loo/look_/look__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Look closely and tell me what you see.
  - If you look carefully you can just see our house from here.
  - ‘Has the mail come yet?’ ‘I'll look and see.’
  - Look! I'm sure that's Jennifer Lawrence!
  - Don't look now, but there's someone staring at you!
  - look at somebody/something She looked at me and smiled.
  - look + adv./prep. I got up and looked out of the window.
  - She looked across to the other side of the room.

### [lose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/luːz/` · **US:** `/luːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/los/lose_/lose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/los/lose_/lose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've lost my keys.
  - The tickets seem to have got lost.
  - She lost her husband in the crowd.

### [lot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lot_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/lɒt/` · **US:** `/lɑːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lot/lot__/lot__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lot/lot__/lot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I care a lot about you.
  - Thanks a lot for your help.
  - I play tennis quite a lot (= often) in the summer.

### [lot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lot_2) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/lɒt/` · **US:** `/lɑːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lot/lot__/lot__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lot/lot__/lot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What a lot of presents!
  - A lot of people are coming to the meeting.
  - black coffee with lots of sugar
  - I saw a lot of her (= I saw her often) last summer.

### [lot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lot_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/lɒt/` · **US:** `/lɑːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lot/lot__/lot__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lot/lot__/lot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘How many do you need?’ ‘A lot.’
  - Have some more cake. There's lots left.
  - He has invited nearly a hundred people but a lot aren't able to come.
  - a lot to do I have a lot to do today.
  - She still has an awful lot (= a very large amount) to learn.

### [love](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/love_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lʌv/` · **US:** `/lʌv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lov/love_/love__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lov/love_/love__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She has earned the love and respect of many people.
  - love for somebody/something a mother’s unconditional love for her children
  - love of somebody/something love of your country

### [love](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/love_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lʌv/` · **US:** `/lʌv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lov/love_/love__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lov/love_/love__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I love you.
  - If you love each other, why not get married?
  - Her much-loved brother lay dying of AIDS.
  - He had become a well-loved member of staff.
  - Relatives need time to grieve over loved ones they have lost.
  - to love your country

### [lunch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lunch_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lʌntʃ/` · **US:** `/lʌntʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lun/lunch/lunch__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lun/lunch/lunch__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's gone to lunch.
  - I'm ready for some lunch.
  - I always eat/have lunch at my desk.
  - for lunch What shall we have for lunch?
  - over lunch Lopez suggested they meet over lunch (= at or during lunchtime).
  - We serve hot and cold lunches.
  - a one-hour lunch break
  - Let's do lunch (= have lunch together).
  - Christmas/Sunday lunch
  - a picnic/buffet lunch
  - We had a pub lunch (= lunch in a pub).


## M

### [machine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/machine_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/məˈʃiːn/` · **US:** `/məˈʃiːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mac/machi/machine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mac/machi/machine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Machines have replaced human labour in many industries.
  - to operate/run a machine
  - How does this machine work?
  - a washing/sewing machine
  - a machine for making plastic toys
  - They have installed a new coffee machine in the staff room.
  - to invent/design a machine
  - In the factory he oversees 14 teams of machine operators.
  - by machine The potatoes are planted by machine.

### [magazine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/magazine) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌmæɡəˈziːn/` · **US:** `/ˈmæɡəziːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mag/magaz/magazine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mag/magaz/magazine__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a weekly/monthly magazine
  - a magazine article/interview/story/feature/piece
  - an online magazine
  - a literary/news magazine
  - to read/edit/publish a magazine
  - a magazine editor/publisher/journalist/reporter
  - Her designer clothes were from the pages of a glossy fashion magazine.
  - She told the magazine she was making a new album.

### [main](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/main_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/meɪn/` · **US:** `/meɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mai/main_/main__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mai/main_/main__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Be careful crossing the main road.
  - the main course (= of a meal)
  - We have our main meal at lunchtime.
  - Reception is in the main building.
  - Poor housing and unemployment are the main problems.
  - Loss of habitat is the main reason for the bird's decline.
  - The main thing is to stay calm.

### [make](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/make_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/meɪk/` · **US:** `/meɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mak/make_/make__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mak/make_/make__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - make something to make a table/dress/cake
  - to make bread/cement/paper
  - She makes her own clothes.
  - How do you make that dish with the peppers and olives in it?
  - made in France (= on a label)
  - be made of something What's your shirt made of?
  - Traditional Japanese houses were made of wood.
  - be made out of something What's your shirt made out of?
  - be made from something Wine is made from grapes.
  - make something into something The grapes are made into wine.
  - make something for somebody She made coffee for us all.
  - make somebody something She made us all coffee.

### [man](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/man_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mæn/` · **US:** `/mæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/man__/man__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/man__/man__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a good-looking young man
  - the relationships between men and women
  - the men's team/game/race/doubles/singles/event
  - The board of directors is dominated by middle-aged white men.
  - He was one of the great men of history.
  - Four armed men burst into the room.
  - An elderly Indian man sat down beside me and recounted his story.
  - He is an average working man trying to make ends meet.

### [many](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/many) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeni/` · **US:** `/ˈmeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/many_/many__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/many_/many__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We don't have very many copies left.
  - You can't have one each. We haven't got many.
  - Many people feel that the law should be changed.
  - Many of those present disagreed.
  - How many children do you have?
  - There are too many mistakes in this essay.
  - He made ten mistakes in as many (= in ten) lines.
  - New drivers have twice as many accidents as experienced drivers.
  - Don't take so many.
  - I've known her for a great many (= very many) years.
  - Even if one person is hurt that is one too many.
  - It was one of my many mistakes.
  - a many-headed monster

### [many](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/many) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeni/` · **US:** `/ˈmeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/many_/many__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/many_/many__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We don't have very many copies left.
  - You can't have one each. We haven't got many.
  - Many people feel that the law should be changed.
  - Many of those present disagreed.
  - How many children do you have?
  - There are too many mistakes in this essay.
  - He made ten mistakes in as many (= in ten) lines.
  - New drivers have twice as many accidents as experienced drivers.
  - Don't take so many.
  - I've known her for a great many (= very many) years.
  - Even if one person is hurt that is one too many.
  - It was one of my many mistakes.
  - a many-headed monster

### [map](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/map_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mæp/` · **US:** `/mæp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/map/map__/map__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/map/map__/map__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a map of France
  - a street map of Miami
  - a map of the area/region/world/route
  - to read a/the map (= understand the information on a map)
  - large-scale maps
  - on a/the map Can you find Black Hill on the map?
  - to be marked/plotted/shown on a map
  - I'll draw you a map of how to get to my house.
  - Click here to download a map.
  - an interactive map

### [March](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/march_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mɑːtʃ/` · **US:** `/mɑːrtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/march/march__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/march/march__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was born in March.
  - (British English) The meeting is on the fifth of March/March the fifth.
  - (North American English) The meeting is on March fifth.
  - We went to Japan last March.
  - I arrived at the end of March.
  - Whale watching trips begin in early March.
  - The seeds germinate from March to May.
  - The study appears in the March issue of the Journal of Paediatrics.

### [market](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/market_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɑːkɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈmɑːrkɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marke/market__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marke/market__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fruit/flower/fish market
  - an antiques market
  - an indoor/a street market
  - market stalls/traders
  - at the market We buy our fruit and vegetables at the market.
  - Thursday is market day.

### [married](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/married) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmærid/` · **US:** `/ˈmærid/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marri/married__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marri/married__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a married man/woman
  - Is he married?
  - a happily/an unhappily married couple
  - married to somebody She's married to John.
  - Sam and Chris are getting married on Saturday.
  - How long have you been married?

### [match](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/match_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mætʃ/` · **US:** `/mætʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/match/match__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/match/match__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (British English) a football match
  - (North American English, British English) a tennis match
  - a league match
  - They are playing an important match against Liverpool on Saturday.
  - to win/lose a match
  - I'll probably watch the match on TV.
  - Dublin took on Toulouse in their opening match of the European Cup.
  - Ireland took the match (= won) 2–1 .

### [match](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/match_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/mætʃ/` · **US:** `/mætʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/match/match__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/match/match__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - match A and B Match the words and pictures.
  - match A to/with B The aim of the competition is to match the quote to the person who said it.
  - The agency tries to match single people with suitable partners.
  - match somebody/something for something The control group in the experiment was matched for age and sex.

### [May](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/may_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/meɪ/` · **US:** `/meɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/may/may_1/may_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/may/may__/may__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was born in May.
  - (British English) The meeting is on the fifth of May/May the fifth.
  - (North American English) The meeting is on May fifth.
  - We went to Japan last May.
  - I arrived at the end of May.
  - last May's election
  - Read about Paris markets in the May issue of the magazine.

### [maybe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/maybe_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeɪbi/` · **US:** `/ˈmeɪbi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/may/maybe/maybe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/may/maybe/maybe__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Maybe he'll come, maybe he won't.
  - ‘Are you going to sell your house?’ ‘Maybe.’
  - Is she right? Maybe, maybe not.
  - Maybe it will do me some good to go for a run.
  - It will cost two, maybe three hundred pounds.
  - We go there maybe once or twice a month.
  - There are two sides to every story, maybe even more in this case.

### [me](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/me_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/miː/` · **US:** `/miː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/me_/me__g/me__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/me_/me__u/me__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't hit me.
  - Excuse me!
  - Give it to me.
  - You're taller than me.
  - Hello, it's me.
  - ‘Who's there?’ ‘Only me.’

### [meal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/miːl/` · **US:** `/miːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/meal_/meal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/meal_/meal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Try not to eat between meals.
  - Lunch is his main meal of the day.
  - (especially British English) to go out for a meal (= to go to a restaurant to have a meal)
  - What time would you like your evening meal?

### [mean](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mean_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/miːn/` · **US:** `/miːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/mean_/mean__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/mean_/mean__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - mean something What does this sentence mean?
  - What does ‘lark’ mean?
  - The word ‘Kuching’ means ‘cat’ in Malay.
  - Many languages use a single word to mean both music and dance.
  - What is meant by ‘batch processing’?

### [meaning](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meaning_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmiːnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈmiːnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/meani/meaning__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/meani/meaning__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's the meaning of this word?
  - Words often have several meanings.
  - I am using the word with its original meaning.
  - The literal meaning of China is ‘the country at the centre’.
  - ‘Honesty’? He doesn't know the meaning of the word!

### [meat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meat) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/miːt/` · **US:** `/miːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mea/meat_/meat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mea/meat_/meat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a piece/slice of meat
  - horse meat (= from a horse)
  - dog meat (= either for a dog or from a dog)
  - meat-eating animals
  - Their diet consists of lean meat and vegetables.
  - Store raw meat on the bottom shelf of the refrigerator.
  - cured meat (= treated with smoke, salt, etc to preserve it)
  - Many processed meats have unhealthy levels of sodium.
  - meat products
  - Use the highest quality cuts of meat, fish or poultry.
  - There's not much meat on this chop.
  - (figurative, humorous) There’s not much meat on her (= she is very thin).

### [meet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meet_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/miːt/` · **US:** `/miːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mee/meet_/meet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mee/meet_/meet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Maybe we'll meet again some time.
  - meet somebody Did you meet anyone in town?
  - I've never met anyone like her.
  - A year or so later I happened to meet him again.
  - I hope we'll meet again soon.

### [meeting](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/meeting) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmiːtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈmiːtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mee/meeti/meeting__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mee/meeti/meeting__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A hundred people attended the public meeting.
  - The meeting will be held in the school hall.
  - to schedule/convene/arrange/organize/call a meeting
  - Helen will chair the meeting (= be in charge of it).
  - The meeting was postponed.
  - an annual meeting
  - a board/council/committee/public meeting
  - a series of meetings
  - What time is the meeting?
  - in a meeting I'll be in a meeting all morning—can you take my calls?
  - at a meeting The issue will be discussed at the next board meeting.
  - meeting of somebody/something a meeting of the United Nations Security Council
  - meeting with somebody She is due to have a meeting with senior government officials.
  - meeting about something A public meeting about the proposal takes place on Monday.
  - meeting between A and B This will be the first meeting between the two leaders since they took office.
  - Facilities include nine meeting rooms.
  - They rely on videoconferencing for virtual meetings.

### [member](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/member) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmembə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmembər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mem/membe/member__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mem/membe/member__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - member of something a member of the family/community
  - He is an important member of our team.
  - a member of the human race
  - Members of the public are invited to come and view the work
  - characteristics common to all members of the species
  - You are welcome to bring along a family member or friend.
  - Every staff member is offered training in customer care.

### [menu](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/menu) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmenjuː/` · **US:** `/ˈmenjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/men/menu_/menu__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/men/menu_/menu__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - May we have the menu?
  - to ask for/look at the menu
  - the lunch/dinner menu
  - on the menu What's on the menu (= for dinner) tonight?
  - The chef put together a special menu of drinks and dishes employing chocolate.

### [message](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/message_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmesɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈmesɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mes/messa/message__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mes/messa/message__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There were no messages for me at the hotel.
  - I never got your message.
  - We've had an urgent message saying that your father's ill.
  - Jenny's not here at the moment. Can I take a message?
  - I left a message on her voicemail.
  - I've been trying to get you all day—don't you ever listen to your messages?
  - message (from somebody) (to somebody) Messages of support have been arriving from all over the country.
  - a televised message from the president to the American people
  - A radio message was sent out to all ships in the area.

### [metre](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/metre) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmiːtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmiːtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/met/metre/metre__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/met/metre/metre__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 50-metre swimming pool
  - Every few metres the cat stopped and turned to look at me.
  - Over 3 700 square metres of office space is available.
  - The huge sculpture is made of 500 cubic metres of ice.
  - an athlete running at 10 metres per second

### [midnight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/midnight) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪdnaɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪdnaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mid/midni/midnight__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mid/midni/midnight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She heard the clock strike midnight.
  - at midnight They had to leave at midnight.
  - At/on the stroke of midnight fireworks lit up the sky.
  - We have to catch the midnight train.
  - Let’s have a midnight feast tonight (= a secret meal that children like to have in the middle of the night).
  - They whiled away the midnight hours playing cards.

### [mile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mile) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/maɪl/` · **US:** `/maɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mil/mile_/mile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mil/mile_/mile__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 20-mile drive to work
  - an area of four square miles
  - a mile-long procession
  - The nearest bank is about half a mile down the road.
  - The boys were left stranded two miles from home.
  - He runs 10 miles every morning.
  - We did about 30 miles a day on our cycling trip.
  - The car must have been doing at least 100 miles an hour.
  - (British English) My car does 35 miles to the gallon.
  - (North American English) My car gets 35 miles to the gallon.
  - (figurative) His thoughts were racing a mile a minute (= very fast).

### [milk](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/milk_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mɪlk/` · **US:** `/mɪlk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mil/milk_/milk__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mil/milk_/milk__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pint/litre of milk
  - a bottle/carton of milk
  - She drank a glass of cool frothy milk.
  - fresh/dried/powdered milk
  - Do you take milk in your tea?
  - milk products (= butter, cheese, etc.)
  - milk production/prices
  - I am allergic to cow's milk.
  - (British English) full-fat/semi-skimmed/skimmed milk (= milk with none/some/most of the fat removed)
  - (North American English) Shall I get skim milk or 2%?

### [million](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/million) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪljən/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪljən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mil/milli/million__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mil/milli/million__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a population of half a million
  - tens of millions of dollars
  - It must be worth a million (= pounds, dollars, etc.).
  - millions of years old

### [minute](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/minute1_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪnɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪnɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/min/minut/minute__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/min/minut/minute__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - minutes to… It's four minutes to six.
  - minutes past… four minutes past two
  - minutes after/before… five minutes after/before midnight
  - in… minutes I'll be back in a few minutes.
  - for… minutes Boil the rice for 20 minutes.
  - within minutes The ship sank within minutes.
  - per minute The pump delivers seven gallons per minute.
  - It's only a ten-minute bus ride into town.
  - Two minutes later the phone rang.
  - I enjoyed every minute of the party.
  - The students took fifteen minutes to complete the questionnaire.
  - Just minutes into the second half of the game Robinson scored his second goal.
  - Eguchi scored two goals in the 87th minute.

### [miss](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/miss_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/mɪs/` · **US:** `/mɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mis/miss_/miss__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mis/miss_/miss__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If I don't leave now I'll miss my plane.
  - Sorry I'm late—have I missed anything?
  - ‘Is Ann there?’ ‘You've just missed her (= she has just left).’
  - You'd better hurry—you don't want to miss the bus.
  - If you miss the deadline, you'll have to pay a fine.

### [mistake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mistake_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mɪˈsteɪk/` · **US:** `/mɪˈsteɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mis/mista/mistake__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mis/mista/mistake__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's easy to make a mistake.
  - Don't worry, we all make mistakes.
  - You are making a big mistake.
  - Leaving school so young was the biggest mistake of my life.
  - a terrible/serious/huge mistake
  - It’s a common mistake (= one that a lot of people make).
  - You must try to learn from your mistakes.
  - He is determined not to repeat the mistakes of his predecessors.
  - This letter is addressed to someone else—there must be some mistake.
  - the mistake of doing something I made the mistake of giving him my address.
  - it is a mistake to do something It would be a mistake to ignore his opinion.
  - It was a big mistake on my part to have trusted her.

### [model](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/model_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɒdl/` · **US:** `/ˈmɑːdl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mod/model/model__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mod/model/model__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a working model (= one in which the parts move) of a fire engine
  - I used to build model aeroplanes.
  - The architect had produced a scale model of the proposed shopping complex.

### [modern](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/modern) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɒdn/` · **US:** `/ˈmɑːdərn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mod/moder/modern__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mod/moder/modern__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the modern industrial world
  - the wonders of modern science
  - modern society
  - In modern times, advances in technology have made the world smaller.
  - They are modern day heroes, battling for change.
  - Modern European history
  - modern Greek
  - Stress is a major problem of modern life.
  - Shakespeare's language can be a problem for modern readers.
  - The modern era has witnessed great change.
  - She studies the early modern period from the 15th to the 18th century.

### [moment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/moment) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈməʊmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈməʊmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mom/momen/moment__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mom/momen/moment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Could you wait a moment, please?
  - One moment, please (= Please wait a short time).
  - for a moment He thought for a moment before replying.
  - For one brief moment I thought, ‘I can handle this.’
  - in a moment I'll be back in a moment.
  - Have you got a moment?
  - This won't take a moment.
  - We arrived not a moment too soon (= almost too late).
  - Moments later (= a very short time later), I heard a terrible crash.

### [Monday](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/monday) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmʌndi/` · **US:** `/ˈmʌndi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mon/monda/monday__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mon/monda/monday__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's Monday today, isn't it?
  - She started work last Monday.
  - Are you busy next Monday?
  - Monday morning/afternoon/evening
  - We'll discuss this at Monday's meeting.
  - I work Monday to Friday.
  - I work Mondays to Fridays.
  - on Monday We'll meet on Monday (= next Monday).
  - We met on Monday (= last Monday).
  - (informal or North American English) We'll meet Monday.
  - on Mondays The museum is closed on Mondays (= every Monday).
  - (especially North American English) The museum is closed Mondays.
  - on a Monday He was born on a Monday.
  - I always do yoga on a Monday (= every Monday).
  - I went to Paris on Thursday, and came back the following Monday.
  - (British English) ‘When did the accident happen?’ ‘It was the Monday (= the Monday of the week we are talking about).’
  - (British English) Come back Monday week (= a week after next Monday).

### [money](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/money) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmʌni/` · **US:** `/ˈmʌni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mon/money/money__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mon/money/money__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to borrow/save/spend/earn money
  - How much money is there in my account?
  - The money is much better in my new job.
  - If the item is not satisfactory, you will get your money back.
  - We'll need to raise more money (= collect or borrow it) next year.
  - Can you lend me some money until tomorrow?
  - Be careful with that—it cost a lot of money.
  - Careful with that—I paid good money for it!
  - This is a great product, well worth the money.
  - They sensibly invested their prize money rather than spending it.
  - To hold another election is a waste of time and money.

### [month](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/month) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mʌnθ/` · **US:** `/mʌnθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mon/month/month__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mon/month/month__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The months of July and August are the hottest.
  - May and June are the two wettest months of the year in Nebraska.
  - during the summer/winter months
  - We're moving house next month.
  - Last month I went on a school trip to Germany.
  - We got married two months ago.
  - a/per month She earns $2 000 a month.
  - The rent is £800 per month.
  - Have you read this month's ‘Physics World’?
  - Prices continue to rise month after month (= over a period of several months).
  - Her anxiety mounted month by month (= as each month passed).

### [more](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/more_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/mɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/mɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mor/more_/more__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mor/more_/more__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was far more intelligent than her sister.
  - He read the letter more carefully the second time.

### [more](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/more_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/mɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/mɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mor/more_/more__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mor/more_/more__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I want some more!
  - more something more bread/cars
  - Only two more days to go!
  - people with more money than sense
  - more of something/somebody I can't stand much more of this.
  - I hope we'll see more of you (= see you again or more often).
  - more than… She earns a lot more than I do.
  - There is room for no more than three cars.

### [more](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/many) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeni/` · **US:** `/ˈmeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mor/more_/more__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mor/more_/more__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We don't have very many copies left.
  - You can't have one each. We haven't got many.
  - Many people feel that the law should be changed.
  - Many of those present disagreed.
  - How many children do you have?
  - There are too many mistakes in this essay.
  - He made ten mistakes in as many (= in ten) lines.
  - New drivers have twice as many accidents as experienced drivers.
  - Don't take so many.
  - I've known her for a great many (= very many) years.
  - Even if one person is hurt that is one too many.
  - It was one of my many mistakes.
  - a many-headed monster

### [morning](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/morning_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɔːnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈmɔːrnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mor/morni/morning__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mor/morni/morning__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They left for Spain early this morning.
  - See you tomorrow morning.
  - She drove to Bristol yesterday morning.
  - She still felt unwell the following morning.
  - in the morning I prefer coffee in the morning.
  - first thing in the morning
  - She woke every morning at the same time.
  - I walk to work most mornings.
  - He's been in a meeting all morning.
  - Our group meets on Friday mornings.
  - on the morning of something We got the news on the morning of the wedding.
  - Have you seen the morning papers?
  - Buses are often delayed during the morning rush hour.
  - I'm not a morning person (= I don't function best in the morning).

### [most](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/most_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/məʊst/` · **US:** `/məʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mos/most_/most__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mos/most_/most__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the most boring/beautiful part
  - It was the people with the least money who gave most generously.

### [most](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/most_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/məʊst/` · **US:** `/məʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mos/most_/most__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mos/most_/most__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who do you think will get (the) most votes?
  - She had the most money of all of them.
  - I spent most time on the first question.
  - Who ate the most?
  - The director has the most to lose.

### [most](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/many) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeni/` · **US:** `/ˈmeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mos/most_/most__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mos/most_/most__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We don't have very many copies left.
  - You can't have one each. We haven't got many.
  - Many people feel that the law should be changed.
  - Many of those present disagreed.
  - How many children do you have?
  - There are too many mistakes in this essay.
  - He made ten mistakes in as many (= in ten) lines.
  - New drivers have twice as many accidents as experienced drivers.
  - Don't take so many.
  - I've known her for a great many (= very many) years.
  - Even if one person is hurt that is one too many.
  - It was one of my many mistakes.
  - a many-headed monster

### [mother](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mother_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmʌðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmʌðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mot/mothe/mother__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mot/mothe/mother__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I want to buy a present for my mother and father.
  - the relationship between mother and baby
  - She's the mother of twins.
  - a mother of three (= with three children)
  - a young/new/single mother
  - an expectant (= pregnant) mother
  - Jenny is a stay-at-home mother of three.
  - Affordable childcare is critical for working mothers.
  - She was a wonderful mother to both her natural and adopted children.
  - the mother chimpanzee caring for her young

### [mountain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mountain) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmaʊntən/` · **US:** `/ˈmaʊntn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mou/mount/mountain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mou/mount/mountain__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the mountains of Andalusia
  - snow-capped mountains
  - The steep mountains tower all around.
  - to climb a mountain
  - in the mountains We spent a week walking in the mountains.
  - to enjoy the mountain air/scenery
  - mountain roads/streams/villages
  - the world's longest mountain range
  - There is a range of mountains to the west.
  - high mountain passes
  - a mountain rescue team
  - There is still snow on the mountain tops.

### [mouse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mouse_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/maʊs/` · **US:** `/maʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mou/mouse/mouse__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mou/mouse/mouse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a house mouse
  - The stores were overrun with rats and mice.
  - She crept upstairs, quiet as a mouse.
  - (figurative) He was a shy little mouse.

### [mouth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mouth_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/maʊθ/` · **US:** `/maʊθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mou/mouth/mouth__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mou/mouth/mouth__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She opened her mouth to say something.
  - His mouth twisted into a wry smile.
  - Their mouths fell open (= they were surprised).
  - Don't talk with your mouth full (= when eating).
  - The creature was foaming/frothing at the mouth.
  - Cover your mouth when you cough.
  - The adult bird drops the worms into the mouths of the hungry chicks.

### [move](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/move_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/muːv/` · **US:** `/muːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mov/move_/move__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mov/move_/move__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't move—stay perfectly still.
  - The bus was already moving when I jumped onto it.
  - The ball was really moving (= moving fast) when it reached him.
  - + adv./prep. He could hear someone moving around in the room above.
  - Phil moved towards the window.
  - You can hardly move in this pub on Saturdays (= because it is so crowded).
  - You can't move for books in her room.
  - Temperatures will drop as the cold front moves down across France.
  - move something I can't move my fingers.
  - move something + adv./prep. We moved our chairs a little nearer.
  - She moved the ball toward the opponent's goal.
  - He moved his arm away from his face.

### [movie](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/movie) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmuːvi/` · **US:** `/ˈmuːvi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mov/movie/movie__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mov/movie/movie__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You'll love this movie.
  - Let's watch a movie tonight.
  - Have you seen the latest Miyazaki movie?
  - to rent/download a movie
  - to make a horror movie
  - a movie starring Jennifer Lawrence
  - a famous movie director
  - the Hollywood movie industry
  - a movie studio

### [much](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/much_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/mʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/mʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/muc/much_/much__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/muc/much_/much__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Thank you very much for the flowers.
  - I would very much like to see you again.
  - He isn't in the office much (= often).
  - You worry too much.
  - My new job is much the same as the old one.
  - Much to her surprise he came back the next day.
  - She's much better today.
  - The other one was much too expensive.
  - Nikolai's English was much the worst.
  - We are very much aware of the lack of food supplies.
  - I'm not much good at tennis.
  - He was much loved by all who knew him.
  - an appeal to raise much-needed cash

### [much](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/much_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/mʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/mʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/muc/much_/much__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/muc/much_/much__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't have much money with me.
  - ‘Got any money?’ ‘Not much.’
  - How much water do you need?
  - How much is it (= What does it cost)?
  - Take as much time as you like.
  - There was so much traffic that we were an hour late.
  - I've got far too much to do.
  - (formal) I lay awake for much of the night.
  - (formal) There was much discussion about the reasons for the failure.

### [much](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/many) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeni/` · **US:** `/ˈmeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/muc/much_/much__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/muc/much_/much__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We don't have very many copies left.
  - You can't have one each. We haven't got many.
  - Many people feel that the law should be changed.
  - Many of those present disagreed.
  - How many children do you have?
  - There are too many mistakes in this essay.
  - He made ten mistakes in as many (= in ten) lines.
  - New drivers have twice as many accidents as experienced drivers.
  - Don't take so many.
  - I've known her for a great many (= very many) years.
  - Even if one person is hurt that is one too many.
  - It was one of my many mistakes.
  - a many-headed monster

### [mum](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mum_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mʌm/` · **US:** `/mʌm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mum/mum_1/mum_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mum/mum__/mum__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Call your mum.
  - My mum says I can't go.
  - Happy Birthday, Mum.
  - A lot of mums and dads have the same worries.
  - She's a single mum with two kids.
  - expectant/new/young/teenage mums
  - stay-at-home/working mums

### [museum](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/museum) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mjuˈziːəm/` · **US:** `/mjuˈziːəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/museu/museum__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/museu/museum__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a museum of modern art
  - a science museum
  - They visited museums and galleries throughout the city.
  - He's the director of a new museum.
  - at/in a museum The items will go on display at the museum next year.
  - a museum director/curator
  - Her prints are in major museum collections.

### [music](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/music) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmjuːzɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈmjuːzɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/music/music__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/music/music__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I like any kind of pop or dance music.
  - He plays traditional Korean music.
  - People often choose to listen to music that suits their moods and activities.
  - She could hear music playing somewhere.
  - to stream/share/download music
  - to write/compose music
  - to perform/record music
  - Every week they get together to make music (= to play music or sing).
  - the popularity of Mozart’s music
  - It was a charming piece of music.
  - The poem has been set to music (= music has been written for it)
  - Birmingham's live music scene
  - music lovers/fans
  - Every summer he runs a three-day music festival.
  - The band released a music video on YouTube today.

### [must](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/must_1) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/mʌst/` · **US:** `/mʌst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/must_/must__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/must_/must__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - All visitors must report to reception.
  - Cars must not be parked in front of the entrance (= it is not allowed).
  - (formal) I must ask you not to do that again.
  - You mustn't say things like that.
  - I must go and get the kids from school.
  - I must admit (= I feel that I should admit) I was surprised it cost so little.
  - (especially British English) Must you always question everything I say? (= it is annoying)
  - ‘Do we have to finish this today?’ ‘Yes, you must.’

### [my](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/my) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/maɪ/` · **US:** `/maɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/my_/my__g/my__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/my_/my__u/my__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where's my passport?
  - My feet are cold.


## N

### [name](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/name_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/neɪm/` · **US:** `/neɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nam/name_/name__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nam/name_/name__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's your name?
  - What is/was the name, please? (= a polite way of asking somebody’s name)
  - Please write your full name and address below.
  - Leave your name and number (= phone number) and we'll call you later.
  - Are you changing your name when you get married?
  - She uses her married name at work.
  - The singer's real name is Bob Rich.
  - His full name was William Augustus Grove.
  - His father was a painter of the same name.
  - Do you know the name of this flower?
  - name for something Rubella is just another name for German measles.
  - under a… name The company reopened under a new name.

### [name](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/name_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/neɪm/` · **US:** `/neɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nam/name_/name__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nam/name_/name__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - name somebody/something (after somebody) He was named after his father (= given his father's first name).
  - name somebody/something (for somebody) The planet Mars is named for the Roman god of war.
  - name somebody/something + noun They named their son John.
  - adv. + named He plays the evil scientist, the aptly named Dr Weird.
  - Rust fungus is so named because of its rusty-coloured spots.

### [natural](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/natural_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnætʃrəl/` · **US:** `/ˈnætʃrəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/natur/natural__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/natur/natural__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the natural world (= of trees, rivers, animals and birds)
  - We should respect animals and the natural environment, which is their home.
  - a country’s natural resources (= its coal, oil, forests, etc.)
  - wildlife in its natural habitat
  - The natural beauty of flowers inspired Monet's experiments in colour.
  - All the materials used are natural.
  - My hair soon grew back to its natural colour (= after being dyed).
  - The clothes are available in warm natural colours.

### [near](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/near_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/nɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/nɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nea/near_/near__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nea/near_/near__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His house is very near.
  - Where's the nearest bank?
  - Wales may be a near neighbour but its history is quite distinct.

### [near](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/near_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/nɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/nɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nea/near_/near__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nea/near_/near__us_1.mp3)
