- **Examples:**
  - A bomb exploded somewhere near.
  - She took a step nearer.
  - Visitors came from near and far.

### [near](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/near_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/nɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/nɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nea/near_/near__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nea/near_/near__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you live near here?
  - Go and sit nearer (to) the fire.

### [need](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/need_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/niːd/` · **US:** `/niːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nee/need_/need__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nee/need_/need__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - need something/somebody Do you need any help?
  - It's here if you need it.
  - Don't go—I might need you.
  - They badly needed a change.
  - We desperately need hard facts on this disease.
  - Food aid is urgently needed.
  - What do you need your own computer for? You can use ours.
  - She needs more time to recover.
  - People need income not handouts.
  - More new housing is needed in the city.
  - I don't need your comments, thank you.
  - need to do something I need to get some sleep.
  - He needs to win this game to stay in the match.
  - You don't need to leave yet, do you?
  - You need to know how toxic the product is.
  - This shirt needs to be washed.
  - something needs doing This shirt needs washing.

### [negative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/negative_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɡətɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈneɡətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/neg/negat/negative__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/neg/negat/negative__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The crisis had a negative effect on trade.
  - Government cuts will have a negative impact on public services.
  - The whole experience was definitely more positive than negative.

### [neighbour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/neighbour_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪbə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nei/neigh/neighbour__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nei/neigh/neighbour__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We've had a lot of support from all our friends and neighbours.
  - Our next-door neighbours are very noisy.
  - neighbour to somebody She's been a very good neighbour to me.

### [never](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/never_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnevə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nev/never/never__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nev/never/never__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You never help me.
  - He'll never forget her.
  - He has never been abroad.
  - She had never seen so much snow.
  - She left the country, vowing never to return again.
  - ‘Would you vote for him?’ ‘Never.’
  - ‘I work for a company called Orion Technology.’ ‘Never heard of them.’
  - Never in all my life have I seen such a horrible thing.
  - Never ever tell anyone your password.
  - Never before has English cuisine been so stylish.

### [new](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/new) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/njuː/` · **US:** `/nuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/new/new__/new__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/new/new__/new__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Have you read her new novel?
  - We retrain staff to use the new technology.
  - The company is trialling a new security system.
  - Have you seen Danielle's new baby?
  - a new addition to the family (= a baby)
  - The school secretary wants to introduce new ways of doing things in the office.
  - This idea isn't new.
  - The latest model has over 100 new features.
  - Check out the band's new album.

### [news](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/news) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/njuːz/` · **US:** `/nuːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/new/news_/news__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/new/news_/news__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's the latest news?
  - Have you heard the news? Pat's leaving!
  - That's great news.
  - Tell me all your news.
  - Do you want the good news or the bad news first?
  - saddened/shocked/surprised by the news
  - Here's a piece of news that may interest you.
  - We've had a bit of good news.
  - news of somebody/something Have you had any news of Patrick?
  - news on something Any news on the deal?
  - news about somebody/something He gave me some news about the office move.
  - news that… Messengers brought news that the battle had been lost.
  - (informal) It's news to me (= I haven't heard it before).

### [newspaper](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/newspaper) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnjuːzpeɪpə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnuːzpeɪpər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/new/newsp/newspaper__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/new/newsp/newspaper__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a daily/weekly newspaper
  - a local/national newspaper
  - an online newspaper
  - a tabloid/broadsheet newspaper
  - a newspaper article/report/headline
  - a newspaper cutting/clipping
  - a newspaper editor/reporter/columnist
  - newspapers and news websites
  - British newspapers and their websites carried no photos of the event.
  - Which newspaper do you read?
  - to publish/own/print a newspaper
  - The newspaper reported on Friday that the boy had been found.
  - in the newspaper I read about it in the newspaper.
  - according to the newspaper The company is considering up to 4000 job losses, according to The Times newspaper.
  - She works for the local newspaper (= the company that produces it).

### [next](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/next_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/nekst/` · **US:** `/nekst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nex/next_/next__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nex/next_/next__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The next train to Baltimore is at ten.
  - The next six months will be the hardest.
  - The next chapter deals with the post-war situation.
  - Who's next?
  - The woman in the next room was talking in a very loud voice.
  - The parcel will arrive in the next few days.
  - I fainted and the next thing I knew I was in the hospital.
  - (informal) Round here, you leave school and next thing you know you're married with three kids.

### [next](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/next_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/nekst/` · **US:** `/nekst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nex/next_/next__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nex/next_/next__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What happened next?
  - Next, I heard the sound of voices.
  - We're excited to see what she'll do next.
  - What came next was a real shock.
  - Strawberries and cream come next.

### [next to](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/next-to) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnekst tu/` · **US:** `/ˈnekst tu/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nex/next_/next_to_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nex/next_/next_to_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We sat next to each other.

### [nice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nice) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/naɪs/` · **US:** `/naɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nic/nice_/nice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nic/nice_/nice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a nice day/smile/place
  - nice weather
  - Did you have a nice time?
  - You look very nice.
  - We all had the flu last week—it wasn't very nice.
  - ‘Do you want to come, too?’ ‘Yes, that would be nice.’
  - The nicest thing about her is that she never criticizes us.
  - It was a nice touch to end the film as it started.
  - it is nice to do something It's nice to know that somebody appreciates what I do.
  - Nice to meet you! (= a friendly greeting when you meet somebody for the first time)
  - It is nice doing something It's been nice meeting you.
  - it is nice that… It's nice that you can come with us.
  - it would be nice if… It would be nice if he moved to London.

### [night](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/night) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/naɪt/` · **US:** `/naɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nig/night/night__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nig/night/night__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at night These animals only come out at night.
  - by night They sleep by day and hunt by night.
  - in the night Does your baby still wake in the night?
  - She woke up in the middle of the night.
  - The accident happened on Friday night.
  - on the night of… on the night of 10 January/January 10
  - Did you hear the storm last night?
  - I lay awake all night.
  - Where did you spend the night?
  - You're welcome to stay the night here.
  - What is he doing calling at this time of night?
  - You'll feel better after you've had a good night's sleep.
  - She spent a sleepless night tossing and turning.
  - The trip was for ten nights.
  - per night The hotel costs €95 per person per night.
  - the night train/boat/flight
  - The night sky was clear and full of stars.
  - Night fell (= it became dark).

### [nine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nine) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/naɪn/` · **US:** `/naɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nin/nine_/nine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nin/nine_/nine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are only nine of these rare animals left.
  - nine of Sweden’s top financial experts
  - Twelve people were invited but only nine turned up.
  - Can you lend me nine dollars?
  - a nine-month contract
  - Look at page nine.
  - Nine and one is ten.
  - Two nines are eighteen.
  - I can't read your writing—is this meant to be a nine?
  - The bulbs are planted in sevens or nines (= groups of seven or nine).
  - We moved to America when I was nine (= nine years old).
  - Shall we meet at nine (= at nine o'clock), then?

### [nineteen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nineteen) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˌnaɪnˈtiːn/` · **US:** `/ˌnaɪnˈtiːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nin/ninet/nineteen__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nin/ninet/nineteen__us_1.mp3)

### [ninety](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ninety) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnaɪnti/` · **US:** `/ˈnaɪnti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nin/ninet/ninety__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nin/ninet/ninety__us_1.mp3)

### [no](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/no_2) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊ/` · **US:** `/nəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/no_/no__g/no__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/no_/no__u/no__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - No student is to leave the room.
  - I have no meetings this morning.
  - There's no bread left.
  - No two days are the same.

### [no](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/no_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊ/` · **US:** `/nəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/no_/no__g/no__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/no_/no__u/no__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Just say yes or no.
  - ‘Are you ready?’ ‘No, I'm not.’
  - Sorry, the answer's no.
  - ‘Another drink?’ ‘No, thanks.’
  - It's about 70—no, I'm wrong—80 kilometres from Rome.
  - No! Don't touch it! It's hot.
  - ‘It was Tony.’ ‘No, you're wrong. It was Ted.’
  - ‘It's not very good, is it?’ ‘No, you're right, it isn't (= I agree).’

### [no one](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/no-one) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊ wʌn/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊ wʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/no_/no_on/no_one_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/no_/no_on/no_one_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - No one was at home.
  - There was no one else around.
  - We were told to speak to no one.

### [nobody](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nobody_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊbədi/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊbədi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nob/nobod/nobody__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nob/nobod/nobody__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Nobody knew what to say.

### [north](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/north_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/nɔːθ/` · **US:** `/nɔːrθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/north/north__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/north/north__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - North London
  - the north bank of the river
  - the north end/side of the building
  - We ended up on the rugged north coast of California.
  - Enter the cathedral through the north door.

### [north](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/north_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/nɔːθ/` · **US:** `/nɔːrθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/north/north__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/north/north__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The house faces north.
  - The train was heading north to Paris.

### [north](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/north_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nɔːθ/` · **US:** `/nɔːrθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/north/north__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/north/north__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which way is north?
  - cold winds coming from the north
  - a north-facing wall
  - to the north (of…) Mount Kenya is to the north of (= further north than) Nairobi.
  - views of distant mountains to north and south

### [nose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nose_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊz/` · **US:** `/nəʊz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nos/nose_/nose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nos/nose_/nose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She had dark eyes and a long narrow nose.
  - He broke his nose in the fight.
  - She wrinkled her nose in disgust.
  - He pressed his nose up against the window.
  - through your nose Try to breathe through your nose.
  - He blew his nose (= cleared it by blowing strongly into a handkerchief).
  - a blocked/runny nose
  - Stop picking your nose! (= removing dirt from it with your finger)
  - He held his nose as he passed the stinking dustbins.
  - (figurative) He held his nose and voted for the bill anyway.
  - He pushed his glasses up the bridge of his nose (= the hard part near the top).

### [not](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/not) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/nɒt/` · **US:** `/nɑːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/not__/not__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/not__/not__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She did not/didn’t see him.
  - It’s not/It isn’t raining.
  - It's cold, isn't it?
  - Don't you eat meat?
  - I can't see from here.
  - He must not go.

### [note](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/note_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊt/` · **US:** `/nəʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/note_/note__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/note_/note__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Please make a note of the dates.
  - She made a mental note (= decided that she must remember) to ask Alan about it.
  - Note to self (= a mental instruction): book more holidays.

### [nothing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nothing) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnʌθɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈnʌθɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/nothi/nothing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/nothi/nothing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There was nothing in her bag.
  - There's nothing you can do to help.
  - The doctor said there was nothing wrong with me.
  - Nothing else matters to him apart from his job.
  - It cost us nothing to go in.
  - (British English) He's five foot nothing (= exactly five feet tall).

### [November](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/november) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nəʊˈvembə(r)/` · **US:** `/nəʊˈvembər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nov/novem/november__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nov/novem/november__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was born in November.
  - (British English) The meeting is on the fifth of November/November the fifth.
  - (North American English) The meeting is on November fifth.
  - We went to Japan last November.
  - I arrived at the end of November.
  - last November's election
  - It was a cold November night.

### [now](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/now_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/naʊ/` · **US:** `/naʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/now/now__/now__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/now/now__/now__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where are you living now?
  - They now have everything they wanted.
  - We now know the truth.
  - It's too late now.
  - It's been two weeks now since she called.
  - I can tell you right now that you're wrong.
  - I didn't receive the letter but it hardly matters now.
  - from now on From now on I'll be more careful.
  - by now He should be home by now.
  - up till now I've lived at home up till now.
  - for now That's all for now.

### [number](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/number_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnʌmbə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnʌmbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/num/numbe/number__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/num/numbe/number__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Think of a number and multiply it by two.
  - a high/low number
  - even numbers (= 2, 4, 6, etc.)
  - odd numbers (= 1, 3, 5, etc.)
  - You owe me 27 dollars? Make it 30, that's a good round number.

### [nurse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nurse_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nɜːs/` · **US:** `/nɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nur/nurse/nurse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nur/nurse/nurse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a registered nurse
  - a qualified/trained nurse
  - She trained as a nurse in Korea.
  - student nurses
  - a male nurse
  - a dental nurse (= one who helps a dentist)
  - a psychiatric nurse (= one who works in a hospital for people with mental illnesses)
  - a team of doctors and nurses
  - a nurses' station (= an office for nurses in a hospital)
  - Nurse Bennett
  - Nurse, come quickly!
  - I work as a nurse in a busy hospital.


## O

### [o'clock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/o-clock) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈklɒk/` · **US:** `/əˈklɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/o_c/o_clo/o_clock_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/o_c/o_clo/o_clock_1_us_1.mp3)

### [object](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/object_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒbdʒekt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːbdʒekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/obj/objec/object__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/obj/objec/object__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - everyday objects such as cups and saucers
  - inanimate objects (= things that are not alive)
  - a physical/material object (= that you can see and touch)

### [October](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/october) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɒkˈtəʊbə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɑːkˈtəʊbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oct/octob/october_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oct/octob/october__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was born in October.

  - (North American English) The meeting is on October fifth.
  - We went to Japan last October.
  - I arrived at the end of October.
  - last October's election
  - the October issue of the magazine

### [of](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/of) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ɒv/` · **US:** `/ʌv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/of_/of__g/of__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/of_/of__u/of__us_1_rr.mp3)

  - the paintings of Monet

### [off](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/off_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɒf/` · **US:** `/ɔːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/off__/off__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/off__/off__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's had his beard shaved off.
  - Take your coat off.
  - The label must have fallen off.
  - Don't leave the toothpaste with the top off.

### [off](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/off_2) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ɒf/` · **US:** `/ɔːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/off__/off__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/off__/off__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I fell off the ladder.
  - Keep off the grass!
  - an island off the coast of Spain
  - They were still 100 metres off the summit.
  - Scientists are still a long way off finding a cure.
  - We're getting right off the subject.

### [office](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/office) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːfɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offic/office__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offic/office__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The company is moving to new offices on the other side of town.
  - Are you going to the office today?
  - in the office I'm sorry, Mr Anders is not in the office today.
  - at the office I've had a tough day at the office.
  - a regional/branch office
  - office workers
  - an office job
  - an office building
  - The new development will include housing and office space.
  - out of the office I'm going to be out of the office next week.

### [often](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/often) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒftən/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːftən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oft/often/often__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oft/often/often__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We often go there.
  - I've often wondered what happened to him.
  - How often do you go to the theatre?
  - I see her quite often.
  - Try to exercise as often as possible.
  - We should meet for lunch more often.
  - It is not often that you get such an opportunity.

### [oh](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/oh_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/əʊ/` · **US:** `/əʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/owe/owe__/owe__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oh_/oh__u/oh__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Oh dear! What's happened now?
  - You can’t come tonight? Oh well, see you next week then.
  - ‘Emma has a new job.’ ‘Oh, has she?’

- **UK:** `/əʊˈkeɪ/` · **US:** `/əʊˈkeɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ok_/ok__g/ok__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ok_/ok__u/ok__us_1.mp3)

### [OK](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ok_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əʊˈkeɪ/` · **US:** `/əʊˈkeɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ok_/ok__g/ok__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ok_/ok__u/ok__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Are you OK?

### [OK](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ok_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/əʊˈkeɪ/` · **US:** `/əʊˈkeɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ok_/ok__g/ok__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ok_/ok__u/ok__us_1.mp3)

### [old](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/old) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əʊld/` · **US:** `/əʊld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/old/old__/old__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/old/old__/old__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - be… years, months, etc. old The baby was only a few hours old.
  - In those days most people left school when they were only fifteen years old.
  - At thirty years old, he was already earning £40 000 a year.
  - two fourteen-year-old boys
  - a class for five-year-olds (= children who are five)
  - I didn't think she was old enough for the responsibility.
  - He's not too old to play Romeo.
  - How old is this building?
  - He's the oldest player in the team.
  - She's much older than me.
  - My two older sisters and I shared a bedroom.

### [on](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/on_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɒn/` · **US:** `/ɑːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/on_/on__g/on__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/on_/on__u/on__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - I didn't have my glasses on.
  - What did she have on (= what was she wearing)?

### [on](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/on_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ɒn/` · **US:** `/ɑːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/on_/on__g/on__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/on_/on__u/on__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a picture on a wall
  - There's a mark on your skirt.
  - the diagram on page 5
  - Put it down on the table.
  - He had been hit on the head.
  - She climbed on to the bed.

### [once](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/once_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wʌns/` · **US:** `/wʌns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onc/once_/once__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onc/once_/once__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've only been there once.
  - He cleans his car once a week.
  - She only sees her parents once every six months.
  - For optimum effect, these drops are used once daily.
  - I had met Lou once before.
  - Last year the region received rain only once.
  - (informal) He only did it the once.
  - Rome is a city you should visit at least once in your life.
  - They meet once a week at a Paris café.

- **UK:** `/wʌn/` · **US:** `/wʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/one/one__/one__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/one/one__/one__us_1.mp3)

  - I'll see you at one (= one o'clock).

### [one](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/one_1) *(number)*

- **Examples:**

  - There's only room for one person.

  - a one-bedroomed apartment
  - I'll see you at one (= one o'clock).

### [one](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/one_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/wʌn/` · **US:** `/wʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/one/one__/one__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/one/one__/one__us_1.mp3)

  - She was wearing her new dress, the red one.
  - Steve's the one with the blue jacket.

### [online](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/online_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɒnˈlaɪn/` · **US:** `/ˌɑːnˈlaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onl/onlin/online__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onl/onlin/online__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Online shopping is both cheap and convenient.
  - online stores/services/retailers
  - online dating/gaming/banking
  - He spends hours playing online games.
  - The online community is a place where customers with similar interests meet to interact.
  - All this information is now online.

### [online](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/online_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɒnˈlaɪn/` · **US:** `/ˌɑːnˈlaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onl/onlin/online__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onl/onlin/online__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Many children would rather go online than watch television.
  - Many people now do their banking online.
  - Some people are only happy to buy products online from businesses they know.
  - Your tax situation is the same whether you trade online or offline.

### [only](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/only_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊnli/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊnli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onl/only_/only__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onl/only_/only__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's their only daughter.
  - We were the only people there.
  - His only answer was a grunt.
  - I was not the only one with moist eyes at the end of the film.
  - The only way he can express himself is through violence.
  - The only reason for this crisis is poor planning.
  - This might be the only time in my life I have this opportunity.
  - The only real difference between them is their packaging.
  - That's the only possible explanation I can find.
  - We have no choice. The only other option is to die.

### [only](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/only_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊnli/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊnli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/onl/only_/only__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/onl/only_/only__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are only a limited number of tickets available.
  - The bar is for members only.
  - You only have to look at her to see she doesn't eat enough.
  - Only five people turned up.
  - They are second only to Nike in the sale of running shoes.
  - The bill will apply only to young workers.

### [open](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/open_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊpən/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊpən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ope/open_/open__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ope/open_/open__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A wasp flew in the open window.
  - She had left the door wide open.
  - The castle gates swung open.
  - The door burst open and the children rushed in.
  - She held the door open for them.
  - In spite of the snow, the roads remained open.
  - The mountain pass is kept open all year.
  - open borders
  - Borders between the countries are open and passports are not required.
  - Taylor headed the ball into the open goal.

### [open](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/open_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊpən/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊpən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ope/open_/open__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ope/open_/open__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Mr Chen opened the car door for his wife.
  - She opened all the windows wide to let some fresh air in.
  - He opened the lid of the piano.
  - Do you have a key that opens this gate?
  - The door opened and Alan walked in.
  - The doors of the bus open automatically.

### [opinion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opinion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɪnjən/` · **US:** `/əˈpɪnjən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opi/opini/opinion__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opi/opini/opinion__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has very strong political opinions.
  - opinion about something/somebody We were invited to give our opinions about how the work should be done.
  - opinion of somebody/something I've recently changed my opinion of her.
  - opinion on something You will have the chance to voice your opinions on the matter.
  - opinion about somebody/something We all have opinions about what is wrong with the process.
  - opinion that… The chairman expressed the opinion that job losses were inevitable.
  - I formed the opinion that he was not to be trusted.
  - (formal) It is our opinion that he should resign.
  - in my opinion In my personal opinion, it's a very sound investment.
  - If you want my opinion, I think you'd be crazy not to accept.
  - (ironic or humorous) In my humble opinion, you were wrong.
  - He writes an opinion piece in the ‘New York Times’.

### [opposite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opposite_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒpəzɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːpəzɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Answers are given on the opposite page.
  - We live further down on the opposite side of the road.
  - It's not easy having a relationship when you live at opposite ends of the country.

### [opposite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opposite_4) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒpəzɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːpəzɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There's a newly married couple living opposite (= on the other side of the road).
  - See opposite (= on the opposite page) for further details.

### [opposite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opposite_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒpəzɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːpəzɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Hot and cold are opposites.
  - the opposite of something What is the opposite of heavy?
  - She says the exact opposite of what she means—it's confusing.
  - the opposite to something The effect was exactly the opposite to what he intended.
  - I thought she would be small and blonde but she's the complete opposite.
  - Archie and Mike are polar opposites forced to live under the same roof.
  - ‘Is it better now?’ ‘Quite the opposite, I'm afraid.’
  - I ask you to do something and you go and do the opposite—why?
  - I have never been asked to leave. In fact the opposite is the case.

### [opposite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opposite_3) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒpəzɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːpəzɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opp/oppos/opposite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I sat opposite him during the meal (= on the other side of the table).
  - The bank is opposite the supermarket (= on the other side of the road).
  - Write your address opposite (= next to) your name.

### [or](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/or_1) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/or_/or__g/or__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/or_/or__u/or__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is your sister older or younger than you?
  - Are you coming or not?
  - Is it a boy or a girl?

### [orange](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/orange_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒrɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ora/orang/orange__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ora/orang/orange__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - yellow and orange flames
  - There was a warm orange glow in the sky.
  - Its petals are orange.
  - Tom was wearing a bright orange jacket.

### [orange](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/orange_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒrɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ora/orang/orange__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ora/orang/orange__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - orange peel/zest/rind
  - an orange tree
  - freshly squeezed orange juice
  - orange groves (= groups of orange trees)
  - orange blossom

### [order](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/order_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ord/order/order__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ord/order/order__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in… order The names are listed in alphabetical order.
  - The winning scores, in reverse order, are as follows.
  - in the/a… order Put the words in the correct order.
  - Let's take the problems in a different order.
  - in order of something arranged in order of preference/importance/priority
  - out of order Scenes in films are often shot out of order.
  - Make sure you get the chronological order right.

### [order](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/order_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ord/order/order__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ord/order/order__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - order (something) I ordered a coffee and a sandwich.
  - Have you ordered yet?
  - The waiter asked if we were ready to order.
  - order somebody/yourself something He ordered himself a pot of tea.
  - order for somebody Will you order for me while I make a phone call?
  - order something for somebody We ordered extra fries for the kids.

### [other](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/other_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʌðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈʌðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oth/other/other__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oth/other/other__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Mr Harris and Mrs Bate and three other teachers were there.
  - Are there any other questions?
  - There seem to be no other survivors.
  - I can't see you now—some other time, maybe.
  - Two buildings were destroyed and many others damaged in the blast.
  - This option is preferable to any other.
  - Some designs are better than others.
  - He was the only other person in the apartment.
  - There was a university party and various other things going on.
  - Dublin is very expensive compared to other major cities in Europe.

### [other](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/other_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈʌðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈʌðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oth/other/other__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oth/other/other__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Mr Harris and Mrs Bate and three other teachers were there.
  - Are there any other questions?
  - There seem to be no other survivors.
  - I can't see you now—some other time, maybe.
  - Two buildings were destroyed and many others damaged in the blast.
  - This option is preferable to any other.
  - Some designs are better than others.
  - He was the only other person in the apartment.
  - There was a university party and various other things going on.
  - Dublin is very expensive compared to other major cities in Europe.

### [our](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/our) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/our/our__/our__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/our/our__/our__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - our daughter/dog/house
  - We showed them some of our photos.
  - Our main export is rice.
  - And now, over to our Rome correspondent…

### [out](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/out_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/aʊt/` · **US:** `/aʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/out__/out__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/out__/out__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She ran out into the corridor.
  - She shook the bag and some coins fell out.
  - out of something I got out of bed.
  - He opened the box and out jumped a frog.
  - Out you go! (= used to order somebody to leave a room)
  - (informal) He ran out the door.

### [out](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/out_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/aʊt/` · **US:** `/aʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/out__/out__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/out__/out__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She ran out into the corridor.
  - She shook the bag and some coins fell out.
  - out of something I got out of bed.
  - He opened the box and out jumped a frog.
  - Out you go! (= used to order somebody to leave a room)
  - (informal) He ran out the door.

### [outside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/outside_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌaʊtˈsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌaʊtˈsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outsi/outside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outsi/outside__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm seeing a patient—please wait outside.
  - I stood outside in the corridor, looking through the window.
  - The house is painted green outside.

### [over](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/over_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊvə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊvər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/over_/over__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/over_/over__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I stopped and crossed over.
  - He rowed us over to the other side of the lake.
  - They have gone over to France.
  - This is my aunt who's over from Canada.
  - I went over (= across the room) and asked her name.
  - Put it down over there.

### [over](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/over_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊvə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊvər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/over_/over__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/over_/over__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She put a blanket over the sleeping child.
  - He wore an overcoat over his suit.
  - She put her hand over her mouth to stop herself from screaming.

### [own](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/own_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əʊn/` · **US:** `/əʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/own/own__/own__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/own/own__/own__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was her own idea.
  - I saw it with my own eyes (= I didn't hear about it from somebody else).
  - Is the car your own?
  - Your day off is your own (= you can spend it as you wish).
  - He desperately wants to live his own life (= be independent and do whatever he wants).
  - Our children are grown up and have children of their own.
  - For reasons of his own (= particular reasons that perhaps only he knew about), he refused to join the club.
  - The accident happened through no fault of her own.
  - He wants to come into the business on his own terms.
  - I need a room of my own.
  - I have my very own room at last.
  - their own personal/unique/private beliefs
  - He was determined to go his own way.
  - I was almost completely in my own little world.
  - Most people want to live in their own homes as they age.

### [own](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/own_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/əʊn/` · **US:** `/əʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/own/own__/own__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/own/own__/own__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was her own idea.
  - I saw it with my own eyes (= I didn't hear about it from somebody else).
  - Is the car your own?
  - Your day off is your own (= you can spend it as you wish).
  - He desperately wants to live his own life (= be independent and do whatever he wants).
  - Our children are grown up and have children of their own.
  - For reasons of his own (= particular reasons that perhaps only he knew about), he refused to join the club.
  - The accident happened through no fault of her own.
  - He wants to come into the business on his own terms.
  - I need a room of my own.
  - I have my very own room at last.
  - their own personal/unique/private beliefs
  - He was determined to go his own way.
  - I was almost completely in my own little world.
  - Most people want to live in their own homes as they age.


## P

### [page](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/page_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/peɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/peɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pag/page_/page__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pag/page_/page__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Turn to page 64.
  - Someone has torn a page out of this book.
  - She slowly turned the pages of the album.
  - a blank page
  - the title/index page
  - the sports/news pages of the newspaper
  - the letters/opinion page
  - Some newspapers devoted several pages to the scandal.
  - on a page The photograph was on the front page of every newspaper.
  - on the opposite/facing/next page
  - over the page The address is over the page (= on the next page).

### [paint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/paint_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/peɪnt/` · **US:** `/peɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pai/paint/paint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pai/paint/paint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - white paint
  - gloss/matt/acrylic paint
  - The woodwork has recently been given a fresh coat of paint.
  - Wet paint! (= used as a sign)
  - The paint is starting to peel off.
  - The house is desperately in need of a lick of paint (= a new coat of paint).
  - We did a quick paint job on the car (= a new coat of paint to make it look better) and hoped the damage wasn't noticeable.
  - a can/pot/tin of paint

### [paint](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/paint_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/peɪnt/` · **US:** `/peɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pai/paint/paint__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pai/paint/paint__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - paint something with something Paint the shed with weather-resistant paint.
  - paint something a brightly painted barge
  - a freshly painted fence
  - have something painted We've had the house painted.
  - paint something + adj. The walls were painted yellow.

### [painting](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/painting) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeɪntɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈpeɪntɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pai/paint/painting__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pai/paint/painting__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a watercolour/an acrylic painting
  - painting of somebody/something Canaletto's paintings of Venice
  - painting by somebody an exhibition of paintings by American artists
  - She has also done abstract paintings and made experimental films.
  - Kahlo's paintings often depict her suffering.

### [pair](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pair_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/peə(r)/` · **US:** `/per/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pai/pair_/pair__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pai/pair_/pair__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pair of shoes/boots
  - a pair of gloves/earrings
  - She pulled on the pair of socks he handed her.
  - a huge pair of eyes
  - A pair of hands grasped her shoulders.
  - a pair of aces/kings (= two playing cards that have the same value)
  - The winner will receive a pair of tickets to Reykjavik.
  - The vase is one of a matching pair.
  - Vermeer painted the two pictures as a pair.

### [paper](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/paper_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeɪpə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpeɪpər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pap/paper/paper__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pap/paper/paper__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a piece/sheet of paper
  - She wrote her name and address on a slip (= a small piece) of paper.
  - He scribbled the number on a scrap of paper.
  - a package wrapped in brown paper
  - recycled paper
  - Make sure you have pen and paper handy.
  - on/onto paper The image is copied onto paper.
  - He brought his lunch in a brown paper bag.
  - a paper cup/plate/napkin
  - This journal is available in paper and electronic form.
  - Experience is more important for this job than paper qualifications (= that exist as official documents but may not have any real value).
  - paper losses/profits (= that are shown in accounts but which may not exist in reality)

### [paragraph](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/paragraph) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpærəɡrɑːf/` · **US:** `/ˈpærəɡræf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/parag/paragraph__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/parag/paragraph__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an opening/introductory paragraph
  - Write a paragraph on each of the topics given below.
  - See paragraph 15 of the handbook.
  - The basic idea is summarized in the following paragraphs.

### [parent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/parent_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeərənt/` · **US:** `/ˈperənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/paren/parent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/paren/parent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's still living with his parents.
  - Sue and Ben have recently become parents.
  - It can be difficult to be a good parent.
  - her adoptive parents (= who have legally adopted her as their child)
  - It's not easy being a foster parent (= who looks after a child for a period of time without legally adopting him/her) to these children.
  - his biological/birth parents (= natural parents, not ones who adopt or foster)
  - Emma is a lone parent with two children.
  - More childcare options and more paid leave will improve the lives of working parents.

### [park](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/park_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːk/` · **US:** `/pɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/park_/park__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/park_/park__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Hyde Park
  - in the park We went for a walk in the park.
  - A public park will be built around the complex.
  - a park bench
  - the park entrance/gates

### [park](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/park_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːk/` · **US:** `/pɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/park_/park__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/park_/park__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He parked and went inside.
  - park + adv./prep. You can't park here.
  - He's parked very badly.
  - park something + adv./prep. You can't park the car here.
  - a badly parked truck
  - A red van was parked in front of the house.
  - I am parked (= my vehicle is parked) just outside.
  - park something My mother parked the car.
  - a parked car
  - They saw his vehicle parked in the driveway.
  - Several police vans were parked outside their home.

### [part](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/part_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːt/` · **US:** `/pɑːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/part_/part__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/part_/part__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We spent part of the time in the museum.
  - Part of the building was destroyed in the fire.
  - Part of the problem is that more and more people live alone.
  - Voters are given only part of the story (= only some of the information).
  - Part of me feels sorry for him (= I feel a little sorry for him, but not very sorry).
  - We spent a good part of the day rehearsing.

### [partner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/partner_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɑːtnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpɑːrtnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/partn/partner__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/partn/partner__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dance/tennis partner
  - The teacher asked the students to choose a partner for the next activity.

### [party](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/party_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɑːti/` · **US:** `/ˈpɑːrti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/party/party__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/party/party__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dinner/tea/cocktail party
  - at a party I was at a birthday party for my friend's five-year-old daughter.
  - to have/throw/give a party
  - to hold/host a party
  - Did you go to the party?
  - party games

### [passport](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/passport) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɑːspɔːt/` · **US:** `/ˈpæspɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/passp/passport__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/passp/passport__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a valid passport
  - a fake passport
  - a South African passport
  - to issue/confiscate a passport
  - I was stopped as I went through passport control (= where passports are checked).
  - EU passport holders
  - a passport photo

### [past](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/past_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːst/` · **US:** `/pæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/past_/past__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/past_/past__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in past years/centuries
  - in times past
  - The time for discussion is past.
  - The days are long past when people worked at the same company all their lives.

### [past](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/past_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːst/` · **US:** `/pæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/past_/past__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/past_/past__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in the past I used to go there often in the past.
  - He had hurt her so many times in the past.
  - the recent/distant past
  - She looked back on the past without regret.
  - of the past Writing letters seems to be a thing of the past.
  - She had learned from her mistakes of the past.
  - I feel that the past and the present and the future are all tied together in some way.

### [past](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/past_3) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːst/` · **US:** `/pæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/past_/past__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/past_/past__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - half past two
  - ten (minutes) past six
  - There's a bus at twenty minutes past the hour (= at 1.20, 2.20, etc.).
  - We arrived at two o'clock and left at ten past (= ten minutes past two).
  - It was past midnight when we got home.

### [pay](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pay_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/peɪ/` · **US:** `/peɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pay/pay__/pay__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pay/pay__/pay__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who's paying?
  - pay for something I'll pay for the tickets.
  - You have to pay extra for a single room.
  - Many consumers are willing to pay more for better service.
  - pay somebody for something Let me pay you for your time.
  - pay for somebody to do something Her parents paid for her to go to Canada.
  - pay somebody Would you mind paying the taxi driver?
  - pay somebody something She's paid $200 a day.
  - pay something Let me pay the bill.
  - pay by something Are you paying by card?
  - pay (in) something to pay (in) cash
  - pay something for something She pays £200 a week for this apartment.
  - pay somebody/something to do something I don't pay you to sit around all day doing nothing!
  - pay somebody something to do something I pay him £10 to clean the car.

### [pen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pen_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pen/` · **US:** `/pen/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pen/pen__/pen__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pen/pen__/pen__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an ink pen
  - Let me grab a pen.
  - to use pen and paper
  - pen and ink
  - in pen a message written in red pen (= using a red pen)
  - from the pen of somebody (figurative) a new book from the pen of (= written by) Zadie Smith

### [pencil](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pencil_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpensl/` · **US:** `/ˈpensl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pen/penci/pencil__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pen/penci/pencil__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll get a pencil and paper.
  - coloured pencils
  - I need to sharpen my pencil.
  - in pencil She scribbled a note in pencil.
  - margin notes in pencil
  - Each picture was carefully drawn with pencil and then colour was added.
  - a pencil drawing/sketch

### [people](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/people_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpiːpl/` · **US:** `/ˈpiːpl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/peo/peopl/people__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/peo/peopl/people__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - At least ten people were killed in the crash.
  - There were a lot of people at the party.
  - Many young people are out of work.
  - older people
  - They are just ordinary people.
  - Millions of working people are struggling.
  - It is important to involve local people in the project.
  - poor/rich people
  - people of faith (= people who are religious)

### [pepper](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pepper_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpepə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpepər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pep/peppe/pepper__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pep/peppe/pepper__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Season with salt and pepper
  - freshly ground pepper
  - sirloin steak with a pepper sauce

### [perfect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/perfect_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɜːfɪkt/` · **US:** `/ˈpɜːrfɪkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perfe/perfect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perfe/perfect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in perfect condition
  - He smiled, revealing a perfect set of teeth.
  - Well I'm sorry—but nobody's perfect (= used when somebody has criticized you).
  - In a perfect world, everybody would have everything they needed.
  - What's your idea of the perfect partner?

### [period](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/period_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɪəriəd/` · **US:** `/ˈpɪriəd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perio/period__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perio/period__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a long/an extended period
  - a short/brief period
  - period of something a period of transition/uncertainty/expansion
  - a period of two years/six months/four weeks
  - a two-year/six-month/four-week period
  - All these changes happened over a period of time.
  - A year-to-year lease has no fixed time period.
  - for a period The offer is available for a limited period only.
  - This compares with a 4% increase for the same period last year.
  - The project will run for a six-month trial period.
  - over a period The hall will be closed over a 2-year period.
  - during a period We know little of her life during that period.
  - The aim is to reduce traffic at peak periods.
  - Tomorrow will be cold with sunny periods.

### [person](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/person) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɜːsn/` · **US:** `/ˈpɜːrsn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perso/person__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perso/person__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What sort of person would do a thing like that?
  - He's a fascinating person.
  - as a person What is she like as a person?
  - He's just the person we need for the job.
  - She's the only person I can talk to about my problems.
  - Gary was the last person I expected to see when I answered the doorbell.
  - I had a letter from the people who used to live next door.
  - I'm not really a city person (= I don't really like cities).

### [personal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/personal) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɜːsənl/` · **US:** `/ˈpɜːrsənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perso/personal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perso/personal__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The novel is written from personal experience.
  - a personal account of her experiences
  - personal effects/belongings/possessions
  - I take personal responsibility for the incident.
  - Of course, this is just a personal opinion.
  - a personal preference/view
  - Her art is deeply personal.
  - intensely/highly personal
  - I need my own personal space.
  - The software is for personal use only (= no one else can use it).

### [phone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/phone_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fəʊn/` · **US:** `/fəʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pho/phone/phone__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pho/phone/phone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I have to make a phone call.
  - The phone rang and Pat answered it.
  - Can someone answer the phone?
  - I hadn't got my phone with me.
  - Please switch off your phones before the show begins.
  - a phone conversation/interview
  - Here is our list of top new phone apps.

### [phone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/phone_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fəʊn/` · **US:** `/fəʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pho/phone/phone__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pho/phone/phone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was just phoning up for a chat.
  - He phoned to invite me out for dinner.
  - phone for something Someone phone for an ambulance!
  - phone about something I’m phoning about your ad in the paper.
  - phone back Could you phone back later?
  - phone home He phoned home, but there was no reply.
  - phone somebody/something Don't forget to phone New York.
  - For reservations, phone 020 281 3964.
  - Someone phoned the police.
  - Phone them up and find out when they are coming.

### [photo](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/photo_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfəʊtəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈfəʊtəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pho/photo/photo__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pho/photo/photo__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a colour/black-and-white photo
  - wedding/family photos
  - your passport/profile photo
  - a group photo of the class
  - I'll take a photo of you. The usual phrase in North American English is take a picture.
  - Fans snapped photos of the event.
  - She posted a photo of the two of them on her Facebook page.
  - The actor shared a photo on Instagram.
  - I've uploaded my holiday photos.
  - to send/tweet a photo
  - to release/publish a photo
  - Visit the photo gallery on our website to see pictures from the event.
  - a photo album (= a book for keeping your photos in)
  - a photo-sharing website

### [photograph](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/photograph_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfəʊtəɡrɑːf/` · **US:** `/ˈfəʊtəɡræf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pho/photo/photograph__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pho/photo/photograph__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - aerial photographs of the crash site
  - colour/black-and-white photographs
  - an exhibition of photographs by Annie Liebowitz
  - a framed photograph of her grandchildren
  - His photograph appeared in the local paper.
  - Please enclose a recent passport-sized photograph of yourself.
  - I spent the day taking photographs of the city.
  - to post/upload a photograph
  - to publish/release a photograph

### [phrase](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/phrase_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/freɪz/` · **US:** `/freɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/phr/phras/phrase__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/phr/phras/phrase__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a memorable/catchy phrase
  - ‘Start slowly’ is the key phrase for the first-time marathon runner.
  - She was, in her own favourite phrase, ‘a woman without a past’.
  - colourful words and phrases

### [piano](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/piano_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/piˈænəʊ/` · **US:** `/piˈænəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pia/piano/piano__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pia/piano/piano__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to play the piano
  - a sonata for violin and piano
  - on piano She was accompanied by her husband on piano.
  - classical/jazz piano music
  - a piano teacher/lesson
  - Ravel’s piano concerto in G
  - a piano solo/duet/trio
  - Alison Evans provided piano accompaniment.

### [picture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/picture_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɪktʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpɪktʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pic/pictu/picture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pic/pictu/picture__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He likes books with lots of pictures in them.
  - picture of somebody/something A picture of flowers hung on the wall.
  - The children were drawing pictures of their pets.
  - She wanted a famous artist to paint her picture (= a picture of herself).

### [piece](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/piece_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/piːs/` · **US:** `/piːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pie/piece/piece__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pie/piece/piece__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - piece of something She wrote something on a small piece of paper.
  - a piece of wood/metal
  - a large piece of land
  - a piece of cake
  - a piece of cheese/meat/bread
  - I've got a piece of grit in my eye.
  - a small/little/tiny piece of something
  - into pieces He cut the pizza into bite-sized pieces.

### [pig](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pig_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɪɡ/` · **US:** `/pɪɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pig/pig__/pig__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pig/pig__/pig__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pig farmer
  - Pigs were grunting and squealing in the yard.
  - to breed/rear pigs

### [pink](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pink_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pɪŋk/` · **US:** `/pɪŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pin/pink_/pink_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pin/pink_/pink__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pale pink roses
  - She went bright pink with embarrassment.
  - The sun was now just a pink glow in the evening sky.
  - She was wearing a hot (= bright) pink top.
  - Outside, the sky is turning pink.

### [pink](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pink_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɪŋk/` · **US:** `/pɪŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pin/pink_/pink_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pin/pink_/pink__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was dressed in pink.
  - She frowned as her cheeks flushed a light shade of pink.
  - The bedroom was decorated in pale pinks.

### [place](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/place_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pleɪs/` · **US:** `/pleɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/place/place__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/place/place__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a place Keep your purse in a safe place.
  - I can't be in two places at once.
  - They organized several demonstrations in public places.
  - I happened to be in the right place at the right time.
  - place where… Is this the place where it happened?
  - place for something This would be a good place for a picnic.
  - There is a time and place for these questions, but not right here and now.
  - place to do something If you're looking for a new service provider, this list is a good place to start.

### [plan](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plan_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/plæn/` · **US:** `/plæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plan_/plan__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plan_/plan__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - plan for something Do you have any plans for the summer?
  - plan to do something There are no plans to build new offices.
  - Your best plan (= the best thing to do) would be to go by car.
  - There's been a change of plan.
  - We can't change our plans now.
  - She told us about her future plans.
  - retirement/travel/wedding plans

### [plan](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plan_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/plæn/` · **US:** `/plæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plan_/plan__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plan_/plan__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - plan something to plan a trip
  - to plan a wedding/holiday/party
  - The men were planning a terror attack.
  - We planned the day down to the last detail.
  - Everything went exactly as planned.
  - The whole event had been carefully/meticulously planned.
  - plan something for something A meeting has been planned for early next year.
  - plan for something It's never too early to start planning for the future.
  - plan how, what, etc… I've been planning how I'm going to spend the day.
  - plan that… They planned that the two routes would connect.
  - plan ahead You can avoid a lot of stress by planning ahead.
  - A well-planned advertising campaign is necessary to bring in visitors.

### [plane](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plane_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pleɪn/` · **US:** `/pleɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plane/plane__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plane/plane__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a passenger/fighter/military/private plane
  - They boarded the plane and flew to Chicago.
  - I caught the next plane to Dublin.
  - The plane took off an hour late.
  - The plane landed in Geneva.
  - to fly a plane
  - by plane She left by plane for Berlin.
  - a plane ticket
  - a plane crash
  - a plane ride/flight/journey

### [plant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plant_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/plɑːnt/` · **US:** `/plænt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plant/plant__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plant/plant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - All plants need light and water.
  - flowering plants
  - native/medicinal/wild plants
  - a tomato/potato plant
  - It's becoming more popular to grow plants organically.
  - The area is home to many rare plant species.
  - the animal and plant life of the area
