  - We'll have the party outside, weather permitting (= if it doesn't rain).
  - in … weather I'm not going out in this weather!
  - Did you have good weather on your trip?
  - The winter weather kept us from going out for a walk.
  - His flight was cancelled due to bad weather.
  - What sort of weather did you have?
  - poor/fine weather
  - What's the weather like?
  - I've been enjoying this beautiful weather.
  - There's going to be a change in the weather.
  - if the weather holds/breaks (= if the good weather continues/changes)
  - The weather is very changeable at the moment.
  - ‘Are you going to the beach tomorrow?’ ‘It depends on the weather.’
  - a weather report
  - a weather map/chart
  - The tent protected us from the worst of the weather.

### [website](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/website) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwebsaɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈwebsaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/web/websi/website__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/web/websi/website__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - For current prices please visit our website.
  - The company has recently launched a new website.
  - She sent me links to several useful websites.
  - the festival's official website
  - on a website I found this information on their website.
  - The video will be posted on our website later today.

### [Wednesday](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wednesday) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwenzdi/` · **US:** `/ˈwenzdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wed/wedne/wednesday_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wed/wedne/wednesday__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's Wednesday today, isn't it?
  - She started work last Wednesday.
  - Are you busy next Wednesday?
  - Wednesday morning/afternoon/evening
  - We'll discuss this at Wednesday's meeting.
  - I work Wednesday to Sunday.
  - I work Wednesdays to Sundays.
  - on Wednesday We'll meet on Wednesday (= next Wednesday).
  - We met on Wednesday (= last Wednesday).
  - (informal or North American English) We'll meet Wednesday.
  - on Wednesdays The museum is closed on Wednesdays (= every Wednesday).
  - (especially North American English) The museum is closed Wednesdays.
  - on a Wednesday He was born on a Wednesday.
  - I always do yoga on a Wednesday (= every Wednesday).
  - I went to Peru on Thursday, and came back the following Wednesday.
  - (British English) ‘When did the accident happen?’ ‘It was the Wednesday (= the Wednesday of the week we are talking about).’
  - (British English) Come back Wednesday week (= a week after next Wednesday).

### [week](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/week) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wiːk/` · **US:** `/wiːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wee/week_/week__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wee/week_/week__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - last/this/next week
  - They won by two goals the previous week.
  - He was returning to Ethiopia the following week.
  - It rained all week.
  - I go dancing every week.
  - What day of the week is it?
  - a/per week She works three days a week.
  - He comes to see us once a week.
  - How much do you earn per week?
  - Water newly planted trees one or two times per week.
  - to have a bad/busy week
  - The rest of the week passed really quickly.
  - throughout the week The cold weather continued throughout the week.
  - It will all be resolved by the end of the week.

### [weekend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weekend_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌwiːkˈend/` · **US:** `/ˈwiːkend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wee/weeke/weekend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wee/weeke/weekend__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - this/next/last weekend
  - at the weekend (British English) The office is closed at the weekend.
  - on the weekend (especially North American English) The office is closed on the weekend.
  - on the weekend of… It happened on the weekend of 24 and 25 April.
  - on a weekend (British English, informal) I like to go out on a weekend.
  - over the weekend Are you doing anything over the weekend?
  - Have a good weekend!
  - We go skiing most weekends in winter.
  - We spent the weekend at the beach.
  - Her birthday is this coming weekend.
  - There was a meeting in Brussels this past weekend.
  - Box office receipts for the film's opening weekend were disappointing.
  - She's away for the weekend.

### [welcome](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/welcome_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · **US:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll go, then. I know when I'm not welcome.
  - + adv./prep. They were anxious to make us feel welcome in their home.
  - Children are always welcome at the hotel.
  - Our neighbours made us welcome as soon as we arrived.
  - I had the feeling we were not welcome at the meeting.
  - Children are always welcome at the hotel.
  - New members are welcome to the club.
  - Poetry Reading. Tonight at 8 p.m. Admission free. All are welcome.
  - a welcome guest/visitor

### [welcome](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/welcome_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · **US:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Welcome home!
  - welcome to something Welcome to Oxford!
  - Good evening everybody. Welcome to the show!

### [welcome](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/welcome_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · **US:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - welcome somebody They were at the door to welcome us.
  - to welcome a visitor/guest
  - welcome somebody to something It is a pleasure to welcome you to our home.
  - welcome somebody + adv./prep. The whole family turned out to welcome him back.

### [well](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/well_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/wel/` · **US:** `/wel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/well_/well__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/well_/well__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't feel very well.
  - Is she well enough to travel?
  - Get well soon! (= for example, on a card)
  - I'm better now, thank you.
  - (informal) He's not a well man.

### [well](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/well_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wel/` · **US:** `/wel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/well_/well__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/well_/well__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They played well in the tournament.
  - The team work well together.
  - The kids all behaved well.
  - The conference was very well organized.
  - Well done! (= expressing admiration for what somebody has done)
  - His campaign was not going well.
  - These animals make very good pets if treated well (= with kindness).
  - People spoke well of (= spoke with approval of) him.
  - She took it very well (= did not react too badly), all things considered.
  - They lived well (= in comfort and spending a lot of money) and were generous with their money.
  - She was determined to marry well (= marry somebody rich and/or with a high social position).

### [well](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/well_3) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/wel/` · **US:** `/wel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/well_/well__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/well_/well__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Well, well—I would never have guessed it!
  - Well, really! What a thing to say!
  - Well, thank goodness that's over!

### [west](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/west_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/west/` · **US:** `/west/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wes/west_/west__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wes/west_/west__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - West Africa
  - the west coast of Scotland
  - on the west side of the building
  - Australia's west coast
  - the museum's west wing

### [west](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/west_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/west/` · **US:** `/west/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wes/west_/west__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wes/west_/west__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This room faces west.
  - The vehicle was last seen heading west on 39th Street.

### [west](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/west_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/west/` · **US:** `/west/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wes/west_/west__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wes/west_/west__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which way is west?
  - Rain is spreading from the west.
  - a west-facing window
  - to the west (of…) He lives to the west of (= further west than) the town.
  - The train line facilitates travel between east and west.

### [what](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/what) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/wɒt/` · **US:** `/wʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wha/what_/what__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wha/what_/what__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - What (= what job) does he do?
  - What time is it?
  - What kind of music do you like?

### [what](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/what) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/wɒt/` · **US:** `/wʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wha/what_/what__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wha/what_/what__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What is your name?
  - What (= what job) does he do?
  - What time is it?
  - What kind of music do you like?

### [when](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/when_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/wen/` · **US:** `/wen/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/when_/when__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/when_/when__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - When can I see you?
  - When (= in what circumstances) would such a solution be possible?

### [when](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/when_3) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/wen/` · **US:** `/wen/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/when_/when__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/when_/when__us_1_rr.mp3)

### [when](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/when_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/wen/` · **US:** `/wen/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/when_/when__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/when_/when__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Until when can you stay?
  - ‘I've got a new job.’ ‘Since when?’

### [where](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/where_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/weə(r)/` · **US:** `/wer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/where/where__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/where/where__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where do you live?
  - I wonder where they will take us to.
  - Where (= at what point) did I go wrong in my calculations?
  - Where (= in what book, newspaper, etc.) did you read that?
  - Just where (= to what situation or final argument) is all this leading us?

### [where](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/where_2) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/weə(r)/` · **US:** `/wer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/where/where__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/where/where__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This is where I live.
  - Sit where I can see you.
  - Where people were concerned, his threshold of boredom was low.
  - That's where (= the point in the argument at which) you're wrong.

### [which](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/which_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/wɪtʃ/` · **US:** `/wɪtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/which/which__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/which/which__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Which way is the wind blowing?

### [which](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/which_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪtʃ/` · **US:** `/wɪtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/which/which__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/which/which__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which is better exercise—swimming or tennis?
  - Which way is the wind blowing?

### [white](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/white_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/waɪt/` · **US:** `/waɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/white/white_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/white/white__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - a white dress
  - a plant with beautiful white flowers
  - The suspect was seen driving a white van.
  - a spacious room with white walls and large windows
  - a set of perfect white teeth
  - His hair was as white as snow.
  - The horse was almost pure white in colour.

### [white](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/white_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/waɪt/` · **US:** `/waɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/white/white_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/white/white__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - in white She was dressed all in white.
  - the pure white of the newly painted walls
  - In China, you wear white when you are mourning.

### [who](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/who_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/huː/` · **US:** `/huː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/who/who__/who__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/who/who__/who__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - I wonder who that letter was from.
  - Who are you phoning?
  - Who's the money for?

### [why](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/why_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/waɪ/` · **US:** `/waɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/why/why__/why__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/why/why__/why__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Why were you late?
  - Tell me why you did it.
  - ‘I would like you to go.’ ‘Why me?’
  - (informal) Why oh why do people keep leaving the door open?

### [wife](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wife) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/waɪf/` · **US:** `/waɪf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wif/wife_/wife__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wif/wife_/wife__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I met my wife at university.
  - He wants a divorce from his estranged wife.
  - He is survived by his wife Anne.
  - She has a wife and two children.
  - He left his wife for a younger woman.
  - wife of somebody the wife of the Italian ambassador
  - She's his second wife.
  - his former/future wife
  - his wife of 25 years
  - The actress was photographed on the red carpet with her wife, Ellen.
  - Many of the players brought their wives or girlfriends along for the trip.
  - He is still struggling with the death of his wife.
  - Steve lives here with his wife and children.

### [will](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/will_3) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/wɪl/` · **US:** `/wɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wil/will_/will__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wil/will_/will__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You'll be in time if you hurry.
  - How long will you be staying in Paris?
  - Fred said he'd be leaving soon.
  - By next year all the money will have been spent.

### [win](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/win_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/wɪn/` · **US:** `/wɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/win__/win__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/win__/win__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which team won?
  - win something to win an election
  - to win a game/race/war/battle
  - She loves to win an argument.
  - win at something to win at cards/chess
  - win (by something) (against somebody/something) France won by six goals to two against Denmark.

### [window](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/window) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɪndəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈwɪndəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/windo/window__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/windo/window__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - out of the window She looked out of the window.
  - through the window He saw her through the rear window of the car.
  - to open/close the window
  - the bedroom/kitchen/car window
  - a broken window
  - The burglars smashed a window to get in.

### [wine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wine_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/waɪn/` · **US:** `/waɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/wine_/wine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/wine_/wine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sparkling wine
  - a dry/sweet wine
  - red/rosé/white wine
  - a selection of fine wines
  - a glass/bottle of wine
  - He never drank wine, beer or spirits.
  - French/Italian/Australian wines
  - We ordered the house wine (= the cheapest wine available in a particular restaurant, sometimes not listed by name).
  - The region produces top-quality wines.
  - the wine industry
  - Do you serve wine by the glass?
  - wines from other regions of France
  - wines from native grapes

### [winter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/winter_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɪntə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwɪntər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/winte/winter__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/winte/winter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a cold/mild/harsh winter
  - a severe/hard winter
  - We went to New Zealand last winter.
  - in (the) winter Our house can be very cold in (the) winter.
  - during the winter Weed seeds are an important source of food for birds during the winter.
  - through/throughout the winter They worked on the building all through the winter.
  - the winter months
  - winter storms/weather
  - a winter coat

### [with](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/with) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/wɪθ/` · **US:** `/wɪθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wit/with_/with__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wit/with_/with__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She lives with her parents.
  - I have a client with me right now.
  - a nice steak with a bottle of red wine

### [without](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/without_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/wɪˈðaʊt/` · **US:** `/wɪˈðaʊt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wit/witho/without__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wit/witho/without__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They had gone two days without food.
  - He found the place without difficulty.
  - She spoke without much enthusiasm.

### [woman](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/woman) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʊmən/` · **US:** `/ˈwʊmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wom/woman/woman__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wom/woman/woman__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 54-year-old woman
  - an interesting young woman
  - married/single women
  - men, women and children
  - a women's group/movement
  - I prefer to see a woman doctor.

### [wonderful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wonderful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʌndəfl/` · **US:** `/ˈwʌndərfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/won/wonde/wonderful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/won/wonde/wonderful__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This is a wonderful opportunity to invest in new markets.
  - The builders have done a wonderful job here.
  - it is wonderful to do something It's wonderful to see you!
  - it is wonderful that… It's so wonderful that you can all come!
  - You've all been absolutely wonderful!
  - a wonderful experience/place/day/book
  - a truly wonderful surprise
  - a really wonderful person
  - We had a wonderful time last night.
  - The organization is doing wonderful things for the community.
  - the strange and wonderful world of Chinese ghost stories
  - to look/sound/feel wonderful
  - It would be wonderful if you could be there.

### [word](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/word_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːd/` · **US:** `/wɜːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/word_/word__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/word_/word__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do not write more than 200 words.
  - Do you know the words to this song?
  - word for something What's the Spanish word for ‘table’?
  - He was a true friend in all senses of the word.
  - Tell me what happened in your own words.
  - I could hear every word they were saying.
  - without speaking/uttering a word
  - He couldn't find the words to thank her enough.
  - Words fail me (= I cannot express how I feel).
  - There are no words to say how sorry we are.
  - I can't remember her exact words.
  - Angry is not the word for it—I was furious.
  - I can never put my feelings into words.

### [work](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/work_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːk/` · **US:** `/wɜːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/work_/work__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/work_/work__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He started work as a security guard.
  - I'm still looking for work.
  - It is difficult to find work in the present economic climate.
  - She's planning to return to work once the children start school.
  - full-time/part-time/unpaid/voluntary work
  - out of work She had been out of work (= without a job) for a year.
  - in work (British English) They are in work (= have a job).
  - What line of work are you in (= what type of work do you do)?
  - off work She's been off work (= not going to work) with a bad back since July.

### [work](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/work_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːk/` · **US:** `/wɜːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/work_/work__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/work_/work__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can't work if I'm cold.
  - The kids always work hard at school.
  - work on something The whole team is currently working on the project.
  - He is working on a new novel.
  - She's outside, working on the car.
  - work at something I've been working at my assignment all day.
  - work with somebody Work with a partner to solve the problem.
  - We work closely with clients to develop specific solutions.
  - + noun Doctors often work very long hours.
  - He works shifts at a metal plant.

### [worker](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worker) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɜːkə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrkər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worke/worker__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worke/worker__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - farm/factory/office workers
  - construction/health/care workers
  - manual/skilled/unskilled workers
  - temporary/part-time/casual workers
  - seasonal migrant workers picking blueberries
  - Aid workers quickly arrived at the scene of the disaster.

### [world](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/world) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːld/` · **US:** `/wɜːrld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/world/world__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/world/world__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a map of the world
  - French is spoken in many parts of the world.
  - in the world Which is the largest city in the world?
  - around/round the world to sail around the world
  - all over the world She has been all over the world.
  - throughout the world Their products are sold throughout the world.
  - They travelled the world for pleasure.
  - He's the world's highest paid entertainer.
  - a meeting of world leaders
  - campaigning for world peace
  - the world economy
  - world markets/trade
  - the world premiere of the movie

### [would](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/would) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/əd/` · **US:** `/əd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wou/would/would__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wou/would/would__us_6_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Would you like a sandwich?
  - Would you have dinner with me on Friday?

### [write](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/write) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/raɪt/` · **US:** `/raɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rig/right/right__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wri/write/write__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - In some countries children don't start learning to read and write until they are six.
  - write in something Please write in pen on both sides of the paper.
  - write with something I haven't got anything to write with.
  - write something Write your name at the top of the paper.
  - The teacher wrote the answers on the board.
  - The ‘b’ had been wrongly written as a ‘d’.

### [writer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/writer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈraɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈraɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wri/write/writer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wri/write/writer__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who's your favourite writer?
  - a travel/cookery/science fiction writer
  - a freelance feature writer for ‘Time’ magazine
  - writers of poetry/fiction
  - the first Russian writer to win the Nobel Prize for Literature
  - She left her job to become a full-time writer.

### [writing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/writing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈraɪtɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈraɪtɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wri/writi/writing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wri/writi/writing__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our son’s having problems with his reading and writing (= at school).
  - a writing case (= containing paper, pens, etc.)
  - Every morning the children do writing.

### [wrong](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wrong_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/rɒŋ/` · **US:** `/rɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wro/wrong/wrong__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wro/wrong/wrong__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I got all the answers wrong.
  - He was driving on the wrong side of the road.
  - Sorry, I must have dialled the wrong number.
  - You're holding the camera the wrong way up!
  - That picture is the wrong way round.
  - I soon realised I'd taken a wrong turn.
  - He got all his calculations wrong.
  - The information is just plain wrong.


## Y

### [yeah](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yeah) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/jeə/` · **US:** `/jeə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yea/yeah_/yeah__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yea/yeah_/yeah__us_1.mp3)

### [year](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/year) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/jɜː(r)/` · **US:** `/jɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yea/year_/year__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yea/year_/year__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Elections take place every year.
  - I lost my job earlier this year.
  - next/last year
  - She died the following year.
  - Three million tourists visit Yellowstone every year.
  - in the year… She was born in the year 1865.
  - in… years The crowd was much smaller than in previous years.
  - In the past few years, she has become one of our top-selling authors.
  - The museum is open all year round (= during the whole year).

### [yellow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yellow_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjeləʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈjeləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yel/yello/yellow__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yel/yello/yellow__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pale yellow flowers
  - a bright yellow waterproof jacket
  - Her teeth were rather yellow.
  - She wore a pale yellow dress.
  - The leaves turn yellow in November.

### [yellow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yellow_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjeləʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈjeləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yel/yello/yellow__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yel/yello/yellow__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was dressed in yellow.
  - the reds and yellows of the trees
  - The hall is decorated in pale yellow.

### [yes](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yes_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/jes/` · **US:** `/jes/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yes/yes__/yes__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yes/yes__/yes__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Is this your car?’ ‘Yes, it is.’
  - ‘Are you coming? Yes or no?’

### [yesterday](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yesterday_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjestədi/` · **US:** `/ˈjestərdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yes/yeste/yesterday__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yes/yeste/yesterday__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A company spokeswoman said yesterday that no final decision had been made yet.
  - yesterday morning/afternoon/evening
  - The company yesterday announced a $40 million deal.
  - To think I was lying on a beach only the day before yesterday.
  - I can remember our wedding as if it were yesterday.
  - They arrived yesterday.
  - Where were you yesterday morning?
  - Figures released yesterday show 4,944 people were awaiting surgery.

### [yesterday](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yesterday_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjestədi/` · **US:** `/ˈjestərdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yes/yeste/yesterday__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yes/yeste/yesterday__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Yesterday was Sunday.
  - What happened at yesterday's meeting?
  - The events of yesterday were clearly still troubling her.
  - She died in the early hours of yesterday.
  - He threatened to resign from the coalition if no agreement was reached by yesterday.

### [you](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/you) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/juː/` · **US:** `/juː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/you__/you__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/you__/you__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You said you knew the way.
  - I thought she told you.
  - Can I sit next to you?
  - I want both of you to help.
  - I don't think that hairstyle is you (= it doesn't suit your appearance or personality).

### [young](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/young_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/jʌŋ/` · **US:** `/jʌŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/young/young_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/young/young__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Young babies need to be wrapped up warmly.
  - a young country
  - Caterpillars eat the young leaves of this plant.
  - It's a young wine, not really up to drinking yet.
  - The night is still young (= it has only just started).
  - Small businesses, especially young ones, tend to operate on a tight budget.

### [your](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/your) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/jə(r)/` · **US:** `/jər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/your_/your__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/your_/your__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I like your dress.
  - Excuse me, is this your seat?
  - The bank is on your right.

### [yourself](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yourself) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/jəˈself/` · **US:** `/jərˈself/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/yours/yourself__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/yours/yourself__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Have you hurt yourself?
  - You don't seem quite yourself today (= you do not seem well or do not seem as happy as usual).
  - Enjoy yourselves!
